  <details>
  <summary>What's missing and why it was split out rather than blocking Phase 3</summary>

  1. **Within-group tab reordering (section 7.2)**: drag-to-reorder and `Move tab left`/`Move tab right` don't exist for either editor group's tab bar. This was never implemented for the single primary group either, before F07 existed -- `TabBar.tsx` has select/close/pin/rename but no reorder of any kind -- so it is genuinely pre-existing scope, not something Phase 3 introduced or regressed, but it is still a real, undelivered piece of section 7.2/acceptance criterion 12 and belongs in the open ledger rather than silently assumed done.
  2. **Per-path selection/scroll/undo state across a group move (section 9.2, acceptance criteria 4/17/20)**: `DocumentViewState` (`sourceSelection`/`sourceScrollTop`/`previewScrollTop`/`lastFocusedPane`) was never built. Moving a tab between groups today preserves its canonical content, dirty state, save authority, and pin state (all real, tested) but not cursor position or scroll offset -- the moved note reopens at its default position in the new group's editor, same as opening any note fresh. CodeMirror undo history is likewise not proven to survive a group move (spec 11.3's required guarantee, including its own fallback: "if preserving full undo history is not technically reliable... the UI must warn before the move"; neither the preservation nor the warning exists yet).

  Everything else Phase 3 claimed -- exactly two groups with unique path ownership, the split shell, per-group view modes, the divider, Split right/Close/Move-to-other-group/Focus commands with a save-error guard on close, and the corresponding test coverage -- is real, implemented, and covered by `src/workspace/store.test.ts`, `src/editorGroups/*.test.tsx`, and new `src/app/App.test.tsx` cases exercising the actual rendered UI end to end. See the Phase 3 entry immediately above (now under `## Implemented`) for its own full verification record.

  </details>

  <details>
  <summary>Scope, dependencies, and what's carried forward from the original combined item</summary>

  Primary scope: sections 5-7 (core product decisions, split UI, creating/closing a split) and section 9's state model, already implemented and unit-tested in `documentGroups.ts` (`createSplitLayout`/`moveTabToGroup`/`mergeSecondaryIntoPrimary`/`updateSplitRatio`/`activateGroup`/`pinGroupTab`/`unpinGroupTab`) but wired into no UI anywhere (confirmed by grep of `App.tsx`/`TabBar.tsx`): no divider, no second rendered pane, no routing of opens to a group.

  Cross-cutting requirements that apply to this phase specifically and must not be dropped: section 11 (editor rendering -- at most two mounted editors, no destructive remounts from divider/panel/theme changes, the undo guarantee), section 12's first four bullets (one save coordinator entry per path regardless of group; group focus never changes write ownership -- F02/F04/etc. integration itself is Phase 5, routing notes below), the subset of section 13's commands needed for two groups (Split right, Close secondary group, Focus primary/secondary/other group, Move active tab to other group, Reset split ratio), section 14's module layout, section 15.1-15.2 concurrency (stale reads, idempotent split, serialized move/reorder), the desktop-relevant states in section 16 (secondary merge blocked by save error, split unavailable because the document area is too narrow), section 17 security (label rendering as text, path containment), section 18.1-18.3 accessibility (tabs pattern, group semantics, separator semantics), and section 19 performance (100ms feedback, no synchronous full-tab-body restore read).

  Acceptance criteria (numbering from spec section 21): 1-8, 12, 17-19, 21, 24 (desktop scope), 25 (desktop scope), 26. Test plan: 22.1-22.3 in full, 22.4's desktop-relevant rows, 22.5's desktop row.

  Out of scope for this phase, split into the sibling items below: compact/Android presentation (Phase 4); F02/F04/F06/F09/F11/F03 routing (Phase 5); removing flat-tab compatibility UI (Phase 6).

  Dependencies: none beyond the already-landed data layer. Must not be implemented in parallel with a conflicting `App.tsx`/`TabBar.tsx`/workspace-settings claim.

  </details>


- ✅ **Maintenance review: the Markdown table toolbar's column/row commands use a strict `<` boundary against each cell's exclusive `sourceTo`, so a cursor resting immediately after a cell's trimmed text — the ordinary position right after typing or clicking a word, before any trailing padding space and the delimiter pipe — falls outside every detected cell/row range**: `src/markdown/tableCommands.ts`'s `columnAt` and `bodyRowAt` both test `cursor < (cell.sourceTo ?? 0)` (an exclusive/half-open interval) against `tables.ts`'s `TableCell.sourceTo`, which is itself documented as the position right *after* the last trimmed character of a cell (i.e. before any trailing whitespace and the pipe). For `add-column-right`/`delete-column`, this means placing the cursor at that exact, completely ordinary boundary position (`cursor === sourceTo`) makes `columnAt` return `null`, so the toolbar command silently does nothing. For `add-row-below`, it is worse than a no-op: `bodyRowAt` also returns `null` at that same boundary on the row's *last* cell, and `edit()`'s `rows.splice((row ?? -1) + 1, 0, ...)` then inserts the new blank row at index `0` — the very top of the table body — instead of below the row the cursor is actually in, silently placing the new row in the wrong location. Reproduced directly against `tableEditAtCursor` (the exact function `MarkdownEditor.tsx`'s table-command effect calls with the live CodeMirror `view.state.selection.main.head`, so this is the real, reachable cursor position, not a synthetic one): for source `"| Name | Status |\n| :--- | ---: |\n| Ada | Active |\n| Bea | Away |"`, placing the cursor immediately after `"Active"` (before its trailing space and the closing pipe) and invoking `add-row-below` inserts the blank row before `"Ada"` instead of after it. An existing test (`tableCommands.test.ts`'s "handles column operations at table boundaries", `cursorInPipe`) already exercises this exact boundary position via `source.indexOf(" | ", ...)`, which lands on the space immediately after `"Name"` — not on the actual pipe character one position later, despite the test's own comment claiming it is "cursor in pipe character" — and asserts `null` as if that were the deliberately-intended behavior; the comment mischaracterizes the position it tests, so this earlier test encoded the bug rather than validating deliberate design. Fix the boundary check (closed interval on the trailing edge) in `columnAt`/`bodyRowAt` so the ordinary post-word cursor position resolves to the correct column/row, correct the mischaracterized existing test to check the actual pipe-character position (which should still correctly return `null`), and add regression tests proving both the column no-op and the row wrong-insertion-location bugs are fixed, revert-confirmed to fail against the unfixed code via `git apply -R` of the fix-only diff.
  <!-- agent-state: {"schema":1,"id":"rm-f25ffc80b950160e","state":"done","touch":["src/markdown/tableCommands.test.ts","src/markdown/tableCommands.ts"],"resources":["markdown-table-commands"],"note":"Landed c0a44cf0fcbb95abac2f50205c375900d69f5ebf on main (fast-forward from claim 9ad9dce, confirmed via git merge-base --is-ancestor). tableCommands.ts's columnAt/bodyRowAt tested cursor \u003c cell.sourceTo (half-open interval), but tables.ts's TableCell.sourceTo is the position right after a cell's last trimmed character (before any trailing padding space and the delimiter pipe), so the ordinary cursor rest position right after typing or clicking a word (cursor === sourceTo) fell outside every detected cell/row. Live and reachable: MarkdownEditor.tsx's table-command effect calls tableEditAtCursor with the real CodeMirror view.state.selection.main.head, the actual editor cursor. Impact: add-column-right/delete-column silently no-op'd at that ordinary position; add-row-below was worse, inserting the new blank row at index 0 (table's very top) instead of below the current row, since bodyRowAt(null) fell through to (row ?? -1) + 1 in edit(). Fixed by closing the interval on the trailing edge (cursor \u003c= sourceTo) in both columnAt and bodyRowAt; adjacent cells cannot collide since their trimmed spans are always separated by at least the delimiter pipe. Also corrected an existing test (handles column operations at table boundaries) whose cursorInPipe was computed via source.indexOf(' | ', ...), landing on the space right after Name (the exact bug boundary), not the literal pipe one position later despite its own comment claiming 'cursor in pipe character' -- it had encoded the bug as intended; fixed to actually test the literal pipe position (still correctly null) with an explicit char-identity assertion. Added 2 new regression tests (column no-op fix; row wrong-insertion-location fix), both revert-confirmed to fail for the intended reason (column: returns null; row: blank row lands before Ada instead of after it) via git apply -R of the fix-only diff to tableCommands.ts, then restored and passing. - ✅ **Maintenance review: a failed note autosave gives no visible indication at all — the error-tracking machinery exists but was never wired to any rendered UI**: `workspace/saveCoordinator.ts`'s `writeRevision` already records a failed write's error message (`entry.lastError`) and invokes an `onError` callback; `App.tsx` wires that callback to `markTabSaveError(path, error)`, which sets `OpenDocument.saveError` (`workspace/store.ts`/`types.ts`), whose own doc comment claims "the user can see this error and retry." None of that was actually true: a repo-wide grep confirmed `saveError` was read by nothing except its own setters and test fixtures, `TabBar.tsx`'s per-tab rendering shows only the tab name and a dirty dot with no error indicator of any kind, and the coordinator's own `retry(session, path)` method and the already-written `clearTabSaveError(path)` helper both had zero callers anywhere outside their own tests. Concretely: a note's autosave write fails (disk full, an external drive unmounted mid-session, an Android SAF grant revoked, a permission error) — the failure is recorded internally but the editor shows nothing different at all; the user keeps typing into what looks like a normal, saving note while their edits accumulate only in memory, with no way to know a write is failing or to retry it, until they eventually close the tab, restart the app, or the workspace changes and the unsaved content is silently gone. This is not a duplicate of the already-fixed fire-and-forget-onOpenFile bug class (that was about async row-click navigation handlers; this is the note-body autosave write path itself giving no failure feedback), and no spec or ROADMAP/CHANGELOG entry discloses this as an intentional limitation — the `OpenDocument.saveError` doc comment's own claim is the opposite. Fixed by rendering a visible `role="alert"` banner (`Couldn't save "{name}": {error}`) above the editor whenever the active tab's `saveError` is set, with a Retry button that calls the coordinator's existing `retry(session, path)`; the banner clears itself once a save actually succeeds by having the `onSaved` callback call the already-written but previously-unused `clearTabSaveError(path)`. Acceptance: a failed autosave shows the alert with the real failure reason; clicking Retry against a since-recovered write clears it once the retry succeeds; regression tests for both, revert-confirmed to fail (test timeout waiting for the alert that never renders) against the unfixed code.

- ✅ **Maintenance review: `automationCommands.ts`'s `leotheca://capture` payload size check measures JavaScript UTF-16 string length instead of the spec-mandated UTF-8 byte size, letting oversized multi-byte captures bypass the 32 KiB limit**: `parseAutomationUrl`'s `capture` case computed `payloadSize` as `text.length + (title?.length ?? 0) + (url?.length ?? 0)`, but `spec/f05-universal-quick-capture-inbox.md` section 5.2 documents `text` as a UTF-8 capture body and its 32 KiB limit in bytes, not UTF-16 code units. JS string `.length` counts UTF-16 code units: any character outside the Basic Latin/most-of-Latin-1 range that still encodes to 2+ UTF-8 bytes (CJK ideographs at 3 bytes, most emoji and other astral-plane characters at 4 bytes via a UTF-16 surrogate pair already counted as `.length === 2`, many emoji, accented Latin, Cyrillic, Greek, and other 2-byte-in-UTF-8 scripts) makes the old check under-count the real payload size, letting a capture up to roughly 2-3x the documented 32 KiB limit through. This is live and reachable: `parseAutomationUrl` is called from `App.tsx`'s `runAutomationUrl`, wired to Capacitor's `CapacitorApp.getLaunchUrl()`/`appUrlOpen` listener on Android and Tauri's `onOpenUrl` on desktop, so an externally-supplied `leotheca://capture?text=...` deep link (from another app, a Shortcuts action, or a shell script) is exactly the untrusted-size input this limit exists to bound. Fixed by measuring `new TextEncoder().encode(text + (title ?? "") + (url ?? "")).length` instead, the real UTF-8 byte count, matching the same-purpose pattern already established in `MarkdownPreview.tsx`'s embed byte budget. Acceptance: a capture whose UTF-16 `.length` is under 32 KiB but whose real UTF-8 byte size exceeds it is now rejected; a capture genuinely within 32 KiB UTF-8 bytes, multi-byte text included, is still accepted; regression tests for both, revert-confirmed to fail against the unfixed `.length`-based check.

- ✅ **Maintenance review: clicking a file tree entry or a sidebar search result whose file can no longer be read fails completely silently (unhandled promise rejection, no user-visible error)**: Same defect class already fixed in `BacklinksPanel.tsx`, `DiagnosticsPanel.tsx`, `BookmarksPanel.tsx`, `GraphView.tsx`, `CanvasView.tsx`, `TaskHubPanel.tsx`, and every `CollectionResults.tsx` view mode, found in the two remaining unguarded call sites: `FileTree.tsx`'s `FileTreeNode.handleClick` (`onOpenFile(entry.path, entry.name)`, no await/catch) and `Sidebar.tsx`'s search-results list (`onClick={() => onOpenFile(entry.path, entry.name, searchQuery.value)}`, same shape). Both are wired from `App.tsx` to the real async `handleOpenFile`, which rethrows a `readTextFile` failure when the request is still current. `FileTree.tsx`'s entries come from `dirChildren`, a directory listing loaded by `loadChildren`/`expandFirstLevel` that is not guaranteed to still match disk by the time a row is clicked (external delete/move, or a sync client, in the window between listing and click); `Sidebar.tsx`'s search results come from `runSearch`, a live search whose results can still go stale in the same window before a result is clicked. Two other bare `onOpenFile` call sites in `Sidebar.tsx` were inspected and left out of scope as a disclosed, deliberately lower-risk residual: `handleCreate`'s post-create open (the file was just created successfully microseconds earlier, and reusing the create dialog's own `error` state isn't visible after the dialog closes) and `FileTree.tsx`'s own re-export are the only two call sites fixed here. Acceptance: `FileTreeNode` awaits `onOpenFile` inside a try/catch with its own per-node inline error message (matching this established pattern's phrasing); `Sidebar.tsx`'s search-result click does the same via a small `handleOpenSearchResult` wrapper with per-result error state; both widen their `onOpenFile` prop type to `void | Promise<void>`; regression tests for both surfaces prove a rejected `onOpenFile` call produces a visible error and that clicking again clears it and retries, revert-confirmed to fail without the fix.

- ✅ **Maintenance review: the Rename Preview dialog's suggested Markdown-link rewrite (F03 Phase 2b-ii) almost always drops the `.md` extension, and adds a wrong extra `../` when the referring note sits above the renamed note's new folder**: `renamePlan.ts`'s `computeRelativePath(fromPath, toPath)` builds the suggested new relative target shown next to every `[label](target)`/`![alt](target)` entry in `plan.markdownEdits`. Its "same directory" branch (`if (fromDir === toDir) return toBasename;`) and its "common ancestor" fallback branch both return `noteBasename(toPath)`, which strips the `.md` extension (it exists to build a wikilink-style bare name, not a literal Markdown path) — so a same-folder or cross-folder-via-common-ancestor rename (the two most common shapes) suggests e.g. `renamed` instead of `renamed.md`, or `../B/renamed` instead of `../B/renamed.md`. Only the third "toPath is a subdirectory of fromDir" branch happens to preserve the extension, because it slices the raw path string instead of calling `noteBasename`. Separately, the "common ancestor" branch's own directory-splitting math (`fromDir.split("/")`/`toDir.split("/")`) treats an empty `dirname()` result (`""`, meaning "no parent directory left to climb, already at a shared root") as one array element (`[""]`) rather than zero, which can inflate the computed `../` up-count by one in that already-narrower case. This dialog is read-only/informational (`RenamePreviewDialog.tsx` says outright "Renaming does not rewrite these yet; update them by hand after continuing"), so nothing on disk is corrupted automatically, but a user who trusts the suggestion and copies it in by hand gets a broken link. Not a duplicate of the already-fixed `renameExecutor.ts` corruption bug (`rm-b8b4267f997dc1e8`, a different function's offset math entirely) or of the disclosed, already-tested "index-as-candidate-filter" limitation this same file's own doc comment and `renamePlan.test.ts` already cover (a note with zero wikilinks is never scanned for markdown links at all — a real, separate, already-acknowledged gap, intentionally left out of this claim's scope). Acceptance: `computeRelativePath` always preserves `toPath`'s real final path segment including its extension, and correctly treats an empty directory as zero path segments in its common-ancestor math; regression tests cover a same-folder rename, a rename into a subfolder from an ancestor referrer, and a rename that requires an `../` prefix, each asserting the exact suggested Markdown link text.

Read-only/informational only (RenamePreviewDialog.tsx: 'Renaming does not rewrite these yet'), so nothing was corrupted on disk automatically, but a user copying the suggestion in by hand would get a broken link. Fixed by rewriting computeRelativePath as one unified common-ancestor algorithm operating on path-segment arrays throughout (root correctly represented as zero segments via a new pathSegments() helper) and using toPath's real final path segment (with extension) instead of noteBasename. - ✅ **Maintenance review: `InkView.tsx` uses its own dead-end, non-tolerant document decoder instead of the properly designed one in `inkDocument.ts`, silently discarding all strokes when a `.ink` file's JSON shape deviates even slightly**: `InkView.tsx` only imports *types* from `./inkDocument` and defines local `decodeInkDocument`/`encodeInkDocument` functions instead of using the real, tolerant `decodeInkDocument`/`serializeInkDocument`/`createEmptyInkDocument` already exported from `inkDocument.ts` (whose own doc comment says it "decodes a standalone ink file without throwing away records from a newer writer": missing `version`/`viewport` default quietly, unknown-shaped strokes and unrecognized top-level fields are preserved verbatim — the same F-010-style tolerant-decode design already applied to `canvasDocument.ts` for the identical reason). A repo-wide grep confirms the real `inkDocument.ts` decoder/serializer have zero production callers, exercised only by their own test file. `InkView.tsx`'s local decoder instead does `if (!parsed.version || !Array.isArray(parsed.strokes) || !parsed.viewport) return getDefaultInkDocument();` — any single deviation (most plausibly a missing top-level `viewport` key, e.g. a hand-edited file, one from a different/older tool, or any minor format drift) discards the *entire* document, including every existing stroke, replacing it in-memory with an empty one, with no error or warning. Concrete failure: an `.ink` file with real strokes but no `viewport` key opens as a blank canvas; the user then draws one new stroke, and the resulting `onChange` (wired to the normal autosave path in `App.tsx`) overwrites the file on disk with only that new stroke — the original drawing is permanently gone. Not a duplicate of the already-fixed ink item (`rm-a28bbd97a6233ac4`, Undo/Redo not persisting through `onChange`) — distinct decode-robustness/data-loss defect, untested by `InkView.test.tsx` (only ever exercises one perfectly-shaped fixture). Acceptance: `InkView.tsx` uses the real `decodeInkDocument`/`serializeInkDocument`/`createEmptyInkDocument` from `inkDocument.ts` instead of its own local shadow copies; a regression test proves a source missing `viewport` but containing a real stroke keeps that stroke rendered and persisted through a subsequent edit, and an unrecognized top-level field survives a round trip; revert-confirmed against the current local decoder.

- ✅ **Maintenance review: pending captures (external deep-links with no workspace open, and Android share-intent text/images) auto-commit to disk the instant any workspace becomes available, completely bypassing the mandatory review step**: `spec/f05-universal-quick-capture-inbox.md` requires review before commit for exactly this class of data — **F05-FR-03** ("External deep-link captures shall require user review before writing"), acceptance criterion 7 ("A deep link cannot supply an absolute destination path or silently commit"), AC8 ("Android sharing of text opens a **recoverable reviewed** capture on cold start and warm start"), and section 7.4 ("Pending captures are distinct from the Markdown inbox note. They are temporary app-private work awaiting an explicit commit.") — but `App.tsx`'s `effect(() => { if (workspacePath.value) { ...void processPendingCaptures(...); } })` (around line 348) fires on *every* change of `workspacePath.value` (cold start, workspace switch, relink) and unconditionally drains the entire pending-capture queue straight to disk, with zero user interaction. Concrete failure: no workspace is open; an OS automation/shortcut fires `leotheca://capture?text=...`; `runAutomationUrl`'s own "no workspace" branch correctly calls `queueCaptureIfNoWorkspace` (source `deep-link`) instead of opening the Capture Sheet, exactly as the spec intends for this case — but the user later opens *any* workspace, and `processPendingCaptures` (`captureProcessor.ts`) immediately appends the captured text to that workspace's inbox note before the user ever sees a Capture Sheet, a preview, or a confirmation. The same happens for `androidShareBridge.ts`'s `processAndroidPendingShareData`, called unconditionally on every app start. `PendingCapturesPanel.tsx` (already unconditionally rendered in `App.tsx`, returns `null` when its queue is empty) is the actual spec-mandated review surface — safe text preview, Review/Retry/Change Destination/Discard actions — but it never gets a chance to run: the auto-effect already wrote and removed the item first. Not a duplicate of any already-fixed maintenance-review item this session (unrelated mechanism to both the fire-and-forget-`onOpenFile` class and the unserialized-write class); `src/capture/captureProcessor.ts` had zero test file before this finding. Acceptance: merely opening/switching a workspace must never write a queued pending capture to disk or remove it from the queue; the only paths that may ever commit a pending capture are the user's own explicit actions in `PendingCapturesPanel` (Review → edit → explicit Capture Sheet submit, or Retry) exactly as spec section 7.4 describes; a regression test proves opening a workspace with a queued deep-link or Android-share capture leaves it `status: "pending"` and never invokes the underlying write call. - ✅ **Maintenance review: Android speech-to-text never actually starts the native `SpeechRecognizer`, so tapping the dictation button is a silent no-op on a real device**: `SpeechController.start()` (`src/speech/speechController.ts`)'s Android branch does `this.setState('recording')` with a comment claiming "recognition is already started via the bridge", but nothing ever starts it: `initSpeechRecognition()` (`speechBridgeImpl.ts`) only calls `isAndroidOfflineSupported()`, a capability check, and `startAndroidSpeechRecognition()` (`capacitorSpeechBridge.ts`), the one function that actually calls `SpeechRecognitionPlugin.startRecognition(...)` → `android.speech.SpeechRecognizer.startListening()` on the Java side, has zero callers anywhere in `src/` (confirmed by repo-wide grep). Concrete failure: a real Android user taps the mic button; the UI cycles idle → starting → recording as if listening began; they speak; they tap stop, which calls `stopSpeechRecognition()` → `SpeechRecognitionPlugin.stopRecognition()` → `speechRecognizer.stopListening()` on a recognizer that was never told to listen — a harmless no-op that resolves normally. Because `startListening` was never called, the Java plugin's `activeCall` was never set, so `onResults`/`onError` never fire, so no text is ever inserted and no error is ever shown: the feature does nothing at all, more silently than the already-fixed "fabricates transcription" bug (`rm-23780de5da6d9091`), which at least produced visible text. Not a duplicate of that entry: that entry's own text explicitly assessed the Java plugin's *internal* implementation as "genuinely implemented, not fake" and never checked whether the TypeScript call chain actually invokes it, which it does not. Also not a duplicate of the fire-and-forget-`onOpenFile` or unserialized-write defect classes already fixed repeatedly this session — unrelated mechanism. Acceptance: `start()`'s Android branch actually calls `startAndroidSpeechRecognition`; its promise (which the native plugin resolves only from `onResults`/`onError`, per `SpeechRecognitionPlugin.java`) is awaited by `stop()` after asking the recognizer to finalize, and its real transcript/error is delivered through the controller's existing `onResult`/`onError` callbacks instead of being silently discarded; the `CapacitorSpeechPlugin.startRecognition` TypeScript return type is corrected to include the `text`/`language` fields the Java side actually resolves with (previously typed as only `{success, error}`); a regression test proves the native call is actually invoked (failing against unfixed code, since it was never called at all) and that a real recognizer result reaches `onResult`. No physical Android device is available in this sandbox, so on-device confirmation of the fix remains a disclosed gap; local TypeScript-level proof that the call chain is now wired end-to-end is the acceptance bar for this item.

- ✅ **Maintenance review: `GraphView`'s and `CanvasView`'s open-file clicks are fire-and-forget, so a stale node/card silently does nothing instead of showing an error**: Same defect class already fixed in `BacklinksPanel.tsx` (`rm-55caa3027a3d3b57`), `DiagnosticsPanel.tsx` (`rm-bd33bc27fac212c4`), and `BookmarksPanel.tsx` (`rm-185aa9efc415b9eb`), found in two call sites those entries' own "out of scope" notes did not cover (only `TagsPanel.tsx`/`CollectionsPanel.tsx`/`FileTree.tsx` were considered and deliberately left out there as lower-risk). `GraphView.tsx`'s `onOpenFile` prop is typed `(path: string, name: string) => void`; its only call site (`handlePointerDown`, line ~375) invokes it directly with no await/catch, and `App.tsx` (line ~1315) passes `(path, name) => { graphOpen.value = false; void handleOpenFile(path, name); }` — `handleOpenFile` is async and rethrows a `readTextFile` failure, so a click on a graph node whose backing file was deleted, renamed, or moved outside the app since the graph's link index snapshot was built produces an unhandled promise rejection: the graph panel still closes (`graphOpen.value = false` already ran), but no tab opens and no error is shown, leaving the user looking at whatever was open before with zero feedback that their click did anything. `CanvasView.tsx`'s `onOpenFile` prop (`(path: string) => void`) has the same shape at its "Open" button (line ~154, `onClick={() => resolved && onOpenFile(resolved)}`); `App.tsx` (line ~1198) passes `onOpenFile={(path) => void handleOpenFile(path, ...)}`. `resolved` only checks that the stored `filePath` resolves to a path inside the workspace (a static string check), not that the file still exists, so a canvas card linking to a since-deleted note fails the same silent way. Acceptance: both call sites await `onOpenFile`/`handleOpenFile` inside a try/catch and show an inline, per-click error (matching the established pattern's UI shape) instead of leaving an unhandled rejection; for `GraphView`, the panel-closing side effect in `App.tsx` must not fire before the open attempt is known to succeed (or the error must otherwise still reach the user despite the panel closing); regression tests for both components proving the error path, revert-confirmed against the existing fix pattern.

- ✅ **Maintenance review: clicking a backlink whose source note can no longer be read fails completely silently (unhandled promise rejection, no user-visible error)**: `linking/BacklinksPanel.tsx`'s `onClick={() => onOpenFile(backlinkPath, fileNameFromPath(backlinkPath))}` calls its `onOpenFile` prop (typed `(path: string, name: string) => void`) directly, without awaiting or catching it, but `App.tsx` passes its own `handleOpenFile` straight through as this prop for every open text note (`<BacklinksPanel path={current.path} onOpenFile={handleOpenFile} />`), and `handleOpenFile` is `async`: it `await readTextFile(path)`s and, when that rejects while the request is still current (`isCurrentFileOpen(authority)`), rethrows rather than swallowing the error. Calling an async function from a plain `void`-typed callback and discarding the returned promise turns that rethrow into an unhandled promise rejection: no tab opens, nothing anywhere in the UI shows an error, and the row's click visibly does nothing. This is the same class of finding, with the identical fix shape, as the just-landed `BookmarksPanel.tsx` (`rm-185aa9efc415b9eb`) and `DiagnosticsPanel.tsx` (`rm-bd33bc27fac212c4`) fixes for an unawaited `onOpenFile`, but a call site neither of those entries named (the `BookmarksPanel` entry's own "out of scope" list names only `TagsPanel.tsx`/`CollectionsPanel.tsx`/`FileTree.tsx`, not this file) and a previously-untested rejection path (`BacklinksPanel.test.tsx`'s existing "clicking a backlink opens it" test only asserts the call happened, via a `vi.fn()` stub that always resolves, never exercising a rejection). A backlink can point at a source note that no longer reads cleanly in the same real, user-reachable ways already documented for bookmarks: the linking note was deleted, renamed outside the app's own rename migration, or moved/deleted by an external tool or sync client while this note's Backlinks panel (rendered for every open text note, not gated behind Settings or a bookmark being added first) is still showing the stale reference — `linkIndex.value` is a point-in-time snapshot rebuilt only on workspace-load/save, not on every external filesystem change. Reproduction: open a note that has at least one backlink, externally delete or move the linking note's file, then click that backlink in the Backlinks panel — nothing happens, no error, no tab. Acceptance criteria: `BacklinksPanel.tsx`'s click handler awaits `onOpenFile` and catches a rejection, surfacing an inline, dismissable per-row error (matching the established "Couldn't open ... — it may have been moved, renamed, or deleted." message/pattern from `BookmarksPanel.tsx`/`DiagnosticsPanel.tsx`) instead of an unhandled rejection with zero user feedback; a regression test proves a rejected `onOpenFile` call produces a visible error rather than a silent no-op, revert-confirmed to fail without the fix. Scope is `BacklinksPanel.tsx` (and its own test file/`linking.css`) only; the same unawaited-`onOpenFile` shape in `TagsPanel.tsx`/`CollectionResults.tsx` remains out of scope for this claim (lower priority, same "derived live from currently-existing data" reasoning already recorded for `TagsPanel.tsx`/`CollectionsPanel.tsx` in the `BookmarksPanel` entry above) — a separate finding if warranted later.

- ✅ **Maintenance review: clicking a Health/Link-Diagnostics finding whose source note can no longer be read fails completely silently (unhandled promise rejection, Settings modal stays open with no feedback)**: `diagnostics/DiagnosticsPanel.tsx`'s `handleSelect` does `await onOpenFile(diagnostic.sourcePath, title)` with no `try`/`catch`, called from `onClick={() => void handleSelect(diagnostic)}`, which discards the returned promise. `settings/SettingsPanel.tsx` passes its own `handleSelectDiagnostic` (`await onOpenFile(path, name); settingsPanelOpen.value = false; ...`) as `DiagnosticsPanel`'s `onOpenFile` prop, itself passed `App.tsx`'s `handleOpenFile` (async, `await readTextFile(path)`s, rethrows on failure when the request is still current). A rejection therefore propagates through two awaited async calls and out through the discarded `void handleSelect(...)` promise: an unhandled promise rejection, no tab opens, the Settings modal never closes (`settingsPanelOpen.value = false` is never reached since the throw happens first), and nothing anywhere tells the user the click did anything at all. This is real and user-reachable, not hypothetical: the diagnostics list is a point-in-time snapshot of `linkIndex.value`, refreshed only when Settings' Health section opens (a `useEffect` keyed on `[settingsPanelOpen.value, workspacePath.value]` calling `rebuildLinkIndex`, confirmed by reading it directly) or on a save/workspace-load elsewhere — not on every external filesystem change. Reproduction: open Settings → Health with at least one broken/missing-heading/ambiguous-heading finding listed, externally delete or move that finding's source note while the modal is still open (another device syncing, an external editor, the OS file manager), then click that finding's row — nothing happens, no error, the modal stays open. Same class of finding, same established fix shape, as the just-landed `BookmarksPanel.tsx` fix (`rm-185aa9efc415b9eb`) for an unawaited `onOpenFile`, but a different, previously-undocumented call site (not mentioned in that entry's own "out of scope" list, which names only `TagsPanel.tsx`/`CollectionsPanel.tsx`/`FileTree.tsx`) and a different, previously-untested code path (`DiagnosticsPanel.test.tsx`'s own "waits for an async onOpenFile" test only exercises the resolve path, never rejection). Acceptance criteria: `DiagnosticsPanel.tsx`'s `handleSelect` catches a rejected `onOpenFile` and surfaces an inline, dismissable per-row error (matching the just-landed `BookmarksPanel.tsx` fix's own message/pattern) instead of an unhandled rejection with zero user feedback and a modal stuck open; a regression test proves a rejected `onOpenFile` call produces a visible error rather than a silent no-op, revert-confirmed to fail without the fix. Scope is `DiagnosticsPanel.tsx` (and its own test file) only; no change needed to `SettingsPanel.tsx`/`App.tsx`.

A bookmark can point at a file that no longer reads cleanly in several real, user-reachable ways: the note was deleted (bookmarks are never pruned on delete — confirmed by grep, `src/bookmarks/store.ts` has no delete-awareness at all); the note was renamed through either live rename entry point, `App.tsx`'s `handleTabRenameSubmit` or `Sidebar.tsx`'s `handleRename` — both funnel through `renameEntry`/`renameOpenTab` only, neither calls the bookmark-path migration logic that exists in `src/refactor/metadataMigrator.ts`'s `bookmarksMigrator`/`renameExecutor.ts`'s `executeRenameOperation` (that migrator is real but dormant, a documented, deliberate gap per this file's own "F03 Phase 2b remainder" entry — not itself a new finding); or the file was moved/deleted by an external tool, sync client, or on a read-only/permission-changed volume. Reproduction: bookmark a note, then delete or externally move that note's file, then click the bookmark in the Bookmarks panel — nothing happens, no console-visible-to-the-user error, no tab, no message. Acceptance criteria: `BookmarksPanel.tsx`'s file-bookmark click handler awaits `onOpenFile` and catches a rejection, surfacing an inline, dismissable error near the affected bookmark (e.g. "Couldn't open — it may have been moved, renamed, or deleted.") instead of an unhandled rejection with zero user feedback; a regression test proves a rejected `onOpenFile` call produces a visible error message rather than a silent no-op, revert-confirmed to fail without the fix. Scope is `BookmarksPanel.tsx` only (and its own test file); the same unawaited-`onOpenFile` shape exists in `TagsPanel.tsx`/`CollectionsPanel.tsx`/`FileTree.tsx` too, but those entries are derived live from currently-existing file content so a stale target is far rarer there and is out of scope for this claim — a separate finding if warranted later.

- ✅ **Maintenance review: `src/editor/DebouncedMarkdownPreview.tsx` and `src/editor/hooks.ts` are orphaned dead code from the same `4ed67ae`/`0a0d346` "feat(performance)" commit series as the already-removed `memoryOptimizations.ts`, `markdownWorker.ts`, `searchIndex.ts`, and `VirtualizedFileTree.tsx`**: `DebouncedMarkdownPreview.tsx` exports a `DebouncedMarkdownPreview` component whose own doc comment says to "use instead of MarkdownPreview for live preview during typing," and `hooks.ts` exports `useDebounce`, `useThrottle`, `useMemoize`, `usePrevious`, and `usePerformanceTimer`. A repo-wide grep for `DebouncedMarkdownPreview` and every one of those five hook names has zero hits outside `hooks.ts` and `DebouncedMarkdownPreview.tsx` themselves (`docs/performance-analysis-2026-09-11.md`, the design doc these files were built from, references them only in its own proposed-code sample, not as a real caller). `App.tsx` renders the plain `MarkdownPreview` directly, which re-parses synchronously on every `source` change via a bare `useMemo` with no debounce anywhere in its own chain, confirming `DebouncedMarkdownPreview` is not a superseded duplicate of debouncing that already happens elsewhere — it is simply never wired in. - ✅ **Maintenance review: Smart Collections writes can race and silently drop a collection create/edit/delete from disk when two mutations overlap**: `src/collections/collectionStore.ts`'s `saveCollections` has the identical unserialized-write defect this session already fixed in `src/bookmarks/store.ts` -- unsurprising, since this file's own top-of-file comment says it follows "bookmarks/store.ts's established shape for a saved, persisted, workspace-scoped list," and evidently copied that shape's bug along with its structure. `saveCollections` calls `writeWorkspaceTextFile` immediately and independently on every invocation, with `collectionsFile.value` snapshotted into the `JSON.stringify` argument at call time; `createCollection`/`updateCollection`/`deleteCollection` each update the signal synchronously then `await saveCollections()`, so two overlapping mutations (create then create, or an edit followed immediately by a delete, before the first write lands) fire two independent, unserialized native writes with no guaranteed resolution order. If the earlier call's now-stale snapshot lands on disk after the later one's, the later mutation is silently reverted on disk even though `collectionsFile.value` (and the UI) correctly reflect it. Confirmed by execution (a throwaway vitest reproduction, since reverted): called `createCollection` twice in a row without awaiting the first -- the second call's native `writeWorkspaceTextFile` fires before the first one's promise even resolves, the same unserialized-call evidence that proved the bookmarks bug. `collectionStore.ts` has no dedicated test file at all today (confirmed: only `CollectionsPanel.test.tsx` exercises it indirectly, and not for this). Acceptance criteria: mirror the same fix already landed for bookmarks (`bookmarksWriteTail`-style queued write tail in `settings/store.ts`'s `saveGlobalConfigOrdered` pattern) -- overlapping `saveCollections` calls must never let an earlier call's native write start independently of a later one; a new regression test in a new `collectionStore.test.ts` proves a later mutation's write does not begin until the earlier one's turn is done, revert-confirmed to fail without the fix.

- ✅ **Maintenance review: Bookmarks/Favorites writes can race and silently drop a bookmark from disk when two mutations overlap**: `src/bookmarks/store.ts`'s `saveBookmarks` reads `bookmarks.value` and calls `writeWorkspaceTextFile` immediately and independently on every call, with no serialization at all between overlapping calls -- unlike this codebase's own established pattern for exactly this class of problem in `src/settings/store.ts`'s `saveGlobalConfigOrdered` (a queued write tail that reads state only when its own turn begins) and `src/tasks/taskMutation.ts`'s `enqueueClosedFileWrite` (an explicit per-path write queue, with its own comment explaining why: "two rapid toggles... must never both read the same disk snapshot and race to write, each silently losing the other's edit"). `addFileBookmark`/`addSearchBookmark`/`removeBookmark` each update the signal synchronously then call `saveBookmarks()`, so two overlapping mutations (e.g. a quick add followed immediately by another add or a remove, before the first write lands) fire two independent, unserialized `writeWorkspaceTextFile` calls, each with `bookmarks.value` already snapshotted into its own `JSON.stringify` argument at call time. If those two native calls happen to resolve out of order (the earlier call's OS-level write completing after the later one's, plausible under real filesystem/IO contention), the earlier call's now-stale snapshot silently overwrites the later one's result on disk, permanently dropping the later bookmark from what is actually persisted even though the in-memory `bookmarks` signal (and the UI) correctly show it. Confirmed by execution (a throwaway vitest reproduction, since reverted): added bookmark A, then immediately added bookmark B (bookmarks.value correctly ends up with both), with the two native `writeWorkspaceTextFile` calls' resolution deliberately reordered (B's write settles first, A's settles last) -- the content that actually lands last on the simulated disk contains only bookmark A, silently missing B. `store.test.ts` has zero coverage of overlapping/concurrent saves (confirmed by reading the file: existing add/remove tests only ever await one mutation fully before starting the next). Acceptance criteria: overlapping `saveBookmarks` calls must never let an earlier call's native write start (or its stale content land) independently of a later one; a new regression test proves a later mutation's write does not even begin until the earlier one's turn is done, and that it then persists the fully current state, revert-confirmed to fail without the fix.

- ✅ **Maintenance review: `appendToInboxNote` silently discards a capture's existing inbox-note content when the append write fails but a later write attempt succeeds**: `src/capture/captureCommit.ts`'s `appendToInboxNote` wraps both the initial `readTextFile` (used to detect "file doesn't exist, create it fresh") and the actual append `writeTextFile` call in the same `try`; its single `catch` block treats *any* error from either call as "the file doesn't exist" and responds by calling `writeTextFile` a second time with only the newly formatted capture content, dropping `existingContent` entirely. So a transient failure on the real append write (a lock/contention hiccup, a momentarily-busy file, a retry after a partial write) is indistinguishable from a genuinely missing file: if that first write fails but a subsequent write attempt (this function's own fallback, or a future retry) succeeds, the inbox note on disk ends up containing only the new capture text, with the user's prior note content silently gone and the function returning success (`{ path, name }`) with no error at all -- exactly the "corrupt/future data overwritten, partial writes, failed saves" defect class `skills/maintenance-review.md` calls out. Confirmed by execution (a throwaway vitest reproduction, since reverted): mock `readTextFile` to resolve with real existing content, mock `writeTextFile` to reject once then resolve; `appendToInboxNote` resolves successfully, but its own last `writeTextFile` call's content omits the existing note text entirely. `captureCommit.test.ts` has zero coverage of `writeTextFile` failing after a successful `readTextFile` (confirmed by reading the file: existing tests only cover a rejected `readTextFile`, meaning "genuinely missing file"). Acceptance criteria: only a failed `readTextFile` (or another explicit "file doesn't exist" signal, not any error from the write step) selects the create-fresh-file path; a failure of the append `writeTextFile` call propagates as a rejected promise instead of triggering a second, content-dropping write; a new regression test proves a failed-then-later-successful append write no longer loses `existingContent`, revert-confirmed to fail without the fix.

- ✅ **The "new-note" automation command (Android home-screen widget cold start) silently drops the request when it races ahead of workspace/settings restoration**: Maintenance-review finding. `App.tsx`'s mount effect kicks off `initSettings()` (async: reads `config.json`, restores SAF/native workspace access, loads `workspaceSettings`, may `readTextFile` the last-active tab) and, independently in a second effect with its own async dispatch (`CapacitorApp.getLaunchUrl()`/`appUrlOpen` on Android, `getCurrentDeepLinkUrls()`/`onOpenUrl` on desktop), processes any pending `leotheca://` deep link via `runAutomationUrl`. There is no ordering between the two: a cold start from the Android "new note" home-screen widget (`LeothecaNewNoteWidgetProvider`, deep-linking to `leotheca://new-note`) very plausibly has its `getLaunchUrl()` resolve before `initSettings()`'s filesystem-bound chain finishes. When that happens, `runAutomationUrl`'s `"new-note"` branch (`if (!workspacePath.value) return;`, no comment justifying it as intentional) silently drops the command: no note is created, no error shown, and the user is left staring at whatever `MainActivity.trackNewNoteCreation`'s native "Creating note…" progress overlay is doing (it polls the workspace root for up to 12s for the expected auto-named file, then just disappears having found nothing). Contrast with the sibling `"capture"` branch a few lines below, which explicitly guards this exact race with `queueCaptureIfNoWorkspace` (a persisted pending-capture queue drained once a workspace becomes available) — `"new-note"` has no equivalent. Acceptance criteria: a `"new-note"` command that arrives before `initSettings` has finished must still result in the note being created (and opened) once settings finish loading, not be dropped; a `"new-note"` command that arrives once settings have finished loading with genuinely no workspace configured must remain a silent no-op, unchanged from today. A regression test must reproduce the drop on unmodified `main` (deferred `initSettings`, fire the deep link, assert `createNoteQuick` is never called while settings are still "loading", then assert it *is* called once `settingsLoaded` flips true) and confirm it fails without the fix.

- ✅ **Maintenance review: Canvas card dragging never recovers from a lost pointer capture (no `pointercancel` handling), so an unrelated later mouse move can silently reposition and persist a stale card's coordinates**: `src/canvas/CanvasView.tsx`'s drag implementation sets `drag` state on `pointerdown` (on `.canvas-card-actions`, via `setPointerCapture`) and only ever clears it on `.canvas-viewport`'s `onPointerUp`; there is no `onPointerCancel` handler anywhere in the file (confirmed by grep: zero matches for `PointerCancel`/`hasPointerCapture`/`releasePointerCapture`), unlike this codebase's own established pattern for exactly this class of bug in `src/ink/InkSurface.tsx` (which tracks the active `pointerId`, checks `hasPointerCapture` before acting on a move, and handles `onPointerCancel` to discard an interrupted gesture — see that file's own "discards stroke when pointer capture is lost before pointer up" and "discards a cancelled stroke without committing a partial edit" tests). `onPointerMove` on `.canvas-viewport` also never checks that the moving pointer's `pointerId` matches the one that started the drag, or that pointer capture is still actually held, so once `drag` is set it keeps applying to any pointer movement over the canvas at all. Confirmed by execution (a throwaway vitest+`@testing-library/preact` reproduction, since reverted): render one card at `(100,100)`, `pointerDown` on its drag handle, fire `pointercancel` (simulating a gesture interrupted by e.g. an OS/browser context switch, a multi-touch conflict, or the tab losing focus mid-drag — none of which the current code recognizes), then fire an unrelated `pointerMove` over the canvas viewport with a different `clientX`/`clientY` (simulating the user simply moving the mouse afterward, e.g. to click a different card) — `onChange` fired with the card silently relocated to the new pointer position and the change written to the (simulated) saved `.canvas` file, with zero user intent to move anything. `CanvasView.test.tsx` has zero drag-related tests today (confirmed by reading the file: only decode/render/toolbar/Open-button coverage exists), so this had no test coverage at all. Acceptance criteria: drag state tracks the originating `pointerId`; `onPointerMove` ignores an event whose `pointerId` doesn't match; `onPointerCancel` (and, for defense in depth, a lost-capture check) clears drag state without applying any further position update; a new regression test proves the pointercancel-then-unrelated-move scenario above no longer calls `onChange` or moves the card, revert-confirmed to fail without the fix.

- ✅ **Maintenance review: Smart Collections' string/path negation operators (`is-not`, `does-not-contain`, `is-not-under-folder`) fail open (match everything) instead of fail closed when a clause's comparison value is missing**: `src/collections/collectionQuery.ts`'s `evaluateStringLike`/`evaluatePathLike` return `true` for these three operators whenever `target === undefined` (no `value` on the clause), e.g. `is-not`: `return target === undefined || folded !== fold(target);`. Confirmed by execution: a clause `{type:"clause", field:{kind:"property",key:"status"}, operator:"is-not"}` (no `value`) evaluates to `true` against a note whose `status` is `"active"` — a filter meaning "status is not \<blank\>" matches every note that has any `status` at all, the opposite of narrowing. Every sibling operator in the same file fails *closed* on a missing value instead (`is`, `contains`, `starts-with`, `ends-with`, `is-under-folder`, `contains-segment`, and the list-negation `contains-no-item` all return `false`/no-match when their value is absent), so this is an inconsistency within the same module, not an intentional "no value means match" convention. Compounding it, `src/collections/collectionDecode.ts`'s `decodeQueryClause` only validates `raw.value` *if present* (`raw.value !== undefined && !isValidQueryValue(raw.value)`); it never requires a value for an operator that needs one to be meaningful, unlike `CollectionBuilder.tsx`'s own `isClauseValid`/`NO_VALUE_OPERATORS`, which already correctly treats every operator outside `{exists, does-not-exist, is-true, is-false, is-empty, is-not-empty}` as invalid without a value — so a value-less negation clause decodes successfully, is not flagged via the file's own `corrupt` signal, and can be silently re-saved as "valid". User-facing impact: any hand-edited, synced-from-another-device, or partially-migrated `.leotheca/collections.json` containing such a clause (or a future Board/Builder regression that ever produces one) turns a supposedly narrowing "is not X" / "does not contain X" / "is not under folder X" filter into "matches every note that has this property/field", with no warning shown anywhere. Acceptance criteria: `decodeQueryClause` requires a valid `value` for every operator outside the no-value set (reusing a single shared constant so the builder and decoder cannot drift apart the way they already have) and drops/flags-as-corrupt a clause that fails this instead of accepting it; as defense in depth, `evaluateStringLike`'s `is-not`/`does-not-contain` and `evaluatePathLike`'s `is-not-under-folder` return `false` (not `true`) when `target === undefined`, matching `contains-no-item`'s existing fail-closed behavior; new regression tests in `collectionQuery.test.ts` and `collectionDecode.test.ts` cover both layers, each revert-confirmed to fail without the fix.
  <!-- agent-state: {"schema":1,"id":"rm-0d1b62d07948b308","state":"done","touch":["src/collections/CollectionBuilder.tsx","src/collections/collectionDecode.test.ts","src/collections/collectionDecode.ts","src/collections/collectionQuery.test.ts","src/collections/collectionQuery.ts","src/collections/collectionTypes.ts"],"resources":["collections-query"],"note":"Landed 3bff179 on main (fast-forward from claim 06c84c3, confirmed via git merge-base --is-ancestor). - ✅ **Maintenance review: `InkView`'s Undo/Redo toolbar buttons never persist the reverted document, so an undone/redone stroke silently reappears on reload**: `src/ink/InkView.tsx`'s `handleUndo`/`handleRedo` call `setHistory`/`setDocument` but, unlike this same component's `handleCommitStroke`/`handleEraseAt`, never call the `onChange` prop (the component's only path to `App.tsx`'s `handleChange`/save pipeline). Confirmed by execution: drawing one stroke (which does call `onChange` once, persisting `strokes.length === 1`) and then clicking Undo removes the stroke from the on-screen `InkSurface` but `onChange` is never called again, so the note's saved `.ink` JSON still contains the pre-undo stroke; the same gap exists for Redo. User-facing impact: a user draws, undoes it, and does anything else (switches notes/tabs, or the app autosaves for an unrelated reason) — the "undone" stroke silently reappears the next time the file is loaded, and a redo is equally never actually saved. Not a disclosed scope gap: neither `ROADMAP.md`'s own Freehand Ink landing entries nor any doc call out undo/redo as unpersisted; the only disclosed remaining gaps for this feature are touch pan/pinch-zoom and Android palm rejection. No `InkView.test.tsx` exists today (only `InkSurface.test.tsx`, which never exercises `InkView`'s own undo/redo wiring), so this had zero test coverage. Acceptance criteria: `handleUndo`/`handleRedo` call `onChange(encodeInkDocument(newDocument))` with the actual reverted/reapplied strokes (derived from the freshly computed `InkHistory.present`, not a value independently recomputed from the pre-update history), matching `handleCommitStroke`/`handleEraseAt`'s existing persistence pattern; a new `InkView.test.tsx` proves a real draw-then-undo (and draw-undo-then-redo) round trip calls `onChange` with the correct, fully reverted/reapplied JSON, revert-confirmed to fail without the fix.

- ✅ **Maintenance review: `renameExecutor.ts`'s Apply and rollback steps corrupt note content whenever a rename's wikilink edits share a file or the renamed basename changes length**: `executeRenameOperation`'s wikilink-update step (F03 Phase 2b remainder, `rm-eab533a0187dc044`, marked `✅` "done" but never wired to any UI — confirmed by a repo-wide grep for `executeRenameOperation`/`renameExecutor` outside the module and its own test file: zero callers) has two distinct, provable data-corruption bugs, both masked by the existing test suite's `mockResolvedValue` (not `mockResolvedValueOnce`) `readNote` stubs, which return the same fixed content on every call regardless of prior writes. (1) The forward-apply loop does `for (const edit of renamePlan.edits) { read; splice at edit.from/edit.to; write }`, reading and writing once **per edit** rather than once **per file**. `renamePlan.ts`'s `planNoteRename` can legitimately push multiple `PlannedWikiLinkEdit`s for the same `path` (a note linking to the renamed note more than once); each one's `from`/`to` is an offset into the original, pre-edit file content. Applying them one at a time re-reads the file that the previous iteration already rewrote, but still uses the original offsets, so any edit after the first in the same file lands at the wrong position once `newText.length !== oldText.length` — corrupting arbitrary surrounding text. (2) `rollbackOperation`'s wikilink-restore loop has the same shape, but is wrong even for a single edit per file: it restores `oldText` at the *original* `[from, to)` range read back from the *already-edited* file, but `to` was computed against the pre-edit content (`to = from + oldText.length`); once the forward edit's `newText` is a different length than `oldText` (the overwhelmingly common case for a real rename), `to` no longer marks the end of the text actually sitting at `from` in the edited file, so a rollback splices at the wrong end-point, corrupting the note it was trying to restore. Rollback runs on every failure after the wikilink-update step succeeds (a metadata-save error, a file-rename error, or any unexpected exception), so this is not a rare path. Acceptance criteria: group edits/restores by path and apply all of a file's edits in one read-modify-write pass (right-to-left by offset, so an earlier edit's anchor is never shifted by a later one already applied to the same in-memory string); rollback must locate the text to restore using the *actual* current span of the previously-written `newText` (its own recorded length), not the stale pre-edit `to`. Add regression tests with two wikilink edits in one file (forward apply) and a length-changing rename followed by a rollback-triggering failure (e.g. file-rename failure), each revert-confirmed to fail without the fix; strengthen `readNote` test doubles to actually reflect prior writes (e.g. `mockImplementation` closing over a mutable string) so this class of bug cannot silently regress again. This is dormant, not yet reachable by a user (no Apply UI wired in), but must be fixed before any future session builds that UI on top of it.

- ✅ **Maintenance review: `src/workspace/markdownWorker.ts`/`public/markdownWorker.js` are fully orphaned dead code from the same commit series as the just-removed `memoryOptimizations.ts`, and the pool manager also has an inverted "available worker" check**: `src/workspace/markdownWorker.ts` (added by the same `4ed67ae`/`0a0d346` "feat(performance)" commit series as `memoryOptimizations.ts`, fixed under `rm-44e5ff0e260a72e9`) exports `getMarkdownWorkerPool`, `parseMarkdownInWorker`, `extractHeadingsInWorker`, and the `useMarkdownWorker` hook, none of which anything else in the repository imports (confirmed by a repo-wide grep for the module path and every one of those exported symbols: zero hits outside the file itself). `public/markdownWorker.js` (the actual Web Worker script it would spawn via `new Worker(new URL("/markdownWorker.js", ...))`) is likewise referenced nowhere else. Neither file has a test. Beyond being dead code, `MarkdownWorkerPool.getAvailableWorker` (`markdownWorker.ts`) has its own inverted-logic bug that would break it the moment anything ever called it: it searches `this.availableWorkers.find(w => !w.isIdle())` — literally "find a worker that is *not* idle" — to satisfy a function named `getAvailableWorker` pulling from a field named `availableWorkers`, backwards from the correct `w.isIdle()`; as written it hands callers a *busy* worker (or `undefined`, sending every request through the 5-second `setInterval` polling fallback that then always manufactures a fresh worker instead of ever reusing one). Acceptance criteria: remove the orphaned, never-wired, untested `markdownWorker.ts` and `markdownWorker.js` (a facade/inert-code cleanup, not a behavior change — nothing depends on either), and their now-fully-inapplicable ESLint `ignores` entries in `eslint.config.js` (after removal, no worker file remains anywhere in the repo, so the "Web worker files have their own global environment" comment and both list entries should come out together, not just have the paths deleted); full verification suite green with no new lint/type errors from the removal.

- ✅ **Maintenance review: orphaned `memoryOptimizations.ts` self-starts a global interval and touches `window` unconditionally at import time, and is mis-classified as a worker file in ESLint config**: `src/workspace/memoryOptimizations.ts` (added by `4ed67ae`/`0a0d346`, "feat(performance)") exports `LRUCache`, `WeakRefCache`, `StringInterner`, `MemoryMonitor`, `BatchProcessor`, and `createWeakEventHandler`, but nothing in `src/`, `src-tauri/`, `android/`, `spec/`, or `documentation/` imports or references any of them (confirmed by a repo-wide grep for every exported symbol and for the module path itself: zero hits outside the file). It has no test file either. Despite being completely dead code, the module still runs `memoryMonitor.start()` as unconditional top-level side-effecting code on line 531, which calls `setInterval` and, inside its tick and `getMemoryUsage()`, unconditionally reads the bare global `window` — a real bug the moment anything ever imports this module: importing it from a non-window global context (a Web Worker such as this same directory's `markdownWorker.ts`, a Node script, a test file, SSR) throws a `ReferenceError` immediately on import, and importing it from the main thread leaks a permanent 1-second `setInterval` with no owner ever calling `.stop()`. `eslint.config.js`'s `ignores` list lumps `src/workspace/memoryOptimizations.ts` in with `public/markdownWorker.js`/`src/workspace/markdownWorker.ts` under the comment "Web worker files have their own global environment" — factually wrong for this file (it is not a worker, and worker globals would not fix a `window`-not-defined error anyway), and looks like the ignore was added to silence real lint findings (`d9edfdd`, "fix(ci): Add markdownWorker.ts and memoryOptimizations.ts to ESLint ignore list") rather than fix them, the kind of CI-weakening-to-conceal-a-defect this repo's own constitution prohibits. Acceptance criteria: remove the orphaned, never-wired, untested `memoryOptimizations.ts` module entirely (a facade/inert-code cleanup, not a behavior change — nothing depends on it) and its now-inapplicable ESLint ignore entry, correcting the remaining comment so it describes only the genuinely-worker file(s) it still covers; full verification suite green with no new lint/type errors from the removal.

- ✅ **`headings.ts`/`tasks.ts`/`blocks.ts`'s shared HTML-comment guard treats any line merely *containing* `<!--` as a block-level HTML comment, silently hiding real headings/tasks/blocks that have a trailing inline comment**: Maintenance-review finding. All three structural scanners copy the same preamble (`headings.ts` is the documented origin; `tasks.ts`/`blocks.ts`'s own doc comments say they skip comments "the same way headings.ts already does"): `const commentStart = line.text.indexOf("<!--"); if (commentStart !== -1) { ...; continue; }`. `indexOf` matches `<!--` anywhere on the line, not just at its start, so a line that is mostly real content with an inline HTML comment appended is treated exactly like a genuine block-level `<!-- ... -->` comment line and skipped entirely, rather than being parsed normally with the trailing comment left as literal trailing text (this codebase's scanners don't strip inline HTML elsewhere either, so that's the correct fallback, not a further gap). Per CommonMark's own HTML-block type-2 rule, the *start* condition requires the line to *begin* with (after up to 3 spaces of indentation) `<!--`; only the *end* condition uses "contains" (`-->`), which these files already implement correctly. Confirmed by execution on current `main`: `scanHeadings(\"# Title <!-- note -->\\n\\nBody\")` returns `[]` (the heading vanishes) where `scanHeadings(\"# Title\\n\\nBody\")` correctly returns one heading; `scanTasks(\"- [ ] Buy milk <!-- reminder -->\\n\")` returns `[]` where the same line without the trailing comment returns the task; `findBlockAtOffset(\"Some paragraph text <!-- note -->\", 0)` returns `undefined` even though offset 0 is well before the comment, where the same text without the comment resolves the paragraph block. `blocks.ts`'s `isContinuationLine` has the identical bug in a second spot (`if (line.text.indexOf(\"<!--\") !== -1) return false;`): a list-item/blockquote continuation line with a trailing inline comment is wrongly treated as ending the block. Real user impact: any heading used for navigation/backlinks/TOC (F04), any Task Hub entry (F02), or any `^block-id` link target (F04/F06) silently disappears the moment its line also carries a trailing `<!-- ... -->` note, with no error or indication anything was dropped. Not caught by any existing test: all three files' own HTML-comment tests only ever put `<!--` at the very start of a line. Acceptance criteria: each file's comment-start detection recognizes a block-level HTML comment only when `<!--` begins the line (after up to 3 spaces of leading indentation, matching each file's own existing `FENCE_RE` convention), not merely appears somewhere on it; a trailing inline comment after real heading/task/paragraph/list-item/blockquote content no longer suppresses that line; every existing block-level-comment test (single-line, multi-line, `^block-id`-inside-a-comment, task-inside-a-comment) keeps passing unchanged; new regression tests cover a heading, a task, a paragraph, and a list/blockquote continuation line each followed by a trailing inline `<!-- ... -->` comment.
  <!-- agent-state: {"schema":1,"id":"rm-7aa97578f1d89485","state":"done","touch":["src/markdown/blocks.test.ts","src/markdown/blocks.ts","src/markdown/headings.test.ts","src/markdown/headings.ts","src/markdown/tables.test.ts","src/markdown/tables.ts","src/markdown/tasks.test.ts","src/markdown/tasks.ts"],"resources":["markdown-block-scanning","markdown-heading-scanning","markdown-table-parser","markdown-task-scanning"],"note":"Landed f29e1d0 on main (fast-forward from claim 49b534a). headings.ts/tasks.ts/blocks.ts/tables.ts each independently detected a block-level HTML comment via line.text.indexOf(\"\u003c!--\"), which matches anywhere on the line; per CommonMark's HTML block type-2 rule only the end condition is 'line contains --\u003e', the start condition requires the line to *begin* with (after up to 3 spaces) \u003c!--. Any heading/task/paragraph/table-row/list-continuation line with a trailing inline \u003c!-- ... --\u003e comment was silently dropped entirely. Fixed with a per-file anchored COMMENT_START_RE = /^ {0,3}\u003c!--/ (matching each file's own FENCE_RE convention) in place of indexOf, including blocks.ts's second occurrence in isContinuationLine. - ✅ **`extractInlineTags`'s `INLINE_TAG_PATTERN`/`isTagBoundary` are ASCII-only, silently truncating or dropping non-ASCII `#tag` names and breaking `extractTags`'s own case-insensitive de-duplication promise**: Maintenance-review finding in `src/tags/tags.ts`. `INLINE_TAG_PATTERN` (`/#(\w[\w-]*(?:\/\w[\w-]*)*)/g`) and `isTagBoundary`'s `/[\w#/]/` check both use plain (non-`u`-flagged) `\w`, which in JavaScript only matches ASCII `[A-Za-z0-9_]`; any non-ASCII letter is invisible to it. Confirmed by execution: `extractInlineTags("Meeting notes #café today.")` returns `["caf"]` (truncated mid-tag at the accented character) instead of `["café"]`; `extractInlineTags("#naïve approach")` returns `["na"]` instead of `["naïve"]`; `extractInlineTags("見て #日本語 タグ")` returns `[]` (the tag is dropped entirely, since the first character after `#` isn't ASCII `\w`) instead of `["日本語"]`. This breaks `extractTags`'s own documented de-duplication guarantee (its doc comment: "canonicalizing both to lowercase sidesteps [needing] an arbitrary rule for which casing wins... two notes spelling what's meant to be the same tag differently"): `extractFrontmatterTags` (`linking/frontmatter.ts`) has no such ASCII restriction, so `extractTags('---\ntags: [café]\n---\n\nAlso #café here.')` returns `["café", "caf"]` (two different tags, one truncated) instead of de-duplicating to `["café"]` — the exact "same tag spelled differently ends up split" problem the doc comment says this function avoids, except here the split is caused by this module's own bug rather than user casing choice. Not caught by any existing test: `tags.test.ts` has no non-ASCII input anywhere. This directly affects real users: any tag containing an accented Latin letter, CJK character, or other non-ASCII script silently corrupts in the Tags panel/tag tree. Acceptance criteria: `INLINE_TAG_PATTERN` and `isTagBoundary` become Unicode-aware (e.g. `u`-flagged regexes using `\p{L}`/`\p{N}` in place of `\w`, matching the existing precedent in `editor/textDirection.ts`'s `STRONG_LTR_CHAR = /\p{L}/u`) while keeping every current ASCII-focused test passing unchanged (hash-in-mid-word exclusion, doubled-hash exclusion, URL-fragment exclusion, fence/inline-code exclusion, nested `/`-segment tags, hyphens); new regression tests cover at least an accented-Latin tag and a CJK tag, both inline and via the `extractTags`/frontmatter de-duplication path above.

- ✅ **`scanBlocks`'s setext-underline guard falls through to the paragraph accumulator when nothing precedes it, absorbing a bare `---`/`===` line into the next block's content**: Maintenance-review finding in `src/markdown/blocks.ts`'s `scanBlocks` (the F04 block-ID/block-link scanner). The setext guard at the line matching `SETEXT_RE.test(line.text)` only fires `paragraphLines.pop(); flushParagraph(); continue;` when `paragraphLines.length > 0`; when it is `0` (right after a blank line, an ATX heading, a fence close, or at document start), the `if` condition is false, there is no `continue`, and the line falls through every remaining classification check straight into `paragraphLines.push(line)`, so the literal `---`/`===` characters become the start of the next paragraph's content instead of being ignored. Confirmed by execution (not just inspection): for `"---\nSome text. ^id"`, `scanBlockIds` returns a paragraph block whose `contentFrom`/`contentTo` cover `"---\nSome text."` (i.e. `source.slice(contentFrom, contentTo)` includes the `---` line), when the correct content is just `"Some text."` — the same input's sibling scanner `headings.ts`'s own `trySetextLevel` branch already handles this exact case correctly: when the line above an underline is unusable (or absent), it silently ignores the underline line instead of treating it as anything, per its `textIsUsable` check. `blocks.ts`'s own module doc comment promises the same exclusion ("excluding heading lines so they are never misread as paragraph content") but the code doesn't implement it for this specific empty-accumulator case. Impact: this corrupts `contentFrom`/`sourceFrom` for `scanBlocks`/`scanBlockIds`/`findBlockAtOffset`, which back the "Copy block link" editor action (`editor/blockLinkActions.ts`); a horizontal rule directly above body text with no blank line between them (valid, common Markdown, e.g. a document's very first two lines) would have its next block's revealed/embedded/copied content wrongly include the literal `---`/`===` text. Not caught by any existing test: `blocks.test.ts`'s setext tests only exercise the `paragraphLines.length > 0` branch. Acceptance criteria: the setext-underline guard skips (does not accumulate) a `SETEXT_RE`-matching line regardless of whether any paragraph text precedes it, matching `headings.ts`'s own silent-ignore behavior for the same unusable-underline case, with a regression test reproducing the exact `"---\nSome text. ^id"` input above (and the analogous `===` case) that fails without the fix and passes with it.
  <!-- agent-state: {"schema":1,"id":"rm-3a526576adafb564","state":"done","touch":["src/markdown/blocks.test.ts","src/markdown/blocks.ts"],"resources":["markdown-block-scanning"],"note":"Landed cf92f93 on main (fast-forward from claim 5c007cb). scanBlocks's SETEXT_RE guard now always continues past a bare ---/=== line, only popping/flushing the preceding paragraph line when one actually exists; previously, when paragraphLines was empty (right after a blank line, an ATX heading, a fence close, or at document start) the line fell through into the paragraph accumulator, so the literal ---/=== text got glued onto the next block's content, corrupting contentFrom/sourceFrom used by scanBlockIds/findBlockAtOffset and the Copy block link editor action. Tests: 3 new in blocks.test.ts, revert-confirmed to fail without the fix. - ✅ **`memoizedSortEntries`'s cache never evicts, growing unboundedly for the app's entire lifetime**: Maintenance-review finding on the same-day, non-claimed `feat(performance): Implement comprehensive performance optimizations` commit (`0a0d346`, the same commit as the `FileTree` stale-`useMemo` regression above), found while closing that item out. `src/workspace/fileTreeStore.ts`'s `memoizedSortEntries` cached results in a plain `Map<string, FsEntry[]>` (`sortEntriesCache`) keyed by a string serializing every entry's `path:isDir` pair plus the sort order, with no eviction logic anywhere: its own `clearSortEntriesCache` export (confirmed via grep) had zero callers in the entire codebase, so nothing ever called it. Every distinct directory listing this function is ever asked to sort — every folder a user expands, every rename/reload that produces a new entries array for the same or a different path — adds one more permanent entry to this map for as long as the app process runs; ironic for a commit whose own stated goal was memory optimization. Acceptance criteria: sorted results stay cached for repeat calls with the same entries array and sort order (preserving the original "prevent unnecessary re-sorting" intent), but the cache must not require every previously-sorted array to be kept alive forever to avoid unbounded growth — a WeakMap keyed by the entries array's own object identity (self-evicting once nothing else references that array, which happens naturally since `dirChildren.value` always gets a new array/Map on any change rather than mutating in place) is one straightforward approach, verified by tests proving the memoization still works and that sorting many throwaway arrays doesn't prevent a kept array from still hitting the fast path.
  <!-- agent-state: {"schema":1,"id":"rm-cbf261fb4784cc9c","state":"done","touch":["src/workspace/fileTreeStore.test.ts","src/workspace/fileTreeStore.ts"],"resources":["file-tree-rendering"],"note":"Landed cebcbd8 on main (fast-forward from claim f561fca). Replaced the unbounded string-keyed sortEntriesCache Map with a WeakMap keyed by the entries array's own object identity, storing {order, result}; since dirChildren.value always gets a new array/Map on any change, cache entries for arrays nothing else references become collectible automatically, no manual eviction needed. Removed the unused clearSortEntriesCache export (zero callers anywhere). Tests: 4 new (correctness matches sortEntries; same array+order returns same reference; order change for same array recomputes; many throwaway arrays don't break a kept array's fast path) -- honestly documented that the leak itself isn't observable via a synchronous unit test (old and new implementations return identical values for every call), so these guard correctness preservation, resting the actual fix's correctness on the WeakMap's documented GC semantics. Verification: tsc --noEmit clean; eslint 0 errors; npx vitest run 2203/2203 passing (+4 new, no regressions); npm run check-version pass; npx vite build pass. No Rust touched.","completed_at":"2026-09-11T18:45:22Z","completed_by":"Claude-Code-cloud-scheduled-kindbardeen-20260911T181651Z-f076c6e1","branch":"agent/rm-cbf261fb4784cc9c/e1ab4d4e4190"} -->
