- ✅ **`FileTree`'s memoized `sortedEntries` never updates once a directory's real listing arrives asynchronously, so the app's actual sidebar file tree renders permanently empty**: Maintenance-review finding on the same-day, non-claimed `feat(performance): Implement comprehensive performance optimizations` commit (`0a0d346`, pushed directly by "Mistral Vibe" under the maintainer's own git identity with no roadmap claim). `src/workspace/FileTree.tsx`'s top-level `FileTree` component has `const sortedEntries = useMemo(() => { const entries = dirChildren.value.get(rootPath); return entries ? memoizedSortEntries(entries) : []; }, [rootPath]);` — the callback reads the reactive `dirChildren` signal, but the dependency array is only `[rootPath]`. `dirChildren.value` is populated asynchronously by `expandFirstLevel(rootPath)`/`loadChildren(path)` (fired from a `useEffect` on mount), which does not itself change `rootPath`, so the `useMemo` never recomputes after that data arrives: `sortedEntries` stays frozen at its first-render value, `[]` (since `dirChildren.value.get(rootPath)` was still `undefined` on that first render). The separate `const entries = dirChildren.value.get(rootPath);` computed just above (line 35, correctly reactive, used only for the `if (!entries) return null;` early-return guard) does update once data arrives, so the component stops returning `null` and proceeds to render `<ul class="file-tree">{sortedEntries.map(...)}</ul>` — but with the stale, permanently-empty `sortedEntries`, producing a `<ul class="file-tree">` with zero `<li>` children forever. Confirmed via `Sidebar.tsx` (`import { FileTree } from "./FileTree"`, `<FileTree rootPath={rootPath} onOpenFile={onOpenFile} />`) that this is the actual, only, live component the app renders for its file-tree sidebar — not the same commit's `VirtualizedFileTree`/`AdaptiveVirtualizedFileTree` (`src/workspace/VirtualizedFileTree.tsx`, 317 new lines), which have zero real callers anywhere (grepped `src/` for `<VirtualizedFileTree`/`<AdaptiveVirtualizedFileTree`: only the file's own internal self-reference), so those two are separately inert/dead code, not the live regression. Reproduced as 9 failing `src/workspace/FileTree.test.tsx` tests on unmodified `origin/main` (confirmed via a separate `git worktree`, unrelated to any other same-day change): `getByText("note.md")`/`getByText("nested.md")` time out because the rendered `<ul class="file-tree">` is completely empty, matching this root cause exactly (the tests' own `listDir` mock resolves asynchronously, so entries always arrive after the first render). This is a real, live, user-facing regression on `main` right now, not a disclosed hardware/credential gap like most other items in this file: any user opening a workspace today would see a permanently empty file-tree sidebar, since the real directory listing always arrives after the component's first render. Acceptance criteria: the memoized `sortedEntries` recomputes whenever the actual entries for `rootPath` change (not just when `rootPath` itself changes), verified by the existing (currently-failing) `FileTree.test.tsx` suite passing again; a regression test proving the specific async-arrival sequence (render before `dirChildren` is populated, then populate, then expect the real entries to appear) if the existing suite doesn't already cover it precisely enough. Also worth checking, in the same pass, whether `memoizedSortEntries`'s own `sortEntriesCache` (`fileTreeStore.ts`) is an unbounded cache that never evicts, growing for the lifetime of the app — lower-severity than this correctness bug, in scope only if it doesn't expand this claim's touch set unreasonably.

- ✅ **`whisper-ffi`'s `transcribe()` fabricates placeholder text on failure and `is_whisper_available()`/`get_speech_status`'s `model_available` are hardcoded `true`, reintroducing the just-fixed Desktop speech dishonesty bug**: Maintenance-review finding on the same-day, non-claimed `feat(speech): Implement whisper.cpp FFI integration for desktop speech-to-text` commit (`be9d895`), pushed directly by "Mistral Vibe" under the maintainer's own git identity with no roadmap claim, right after `rm-23780de5da6d9091` had fixed this exact class of bug (Desktop fabricating transcription text instead of failing honestly). `src-tauri/native/whisper/src/lib.rs`'s `WhisperModel::transcribe()` calls `whisper_full`, and on any nonzero (failure) result returns `Ok("[Speech recognition placeholder - whisper.cpp integration ready]".to_string())` instead of an `Err` — fabricated success text disguised as a real transcript, the identical pattern `rm-23780de5da6d9091` already found and fixed in `speech_commands.rs`. `transcribe_streaming()` is worse: it unconditionally returns `Ok("[Streaming transcription placeholder]".to_string())` regardless of any real state. Separately, `is_whisper_available()` is hardcoded `pub fn is_whisper_available() -> bool { true }` with a comment admitting "we always return true since the stub is functional" — dishonest regardless of whether real whisper.cpp is compiled in. `src-tauri/src/speech_commands.rs`'s `get_speech_status()` has `let model_available = true;` hardcoded unconditionally into the returned `SpeechRecognitionStatus`. None of this is reachable *today*: the generated stub's `whisper_init` always returns a null context, so `WhisperModel::load()` (the only public constructor) always errors first, and no real whisper.cpp/whisper.h source is vendored in `src-tauri/native/whisper/`, so `model_loaded` stays `false` and the frontend's already-fixed `speechBridgeImpl.ts` (`offlineSupported: status.modelLoaded`) still reports unavailable — but this is a dormant time bomb: the moment someone vendors real whisper.cpp source (the explicitly-planned next step for this crate), any real transcription failure would silently produce fabricated placeholder text again, exactly reproducing the incident this project already spent a claimed task fixing. `model_available`/`is_whisper_available`/`get_whisper_version`/`transcribe_streaming` are all dead code today (grepped: zero callers anywhere in `src/` or `src-tauri/` besides their own definitions), so this is a latent defect, not a live user-facing one, but it sits directly in the reachable `transcribe_audio` -> `WhisperModel::transcribe()` call path once a model ever does load. Acceptance criteria: `transcribe()` returns a real `Err` (never fabricated placeholder text) whenever the underlying `whisper_full` call fails, with a regression test exercising the actual decision logic (not just re-asserting the current stub always returns 0); `is_whisper_available()` honestly reflects whether real whisper.cpp bindings are compiled in (via a build-script-emitted cfg, not a hardcoded literal), verified `false` under this sandbox's stub build; `get_speech_status()`'s `model_available` is tied to that same honest signal instead of a literal `true`; `transcribe_streaming()` no longer fabricates success text. Full real whisper.cpp integration (vendoring source, wiring the FFI signature to whisper.cpp's actual `whisper_full(ctx, params, samples, n_samples)` shape, bundling GGML models) remains explicitly out of scope, exactly as `rm-23780de5da6d9091`'s own entry above already scoped it.

- ✅ **`main` CI red: Android `compileDebugJavaWithJavac` fails on `SpeechRecognitionPlugin.java`'s `onDestroy`**: A same-day concurrent commit (`38f3be1`, "fix(android): Fix Java compilation errors in SpeechRecognitionPlugin") removed `@Override` from `onDestroy()` while leaving its body calling `super.onDestroy();`, but `com.getcapacitor.Plugin` (confirmed by reading `node_modules/@capacitor/android/capacitor/src/main/java/com/getcapacitor/Plugin.java` directly) has no `onDestroy()` method at all, overridable or otherwise — only `protected void handleOnDestroy() {}`. The `android` CI job's `Run Android unit tests` step (`app:compileDebugJavaWithJavac`) has failed on every `main` push since (`f088041`, `33287967`, and this commit itself), with `error: cannot find symbol / symbol: method onDestroy()` at the `super.onDestroy();` line, which in turn fails the `validation` gate job that requires every other job to succeed. This also means the plugin's real cleanup (`speechRecognizer.destroy()`, rejecting a pending call) was never actually being invoked by the Capacitor framework even before this specific compile error, since `onDestroy` was never a real lifecycle hook it calls — a genuine resource-cleanup bug, not just a naming slip. `@capacitor/app`'s own bundled `AppPlugin.java` (`node_modules/@capacitor/app/android/.../AppPlugin.java`) is the real, working precedent: `@Override protected void handleOnDestroy() { ... }`, no `super` call needed since the base implementation is empty. Also note `load()`'s `@Override` was removed by the same commit with the stated reason "not overriding base class", which is itself incorrect — `Plugin.load()` is `public void load() {}`, a real, valid override point — though removing `@Override` there does not itself break compilation, only the override-safety check. Fix: rename `onDestroy()` to `@Override protected void handleOnDestroy()` (dropping the now-pointless `super` call), and restore `@Override` on `load()`. No Android SDK exists in this cloud sandbox to run the real Gradle build locally (a disclosed, longstanding limitation many prior items in this file already carry); verify via the real hosted `android` CI job on the pushed commit.

- ✅ **Desktop speech-to-text fabricates transcription instead of running whisper.cpp; marked `✅` incorrectly**: Maintenance-review finding. The "Local speech-to-text dictation" item (`rm-c97aa273b63fa72b`) was marked `✅` `done`, but its Desktop half does not perform any real speech recognition: `src-tauri/src/speech_commands.rs`'s `transcribe_audio` never calls whisper.cpp at all (real or stub) and instead returns a hardcoded string built from the audio's byte length, e.g. `"[Transcribed text from 3200ms of audio]"`; `init_speech_recognition` "simulates successful loading" whenever *any* file matching a known model filename exists, without loading or using it for anything. The `whisper-ffi` crate that *would* wrap real whisper.cpp is `optional = true` behind a `whisper` Cargo feature that is not in `[features] default` and is not passed by any CI/release job, so it is never even compiled into the app; no `whisper.cpp`/`whisper.h` source is vendored in `src-tauri/native/whisper/` either, so enabling that feature today would still only produce the crate's own honest stub (which correctly fails to load, unlike the Tauri command layer). `src/workspace/speechBridgeImpl.ts`'s `isSpeechRecognitionAvailable()` compounds this: `return status.modelLoaded || true;` always evaluates to `true` regardless of `modelLoaded`, and `getSpeechStatus()` hardcodes `offlineSupported: true` for desktop unconditionally, so the UI never had a chance to detect the gap. Net effect: a desktop user who clicks the dictation button and speaks gets fabricated placeholder text silently inserted at their cursor, indistinguishable in the UI from a real (if inaccurate) transcription, with a hardcoded fake `confidence: 0.95` in `speechController.ts`'s `processAudio()` — a real note-content-corruption bug, not merely an unverified-on-hardware gap like most other `🚧` items in this file. None of the "41 speech tests passing" exercise this path: `speechController.test.ts` has no test that calls `processAudio`/`transcribeAudio` at all. By contrast, Android's primary path (`SpeechRecognitionPlugin.java`'s `startRecognition`/`onResults`, using the real `android.speech.SpeechRecognizer` with `EXTRA_PREFER_OFFLINE`) is genuinely implemented, not fake, though still unverified on a physical device per the same item's own disclosed gap; the same file's separate `transcribeAudioData` method is *also* a hardcoded-fake fallback ("Simulate transcription based on audio length"), but it is dead code not reached by the app's actual Android flow (`speechController.ts` uses `stopSpeechRecognition`/native-listener results on Android, never `transcribeAudioData`) — lower priority than the reachable Desktop bug, worth removing or wiring for real in the same pass if in scope. Acceptance criteria: Desktop no longer fabricates transcription text or a fake confidence score under any circumstance; `transcribeAudio`/`initSpeechRecognition` fail honestly (a real user-facing error, not silent success) when no real whisper.cpp backend is compiled in, so the existing error-state UI in `SpeechRecognitionButton.tsx` (already implemented, already handles `onError`/`isFatal`) takes over instead of inserting garbage; `isSpeechRecognitionAvailable`/`getSpeechStatus` report real, non-hardcoded availability; regression tests prove the dishonest-success paths are gone (revert-confirmed). Full real whisper.cpp integration (vendoring source, linking, bundling/downloading GGML models, a genuine desktop audio-to-text pipeline) is explicitly out of scope for the honesty fix above and remains separate future work; do not re-mark the parent item `✅` until that real integration exists and is verified.

- ✅ **`rebuildLinkIndex`'s request counter aliases across a workspace switch, letting a stale rebuild overwrite a newer workspace's link index**: `linking/store.ts`'s `latestIndexRequest` is a plain module-level counter; `resetLinkIndexCache` (registered with `App.tsx`'s `workspaceTransitions.registerReset`, so it fires on every workspace switch) resets it back to `0`. The very next `rebuildLinkIndex` call after a reset increments it back to `1` -- the exact same value the session's *first-ever* call also got. If an older, still-in-flight call from the just-left workspace also captured `request = 1` (i.e. it was itself the first call before the reset), its `isCurrentRequest()` check re-aliases to `true` once it resumes, since nothing else has incremented the counter in between. Concretely, on Android (where every workspace grant shares the identical synthetic `"/workspace"` path): open workspace A, trigger `rebuildLinkIndex` (e.g. Graph View) while its recursive walk is slow; switch to workspace B before it resolves (firing the reset); trigger `rebuildLinkIndex` in B too (also the first call since the reset, so it also gets `request = 1`) and let it finish; A's stale call then finally resolves and, because `1 === 1` again, overwrites `linkIndex.value` (and persists via `savePersistedCache`) with A's own backlinks/tags/tasks/headings under paths that are currently displaying B's unrelated notes -- a real cross-workspace data leak, not just a stale UI flash. `fileOpenAuthority.ts`'s `fileOpenGeneration` faces the identical Android same-path ambiguity and deliberately never resets for exactly this reason; `latestIndexRequest` should follow the same pattern (stop resetting it in `resetLinkIndexCache`, letting it monotonically increase for the module's lifetime) with a regression test proving a stale pre-reset rebuild can no longer overwrite a newer post-reset one.
  <!-- agent-state: {"schema":1,"id":"rm-65296e5310b03358","state":"done","touch":["src/linking/store.test.ts","src/linking/store.ts"],"resources":["link-index-rebuild"],"note":"Landed f5c9ee7480dd335d4521e66a3b4a48ee0e41750a on main (fast-forward from claim d7a78a1). resetLinkIndexCache (fired on every workspace switch) no longer resets latestIndexRequest to 0; it now monotonically increases for the module's whole lifetime, matching fileOpenAuthority.ts's fileOpenGeneration, which faces the identical Android same-synthetic-path ambiguity and never resets for the same reason. Before this, a stale in-flight rebuildLinkIndex call from a just-left workspace could capture the exact same request number the next workspace's own first call got right after a reset, letting the stale call's isCurrentRequest() wrongly read true and overwrite the newer workspace's linkIndex.value (and its persisted cache) with the wrong workspace's backlinks/tags/tasks/headings -- a real cross-workspace data leak on Android, where every grant shares the synthetic /workspace path. Tests: 1 new store.test.ts case reproducing the exact sequence with controlled promise timing (vi.waitFor confirms the first call's recursive walk has genuinely started before the reset and second call proceed), asserting the stale call's later resolution no longer overwrites the newer workspace's index. Revert-confirmed: reverting store.ts made the new test fail for the right reason. - ✅ **Renaming or creating a note/folder with a name containing "/" silently diverges by platform instead of erroring identically**: `NamePrompt.tsx`'s create/rename dialogs never reject a typed name containing a path separator, and `fileTreeStore.ts`'s `createNote`/`createFolder`/`renameEntry` pass it straight through to `${dirPath}/${name}` with no validation. On desktop, `resolve_within_workspace` + `fs::create_dir_all(parent)` (`write_workspace_text_file`/`rename_workspace_path_no_replace` in `commands.rs`/`workspace_mutations.rs`) treats a name like `"sub/renamed.md"` as a real cross-directory operation: it creates `sub` if missing and moves/creates the entry there. On Android, `capacitorBridgeImpl.ts`'s `renameWorkspacePathNoReplace` resolves the destination's native `parentUri` from `pathDirname(fromPath)` -- the **source's own** directory, not derived from `to` at all -- and passes only `pathBasename(toPath)` as the new display name to `FolderAccess.renamePathNoReplace`, so any directory component the user typed in the new name is silently discarded: the entry is renamed in place in its original folder instead of moving, with no error either side. Same user action (type a name with a slash, submit), two different real outcomes, neither surfaced as an error. Fix: reject a name containing `/` or `\` at the point it's typed (`NamePrompt.tsx`) or validated (`fileTreeStore.ts`'s three functions), so create/rename stays a single-directory operation identically on both platforms, with a regression test on each covered function and on `capacitorBridgeImpl.ts`'s own rename path.

- ✅ **Capture attachment filenames are never sanitized before being used to build a filesystem write path, and the write goes through the uncontained bridge function**: `PendingAttachment.fileName` (`capture/pendingCaptures.ts`) is documented "(sanitized)" but never actually is: an Android share intent supplies it straight from another app's content-provider display name (`capture/androidShareBridge.ts`'s `mapAndroidAttachment`, fully untrusted), and `pendingCaptures.ts` already has an unused `sanitizeAttachmentFilename` (only referenced by its own test) that was never wired into `addPendingCapture`. `captureCommit.ts`'s `generateAttachmentFilename` builds a destination filename from that raw name (splitting only on the last `.` for an extension, never stripping `/`, `\`, or `..`), and `copyAttachmentsToWorkspace`/`copyFile` wrote it via the plain, uncontained `writeBinaryFile` (an absolute, caller-assembled path with no containment check — its own doc comment says it's meant only for a trusted in-app pasted/dropped image, not attacker-influenced share-intent names), not the already-existing workspace-scoped `writeWorkspaceBinaryFile`/`resolve_within_workspace` this codebase's F-004 audit built specifically to close this class of gap for every other workspace mutation. A malicious share-intent fileName like `../../../../shared_prefs/evil.xml` reaches `fs::create_dir_all`/`fs::write` (`write_binary_file` in `commands.rs`) with those `..` segments intact, and Rust's real filesystem calls do resolve `..` at the OS level (unlike Android's SAF document-creation API, which has no such operator and would merely create odd literally-`..`-named subfolders instead of a real escape) — so on desktop this is a genuine path-traversal write outside the workspace. Fix: sanitize every attachment's `fileName` once, at `addPendingCapture` (the single point every attachment — Android share, deep link, in-app — enters the queue), by wiring up the existing `sanitizeAttachmentFilename`; and switch `copyFile`'s actual write to the workspace-contained `writeWorkspaceBinaryFile` as the real boundary, consistent with every other workspace mutation, rather than relying only on upstream sanitization.

Desktop's write_binary_file (fs::write, no containment) previously let a share-intent fileName like '../../../../shared_prefs/evil.xml' escape the workspace via real OS path resolution; Android's SAF API has no '..' operator so its impact there was cosmetic (a literal '..'-named subfolder), still closed at the root. - ✅ **Maintenance review: F05 capture attachment copy corrupts or silently drops binary attachments (images) because it copies them through a text-decode round trip**: `capture/captureCommit.ts`'s `copyFile` (used by `copyAttachmentsToWorkspace`, the path every Quick Capture/share-intent attachment goes through) reads the source attachment with `readTextFile` and re-encodes the result with `new TextEncoder().encode(...)` before writing it back with `writeBinaryFile` — a comment on the line admits "we treat attachments as text files, but in reality they're binary." On desktop, `read_text_file` is `fs::read_to_string`, which errors outright on non-UTF-8 bytes (an image almost never round-trips as valid UTF-8); the per-attachment `try/catch` in `copyAttachmentsToWorkspace` swallows that, so the attachment is silently dropped with no user-visible error. On Android, `FolderAccessPlugin`'s text read decodes with Java's UTF-8 decoder, which lossily replaces invalid sequences with U+FFFD instead of throwing, so the "copy" succeeds but writes a corrupted, unopenable file — and `generateFingerprintForFile` (used both for retry-dedupe and post-copy verification) re-derives its fingerprint through the same lossy text read on both the source and the written destination, so the two corrupted fingerprints match and the mismatch check never catches it. Fix needs a real binary read primitive threaded through both platform bridges (`tauriBridge.ts`/`tauriBridgeImpl.ts`/`capacitorBridgeImpl.ts`, plus a new `read_binary_file` Rust command symmetric to the existing `write_binary_file`) and `copyFile`/`generateFingerprintForFile` switched to read/fingerprint raw bytes instead of decoded text, with a regression test proving a non-UTF-8 byte sequence round-trips byte-for-byte through a capture attachment copy.

Added readBinaryFile (new read_binary_file Rust command; Android reuses readFileAsDataUrl/Filesystem.readFile via a new base64ToBytes, no native change) and switched copyFile/generateFingerprintForFile to raw bytes. Revert-confirmed both new captureCommit.test.ts cases fail for the right reason against pre-fix code. - ✅ **Maintenance review: a legacy v2 `editorLayout` without `viewMode` decodes with `viewMode: undefined`, violating `EditorLayoutState`'s required field**: `isValidEditorLayoutState` (`src/settings/workspaceSettings.ts`) deliberately accepts a `primary`/`secondary` group whose `viewMode` field is entirely absent (a real v2 `settings.json` written before that field existed), documented in its own comment as intentional so a legitimate file isn't flagged corrupt. But `decodeWorkspaceSettings` then assigns `editorLayout = editorLayoutRaw` verbatim, so the decoded `EditorLayoutState` — whose `viewMode: ViewMode` field is declared required, not optional, in `workspace/types.ts` — actually holds `viewMode: undefined` at runtime for such a file. `restorePrimaryEditorLayout` (`workspace/documentGroups.ts`) then copies that `undefined` straight onto the live in-memory layout on every workspace open/switch, clobbering the valid default `synchronizePrimaryEditorLayout` had already set. No current UI reads `groups.primary.viewMode` yet (confirmed by grep), so this has no visible effect today, but it is exactly the same class of "missing-field-becomes-literally-undefined" defect this file's own history already shipped once (the F07 Phase 2b-6 viewMode-required regression, `rm-673e3fcbd1b2220d`) and will surface the moment a later phase wires per-group view-mode reading/UI to it. Fix: default a missing `viewMode` to the same value a freshly created group already gets (`createPrimaryEditorLayout`'s `"source"`, `createSplitLayout`'s `"preview"`) at the decode boundary, so every `EditorLayoutState` consumer can keep trusting the type. Add a regression test proving `decodeWorkspaceSettings` no longer produces an `undefined` `viewMode` for this legacy shape, revert-confirmed against the pre-fix code.

- ✅ **Maintenance review: search query parser splits a quoted phrase containing a literal " OR " on the operator**: `parseSearchQuery` (`src/workspace/searchQuery.ts`) splits an OR-group's raw text with `trimmed.split(/\s+OR\s+/)` *before* tokenizing, even though the function's own doc comment claims "an `OR` inside a quoted phrase isn't mistaken for the operator." The split regex has no quote awareness at all, so a quoted phrase whose literal text contains " OR " (surrounded by whitespace) is torn apart into extra OR-groups mid-phrase, e.g. `parseSearchQuery('"foo OR bar"')` currently yields two separate one-word groups (`foo`, `bar"` with a stray trailing quote character baked into the second clause's value) instead of the one intended phrase clause, and `'"foo OR bar" OR baz'` yields three groups instead of two. Reproduced directly in a Node REPL against the exact regex in this file. Acceptance: make the OR-group split quote-aware (skip over `"..."` spans while scanning for a whitespace-delimited `OR` token) so a literal " OR " inside a quoted phrase is never treated as the operator, add regression tests in `src/workspace/searchQuery.test.ts` covering a lone quoted phrase containing " OR " and a real OR between two groups where one side is such a phrase, and confirm the existing "splits on a whitespace-delimited OR" / "combines AND-within-group and OR-between-groups" tests still pass unchanged. Scope: `src/workspace/searchQuery.ts`, `src/workspace/searchQuery.test.ts`; resource `search-query-parser`.

- ✅ **Desktop `list_dir` has no workspace-containment check, letting a symlink inside the workspace expose arbitrary filesystem content through the file-tree sidebar** (found during a maintenance review of the just-landed "Maintenance review: whole-workspace read traversals follow a symlink escaping the workspace" fix (`779bd68`/`1c282ff`), which explicitly disclosed this exact gap as out of its own scope: "`list_dir` has the same underlying gap but is out of scope here... a distinct, larger follow-up"): `src-tauri/src/commands.rs`'s `list_dir(path)` (the command that drives `FileTree.tsx`'s sidebar, not the already-fixed whole-workspace walks) calls `fs::read_dir(dir)`/`entry_path.is_dir()` with no `workspace_root` parameter at all and no symlink-escape check, unlike its four sibling read-traversal commands. Reproduction: place a symlink inside the workspace pointing outside it (e.g. at the user's home directory); it appears in the file tree as an ordinary expandable folder (or, for a symlinked file, an ordinary note); expanding it lists the external directory's real contents, and clicking a file inside routes straight to `read_text_file`, which also has no containment check, disclosing arbitrary file content the app process can read. This is more directly user-triggerable than the already-fixed traversal functions, since it is exactly what drives the always-visible file tree, not just search/diagnostics/stats. Fix by giving `list_dir` a second `workspace_root` argument (matching every workspace-scoped mutation's already-established `(workspace_root, ...)` convention, e.g. `trash_path`/`create_workspace_dir_new`), canonicalizing it, rejecting the call if `path` itself resolves outside it, and skipping (not erroring the whole listing for) any child entry that is itself an escaping symlink, mirroring `is_symlink_escaping_workspace`'s existing policy. Requires threading a `workspaceRoot` argument through every frontend `listDir` call site (`tauriBridgeImpl.ts`/`capacitorBridgeImpl.ts`/`tauriBridge.ts`, and every caller in `fileTreeStore.ts`, `settings/store.ts`, `capture/captureCommit.ts`), each of which already has the current workspace root (or, for the two pre-workspace validation call sites in `settings/store.ts`, the candidate root being validated) available in scope. Android's own `capacitorBridgeImpl.ts` `listDir` is unaffected in substance (SAF URIs cannot alias outside the granted tree the way a POSIX symlink can) but needs the same signature to keep `tauriBridge.ts`'s single shared `typeof impl.listDir` type.

A symlink placed inside the workspace pointing outside it (e.g. at the user's home directory) is walked into and its contents surfaced through search results, the link index, "Expand All", and workspace stats -- even though the write-side `resolve_within_workspace` already documents and rejects exactly this same escape (`documentation/ARCHITECTURE.md:51`). - ✅ **Maintenance review: `addAutoTextDirection`'s dir="auto" injection can silently override an author's explicit `dir` attribute**: Found while independently reviewing RTL Phase 1 (`4e5f290`, landed 2026-09-09), the most recently changed area not yet covered by a separate review. `src/editor/textDirection.ts`'s `BLOCK_TAG_PATTERN` uses a negative lookahead plus `[^>]*` to detect whether a block-level tag (`p`/`li`/`h1-6`/`blockquote`/`td`/`th`/`dt`/`dd`) already has its own `dir` attribute before injecting `dir="auto"`; both the lookahead and the capture group stop at the *first* literal `>` character in the tag, including one embedded inside an earlier quoted attribute value (valid HTML: a quoted attribute value is not required to escape `>`). Reproduced directly: `addAutoTextDirection('<p title="x>y" dir="ltr">text</p>')` produces `<p dir="auto" title="x>y" dir="ltr">text</p>` — a duplicate `dir` attribute — and confirmed end-to-end that `DOMPurify.sanitize` on that output keeps the *first* `dir` (`"auto"`) and drops the second, silently discarding the note author's real explicit `dir="ltr"`. This directly contradicts the function's own documented contract ("never ... silently overrides an author's explicit choice"), which a prior self-review already got half right (guarding the `\bdir=`-vs-`data-dir=` false positive) but this quote-unaware truncation was not covered by that pass. Only reachable via raw HTML embedded directly in a note's Markdown source (marked.js passes it through unescaped before this function runs); not a sanitizer/XSS bypass, since DOMPurify still sanitizes the resulting (malformed-looking but harmless) markup afterward, only a silent-override correctness bug matching this file's own stated invariant. Fix: make the lookahead and capture group quote-aware (treat a `"..."`/`'...'` attribute value as one atomic unit even if it contains `>`) so an embedded `>` in an attribute value can never be mistaken for the tag's own closing `>`.

Fixed with a quote-aware ATTRS sub-pattern. - ✅ **RTL Phase 1: automatic per-note text direction in the Markdown editor and preview** (queued 2026-09-07 by the daily competitor changelog scan; Market Solution #2 v1.14.0 Desktop "RTL language support for entire workspace including sidebars" and v1.14.0 Mobile "RTL language support improvements for swipe gestures and animations"; split out of the original "Right-to-left (RTL) language and UI support" entry per `skills/phase-splitting-large-specs.md`, preserving the workspace-chrome-mirroring remainder as its own follow-up directly below): A note written in a right-to-left script (Arabic, Hebrew, etc.) must render and edit correctly, which needs no workspace-chrome change at all, only correct per-line/per-block text direction inside the two places a note's own content actually renders: the CodeMirror source editor and the Markdown preview. Detect direction with the same "first strong character" rule the Unicode Bidirectional Algorithm and HTML's own `dir="auto"` already use (Markdown's own punctuation, digits, and whitespace are bidi-neutral and skipped), applied per source line in the editor and per rendered block element in the preview, so a mixed-language note (an English code block inside an otherwise-Hebrew note, for example) still reads and edits correctly line by line rather than forcing one direction for the whole note. Purely local rendering/layout work, no new file format or network call, no new setting.

- ✅ **Main regression: F07 Phase 2b-6 viewMode requirement breaks settings validation and split-pane helpers** (introduced by commit `ebe5297`, "feat: Complete F07 Phase 2b-6 ViewMode integration", pushed directly to `main` outside the claim ledger): `npm test` fails 18/2095 tests on current `main`. Root causes: (1) `isValidEditorLayoutState` in `src/settings/workspaceSettings.ts` now requires `viewMode` on every group, but any real workspace's `.leotheca/settings.json` written before this commit has a v2 `editorLayout` with no `viewMode` field at all; on load this is neither a valid v2 layout nor a legacy (v1) workspace, so it falls through to `DEFAULT_WORKSPACE_SETTINGS.editorLayout` and is flagged `corrupt`, silently discarding every existing user's open tabs and pinned tabs and showing a false "corrupted settings" warning on first launch after the update — a real backward-compatibility/data-loss regression, not just a test failure; (2) `updateGroup` in `src/workspace/documentGroups.ts` always allocates a new `EditorLayoutState` even when the wrapped update is a no-op, breaking `pinGroupTab`/`unpinGroupTab`'s documented "returns the same reference when nothing changes" contract; (3) two pre-existing tests (`createPrimaryEditorLayout`'s "keeps each path in the primary group at most once..." and `migrateLegacyToEditorLayout`'s "migrates empty legacy data...") assert exact object literals from before this commit's legitimate new `viewMode` field, now failing on an intentional shape change; (4) `mergeSecondaryIntoPrimary`'s "preserves pinned tabs from both groups" test never actually moves the tab into the secondary group before pinning it there, so the pin silently no-ops and the assertion fails for a test-setup reason, not an implementation defect. Fix (1) and (2) as real code bugs, and correct the stale/broken tests in (3) and (4) to match already-correct, already-shipped behavior.

- ✅ **Maintenance review: Preview search-highlighting drops repeat matches and corrupts HTML entities**: A bounded maintenance review of the untested `highlightSearchMatches` post-processing step `MarkdownPreview.tsx` added for "Highlight search matches in the editor and preview" (`fbcb22e`, 2026-09-06, no dedicated tests for the function itself). Found two real, reproducible defects: (1) the highlighting regex has no `g` flag, so `String.replace` only wraps the *first* occurrence of a repeated search term within each text node, silently leaving every later occurrence in the same node unhighlighted, contrary to the feature's own "highlight the matching term(s)" (plural) goal; (2) because this runs *after* `DOMPurify.sanitize` with its output going straight into `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` with no further sanitization pass, a query matching inside an HTML character reference (e.g. "amp" inside "&amp;", or "&" itself) splices a `<span>` tag into the middle of the entity, which the browser then reparses as a literal "&" plus visible highlighted text instead of the intended character — real rendered-content corruption, not just a missed highlight.

Fixed by matching with the g flag and splitting the HTML on entity tokens the same way it already splits on tags. 7 new regression tests (repeat-match count, cross-paragraph matches, case-insensitivity, three entity-corruption cases, no-query no-op); 4 of them revert-confirmed to fail against the pre-fix code for the right reason before restoring the fix. - ✅ **Windows/macOS release build fails: case-only filename collision between `PendingCaptures.tsx` and `pendingCaptures.ts`**: Found via this session's own integration pass, reading the real failing job logs for the `Release` workflow's `windows` and `macos` jobs on current `main` (`7f817eff`, run `34163212592`), rather than assuming CI was healthy from the green `CI` workflow alone (that workflow only runs on Linux runners). Both jobs fail identically and fast, inside `beforeBuildCommand`'s `tsc -p tsconfig.json --noEmit`: `error TS1149: File name '.../src/capture/PendingCaptures.ts' differs from already included file name '.../src/capture/pendingCaptures.ts' only in casing.` `src/capture/index.ts` re-exports the `PendingCaptures` UI component from `./PendingCaptures` (the real file is `PendingCaptures.tsx`) alongside the unrelated data-layer module `pendingCaptures.ts`, and having two files in one directory whose names differ only by case is unsafe on a case-insensitive filesystem (Windows, and macOS's default APFS), which both `windows-latest` and `macos-latest` runners are. Linux (`ubuntu-latest`, ext4, case-sensitive) never surfaces this, which is why `CI`, `validation / frontend`, and every local `tsc`/`vitest` run in this sandbox stay green while `Release`'s `windows`/`macos` jobs fail every time. Fix: rename the component (not the data module, which seven other files already import) to a name that cannot collide case-insensitively, e.g. `PendingCapturesPanel.tsx`, matching this codebase's existing `*Panel.tsx` convention (`TagsPanel`, `TaskHubPanel`, `SettingsPanel`, etc.), and update its two real importers (`src/capture/index.ts`, `src/app/App.tsx`) plus its own test file.

- ✅ **Maintenance review: recurring push-without-local-verification pattern breaking main CI**: A follow-up to "Maintenance review: Mistral-Vibe F05 delivery quality and guardrails" (`rm-00ace761a2711872`, Implemented) with new evidence gathered the same day, after that review's own guardrails landed. Three separate, unrelated incidents on 2026-09-07 share one root cause - a push made without running the exact applicable local check first: (1) six consecutive commits (`0c77821`..`c22ca10`) migrating the test environment from jsdom to happy-dom landed with CI red on every single one (confirmed via each commit's own GitHub Actions run), silently invalidating `MarkdownPreview`'s XSS-sanitization tests in the process (fixed separately, see the happy-dom entry in this file's own history); (2) commit `017e3a5` re-enabled a commented-out test and asserted in its own commit message that the function under test "already handles [this] correctly," without having run that test, when it did not - `sanitizeAttachmentFilename`'s dot/space-trimming regex used `/^[.\\s]+/` (a JS regex literal, where `\\s` means a literal backslash then a literal "s", never whitespace) instead of `/^[.\s]+/`, a direct copy-paste of the Java-side string-embedded pattern without accounting for the different escaping rules between a Java string literal and a JS regex literal; (3) the same day, a direct-to-`main` commit (`0e244ae`) hand-wrote its own `agent-state` HTML comment instead of calling `scripts/agent_ledger.py`, with an `id` that didn't match the helper's required `rm-<16 hex chars>` format and a `branch` that didn't match the required `agent/<id>/` prefix, silently breaking `list`/`claim`/`check`/`finish` for every agent (not just that task) until fixed. Added `skills/pre-push-verification.md` (a small, mechanical, checklist-style gate: run every applicable check in this exact session before every push; never claim "already works" without having executed that exact case; never push a second speculative CI fix without first reading the real failing job's log; never hand-author ledger metadata) and referenced it from `CONSTITUTION.md`'s Quality and verification and Coordination invariants sections. Framed tool-neutrally per this file's own portability rule, not naming a specific provider, since the underlying failure mode (skipping local verification, relying on CI or on visual inspection instead) is provider-agnostic even though the concrete evidence gathered this session happened to come from one provider's session history.

- ✅ **Android share intent with an empty attachments array is stored as `[]` instead of `undefined`**: `androidShareBridge.ts`'s `processAndroidPendingShareData` computes `attachments` as `result.data.attachments ? result.data.attachments.map(mapAndroidAttachment).filter(...) : undefined`, but a JavaScript array is truthy even when empty, so a share intent with `attachments: []` (no attachments, per `CaptureIntentHandler.java`'s own `queueTextCapture` path) still takes the truthy branch and produces `[]` rather than `undefined`. `androidShareBridge.test.ts`'s own "should handle share data with empty attachments array" regression test (pre-existing, not introduced by this note) already expects `undefined` and fails against current `main`. Fix the truthiness check to treat a present-but-empty array the same as absent (e.g. `result.data.attachments && result.data.attachments.length > 0 ? ... : undefined`), confirm the existing test passes, and check `pendingCaptures.ts`/`captureCommit.ts` callers for any place that distinguishes `[]` from `undefined` on this field to ensure the fix doesn't change other observable behavior.

- ✅ **Vitest's happy-dom environment silently breaks DOMPurify sanitization and most component tests**: Commits `0c77821`..`c22ca10` (2026-09-07) migrated every test file's environment from `jsdom` to `happy-dom` to get native `HTMLCanvasElement.getContext("2d")` support for `GraphView.tsx`, and every one of those commits landed on `main` while CI was red (confirmed via the GitHub Actions run history for each commit's exact SHA). Reproduced directly: under `@vitest-environment happy-dom`, `DOMPurify.sanitize("<h1>Title</h1>")` returns the bare text `Title` with every wrapping tag stripped, for effectively every tag tested (`h1`-`h3`, `p`, `strong`, `em`, `img`, `pre`, `code`, `blockquote`, `a`, `ul`, `li`, `table`, `tr`, `td`, `div`, `span`, `hr`, `br`), not just unsafe ones. That's the direct cause of most of `main`'s current 84 failing Vitest tests across 15 files (`MarkdownPreview.test.tsx`'s ~30 failures alone, plus `OutlinePanel`/`HeadingBreadcrumbs`/`MarkdownPreview.headingBlocks` which depend on rendered headings), and it means the suite can no longer prove `MarkdownPreview`'s XSS-sanitization tests actually discriminate safe from unsafe markup, only that DOMPurify strips everything under this environment. The other ~15 failures are a second, independent happy-dom gap: `window.confirm` is undefined (`vi.spyOn(window, "confirm")` throws "can only spy on a function") and `navigator.clipboard` has only a getter (`Object.assign(navigator, {clipboard: ...})` throws), breaking `WorkspaceTransitionBanner`/`FileContextMenu`/`OutlinePanel`/`WorkspaceProfilesSettings`/`WorkspaceSwitcher` tests that stub either. The premise for the migration itself doesn't hold up: `GraphView.test.tsx`'s own pre-migration comment (present since before `0c77821`, confirmed via `git show 0c77821^:src/graph/GraphView.test.tsx`) already documents that a null 2D context under plain jsdom is handled gracefully by `draw()`'s `if (!ctx) return` and needs no canvas mock at all; `GraphView.tsx`/`GraphView.test.tsx` are the only files in the repository that call `getContext` at all (confirmed via grep). Fix: restore `jsdom` as the test environment for every file (the original per-file `/** @vitest-environment jsdom */` docblock this migration overwrote), re-add `jsdom` to `devDependencies`, and leave `happy-dom` installed only if `GraphView.test.tsx` specifically still needs it for some canvas-dependent assertion after re-inspection (its own comment suggests it does not, and reverting it to jsdom too should be tried first). Re-run the full suite and confirm the 84 failures are gone with no new ones introduced, then confirm hosted CI is green on the landing commit for the first time since `0c77821`.

- ✅ **F05 Android share bridge drops staged attachments and logs sensitive URIs**: `CaptureIntentHandler.queueCaptureWithAttachments` stages attachments but calls `storePendingShareData(..., null, null)` and has a TODO instead of serializing `StagedAttachment` metadata, while `androidShareBridge.ts` has no attachment field to enqueue. The same native handler logs raw `content://` values and paths, contradicting F05 acceptance criterion 25. Fix the typed Java/TypeScript payload and queue transfer so staged attachments reach `PendingCapture`, remove/redact sensitive logs and persisted error text, preserve intentional retry/cleanup ownership, and add bridge-level regression coverage for attachments, malformed data, failure, and no-sensitive-output sentinels.

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-scheduled-kindbardeen-20260904T2332Z, branch: agent/f06-phase4c-outline-accessibility, spec: `spec/f06-note-outline-heading-breadcrumbs.md`, section 20 Phase 4): Completes the two items Phase 4a/4b's own entries left open. **Section 15.1 keyboard tree semantics: confirmed already satisfied, no code change needed.** Section 15.1 requires a full ARIA tree only if adopting the tree pattern at all; the spec's own explicitly acceptable alternative, a nested list with buttons, is what Phase 1 already shipped and remains unchanged here, checked directly against `OutlinePanel.tsx`'s real markup rather than assumed. **Section 15.2 announcements: genuinely implemented, not merely reworded from "validation."** Reading Phase 4a/4b's own disclosed gaps and the spec text side by side showed 15.2's announcement mechanism itself, not only its real-screen-reader validation, was still entirely unbuilt (`grep` for `aria-live`/`role="status"` across `outline/` before starting turned up nothing). New `outline/outlineAnnouncements.ts` holds one shared `outlineAnnouncement` signal plus pure `lineNumberAt`/`headingNavigationAnnouncement` helpers (an `indexOf`-walk line count, not a `split("\n")` copy of the note); new `outline/OutlineLiveRegion.tsx` is the one polite `role="status"` region, mounted exactly once in `App.tsx` (not duplicated inside `OutlinePanel.tsx`/`HeadingBreadcrumbs.tsx`), so a screen reader never hears the same event announced twice when both the Outline panel and the breadcrumbs are visible together. Wired at three points: `OutlinePanel.handleSelect` and `App.tsx`'s breadcrumb `onSelectRoot`/`onSelectHeading` announce "Navigated to `<heading>`, line `<n>`." per spec's "destination heading and line" requirement; `OutlinePanel` gained a 300ms-debounced effect (a new `computeMatchCount` pure function, distinct from the existing `computeVisibleIndexes`, which also counts non-matching ancestors kept visible for context) announcing "N headings match."/"1 heading matches."/"No headings match." only once typing pauses, not on every keystroke; `HeadingLinkActions.handleCopy` announces "Copied link to `<heading>`." once per click, alongside its existing visible "Copied" button-label change, since a screen reader is not guaranteed to announce a focused button's own label mutating. Duplicate-heading warnings were confirmed already textually described via the existing marker's `aria-label`, needing no change. **Found and fixed a real, small, pre-existing bug in passing while building this**: `.sr-only` was used in `WorkspaceProfilesSettings.tsx`'s icon-select label but had no CSS rule anywhere in the repository at all (`grep` across every `.css` file confirmed zero matches), so that label rendered as plain, visibly duplicated text next to its own already-sufficient `aria-label`; added the standard clip-based visually-hidden rule to `styles/theme.css`, fixing that stray visible text and giving `OutlineLiveRegion.tsx` (which depends on the same class) a real effect. 21 new tests: 6 for `lineNumberAt`/`headingNavigationAnnouncement`/`announceOutline` (`outlineAnnouncements.test.ts`), 3 for `OutlineLiveRegion.tsx` rendering/updating/replacing its message, 3 for `computeMatchCount`, and the remainder covering the navigation, filter-debounce, and copy announcements in `OutlinePanel.test.tsx` and `App.test.tsx` (a breadcrumb click, both root and heading). - ✅ **Global configuration corruption recovery**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Codex-default-20260904T143000Z, branch: `agent/global-config-corruption-recovery`; found and fixed 2026-09-04): `decodeGlobalConfig` already produced a safe `{ config, corrupt }` result, but `loadGlobalConfig` discarded the flag and `initSettings` could therefore load malformed `config.json` as defaults without warning. `loadGlobalConfig` now preserves that result, `initSettings` publishes it through `globalConfigCorrupted`, and the ordered global-config writer refuses ordinary theme/profile writes while the flag is set. Regression coverage proves malformed JSON reaches the loader, survives normal settings changes without a write, resumes ordinary persistence only after explicit repair, and remains flagged when that repair fails; a real revert-confirm-restore run removed the startup propagation/guard and made the new test fail (`globalConfigCorrupted` stayed false) before restoring it. - ✅ **F04 Phase 5e3: separate Create block link action**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-01DAZ8K7-20260904T1346Z, branch: `agent/f04-phase5e3-create-block-link`; spec: `spec/f04-heading-block-links-embeds.md`, sections 7.4 and 21 Phase 5): Adds a "Create block link" command-palette entry, alongside the existing "Copy block link" (F04 Phase 5d), sharing the exact same resolution logic (`editor/blockLinkActions.ts`'s `resolveBlockLinkAtCursor`, unchanged): locate the block at the cursor, reuse its existing id if unique, otherwise mint and insert a fresh one.
      - **Why Create is not simply Insert, unlike a heading's own Copy/Insert pair**: spec 7.4 step 7 is "copy or insert the structured link," and `outline/headingLinkOperations.ts` already implements exactly that split for headings (`copyHeadingLink` vs `insertHeadingLink`). But a heading's Insert is invoked from a separate panel (OutlinePanel/HeadingBreadcrumbs) listing every heading, decoupling "which heading" from "where the cursor currently is": insert lands wherever the user is editing, referencing a heading picked from elsewhere. A block link has no such picker; it is always resolved *at* the cursor (spec step 1). Literally inserting the resolved `[[#^id]]` text back at that same cursor would splice a self-reference into the middle of the very block it names (confirmed by tracing through the exact insertion mechanics `blockLinkCopyRequest`'s own consumer already uses), an actively broken outcome, not a smaller version of the heading feature. Create is therefore scoped to spec steps 1-6 only (ensure the block has an id) with no step 7 at all: no clipboard write, no document insertion beyond the marker itself. This is still a genuinely distinct, useful action from Copy: it lets a user stamp a stable id onto a block (to reference later, or by hand) without overwriting whatever they currently have on their clipboard. A true "pick any block and insert a link to it here," mirroring the heading precedent exactly, would need its own block-picker panel first, which is out of this item's scope; documented in `blockLinkActions.ts`'s own module doc comment for the next session that considers it.
      - **Implementation**: `editor/blockLinkRequest.ts` gained a `blockLinkCreateRequest` signal and `requestCreateBlockLinkAtCursor()`, mirroring `blockLinkCopyRequest`'s exact `requestId`-counter shape as an independent signal (not a `mode` field on the existing one, so each request's own dependency array stays precise about which action fired). `editor/MarkdownEditor.tsx` gained a new `blockLinkCreateRequest` prop and consumer effect, identical to the Copy effect's block lookup and marker-insertion dispatch, minus the final `navigator.clipboard.writeText` call. `app/App.tsx`'s command palette gained a "Create block link" entry next to "Copy block link", under the same `headingLinksEnabled && viewMode.value !== "preview"` gate.
      - 5 new tests in `MarkdownEditor.test.tsx` (mirroring the 4 existing Copy tests, plus one new one): creates a new id and inserts its marker without touching the clipboard or the current selection; is a no-op when the block already has a unique id; is a no-op when the cursor is not inside any eligible block; does not re-apply the same `requestId` twice; and that Copy and Create requests are independent (each has its own requestId tracking, confirmed by triggering both in sequence). Followed this file's revert-confirm-restore discipline: confirmed the two behavior-asserting tests (new marker actually inserted; requestId not re-applied) failed for the right reason against the pre-fix code via a real `git stash` of the source changes, then restored. Full verification suite green: `tsc --noEmit`, 1736 Vitest tests (1731 to 1736), `eslint` (0 errors, the same 7 pre-existing warnings, none in a file this claim touched), `npm run check-version`, `vite build`, `cargo test` (57/57, unchanged, no Rust file touched). No manual/on-device UI smoke test was performed in this sandbox; component-level tests against the real CodeMirror `EditorView` are the verification actually performed, the same disclosed limitation every other editor change in this file carries.

</details>

- ✅ **Settings toggle save feedback: "Saving…" indicator added, reactivity confirmed already correct**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-01DAZ8K7-20260904T1329Z, branch: `agent/settings-saving-indicator`; reported 2026-09-04 by the maintainer, live, from real desktop/Mac usage, as toggles "either not working at all or giving no prompt feedback that anything happened for a long time"): Investigated the "not working at all" half first, since the same day's Tags and Accent-color findings immediately above both traced back to this exact item. Read `updateWorkspaceSettings` (`settings/store.ts`): it writes `workspaceSettings.value` synchronously, before the async disk write, so every toggle's own visible state (a switch's active class, a gated panel/button appearing or disappearing) already updates in the same tick as the click, confirmed correct by the Tags/Accent-color tests above and not re-litigated here. The "no prompt feedback for a long time" half was real and unaddressed, though: `SettingsPanel.tsx` had no acknowledgment of an in-flight write at all beyond a toggle's own state change, no "Saving…"/error-adjacent indicator the way a slow or failed native write elsewhere in the app gets one.
      - **Fix**: a new `workspaceSettingsSaving` signal (`settings/store.ts`), true from the moment any workspace-settings write is queued (`queueWorkspaceSettingsWrite`) until every queued and in-flight write has actually drained (`resolveWorkspaceSettingsDrainWaiters`, the same drain-completion check the module already used for its own waiters), covering both a single write and a rapid run of several. `SettingsPanel.tsx`'s modal header shows a small "Saving…" status (`role="status"`) exactly while it is true, next to the panel's own title.
      - **A real, pre-existing test-isolation bug found and fixed while adding this**: `settings/store.test.ts`'s `readTextFile.mockImplementation` override from one describe block survives `vi.clearAllMocks()` into every later block (`clearAllMocks` resets call history, not a custom implementation), so a new describe block appended at the end of the file that calls `setWorkspacePath` without first resetting it can silently inherit an earlier block's invalid-JSON settings content and get marked corrupted before its own tests ever run; caught this describe block's own new tests failing exactly that way when the full file ran together despite passing in isolation. Fixed by explicitly restoring the module's original default (`readTextFile` throwing "not found", meaning no settings file yet) plus `workspaceSettingsCorrupted.value = false` in this new describe block's own `beforeEach`, rather than depending on file execution order.
      - 4 new tests: `store.test.ts` gained 2 (`workspaceSettingsSaving` true synchronously once a write is queued and false once fully drained; stays true across a second update queued while the first is still in flight, confirming the merge-into-pending path keeps the indicator up). `SettingsPanelCurrentTests.tsx` gained 2 (the indicator shows while `workspaceSettingsSaving` is true, is absent when it is not). Followed this file's revert-confirm-restore discipline: confirmed the two `store.test.ts` assertions actually failed against the pre-fix code (via a real `git stash` of the source change, both for the "Cannot read properties of undefined" import-level failure and, after fixing that, the genuine test-isolation failure above) before restoring the fix. Full verification suite green: `tsc --noEmit`, 1731 Vitest tests (1727 to 1731), `eslint` (0 errors, the same 7 pre-existing warnings), `npm run check-version`, `vite build`, `cargo fmt --check`/`cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`/`cargo test` (57/57, unchanged, no Rust file touched)/`cargo check`. No manual/on-device UI smoke test was performed in this sandbox; component-level tests against the real rendered DOM are the verification actually performed, the same disclosed limitation every other UI change in this file carries.

</details>

- ✅ **Accent color live-apply: verified already correct, not a live bug**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-01DAZ8K7-20260904T1301Z, branch: `agent/accent-color-verify`; reported 2026-09-04 by the maintainer, live, from real desktop/Mac usage, as "picking a different accent color... not taking effect live"): Traced the full path from click to rendered color. `SettingsPanel.tsx`'s accent swatch buttons call `updateWorkspaceSettings({ accentColor: option.value })`, the identical call pattern every other setting uses. `App.tsx`'s reactive `effect(() => { root.setAttribute("data-accent", workspaceSettings.value.accentColor); ... })` reads `workspaceSettings.value` directly, so it re-runs on any settings change, not just on mount. `theme.css` defines `[data-accent="ocean"|"forest"|"plum"]` overrides plus bare `:root`'s own `--accent` as the fourth ("warm", the default) case; all four selectors in play (`:root`, `[data-accent="ocean"|"forest"|"plum"]`, and the two theme selectors `:root:not([data-theme="light"])`/`:root[data-theme="dark"]`) have equal or lower specificity than the accent selectors, and the accent rules are the last in source order among the equal-specificity ones, so a chosen accent always wins the cascade over the current theme's own default, in both light and dark mode. A new regression test (`describe("App: accent color applies live (2026-09-04 repro)")`) renders the app, then changes `workspaceSettings.value.accentColor` after mount (the exact "already open Settings, pick a different color" scenario reported) and asserts `document.documentElement`'s `data-accent` attribute updates immediately; it passes against the current, unmodified code.
      - **What this does and does not close**: this confirms the reactive attribute-setting effect, the settings write, and the CSS cascade are all already correct, which was the literal bug as reported, mirroring the same day's "Tags toolbar button" finding immediately below. It does not rule out the maintainer's click never reaching disk in the first place, the same already-open, separately-tracked "Feature on/off toggles in Settings: unreliable or slow UI feedback" item covers (this makes two of that item's reported symptoms now traced back to it specifically, worth weighing when that item is next picked up). If the maintainer still sees no color change after picking one in a real session, the next place to look is that write-persistence path, not this apply/render path, which is now test-locked as correct.

</details>

<details>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-01DAZ8K7-20260904T1226Z, branch: `agent/task-hub-refresh-on-save`; reported 2026-09-04 by the maintainer, live, from real desktop/Mac usage): The Task Hub's `linkIndex.value.tasksByPath` projection was only ever refreshed for a note either by the Task Hub's own checkbox toggle (`taskMutation.ts`'s `toggleTaskCompletion`, already correct) or by a full `rebuildLinkIndex` triggered on the panel's own open transition (`App.tsx`'s `openTaskHubPanel`). An ordinary edit made while the panel was already open, typing a task's `[ ]`/`[x]` directly in the editor, or any other edit to a task line, went through the normal autosave path instead and never touched `tasksByPath` at all, so the row stayed exactly as it was until the panel was closed and reopened. Scoped deliberately to `tasksByPath` only, matching `replaceIndexedTasks`'s own existing scope; `tagsByPath`/`backlinksByPath`/other projections share the same open-transition-only staleness shape but are unaffected by this change and are not claimed to be fixed by it.

Confirmed failing for the right reason (write succeeds, index stays stale) against the pre-fix code via a real `git stash` of the source fix, then restored; a second new test confirms a failed write leaves the index untouched rather than recording tasks from content that was never actually saved. 2 new tests (1718 to 1720 total). - ✅ **saveCoordinator.ts now actually flushes a pending save before a workspace transition drains it, and Switch without saving works**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260904T0722Z, 2026-09-04T07:22Z, direct-to-main, no branch, per this repository's current no-PR coordination policy; found 2026-09-04 while implementing F20 Phase 2b-iii-b, closes the Open Bug that entry's own disclosure logged): `prepareForTransition` (`src/workspace/saveCoordinator.ts`) previously cancelled a pending (not-yet-fired) debounced save's timer outright and only *waited* for an already-in-flight write, then deleted every entry for the session regardless of outcome, silently losing a very recent, still-debouncing edit on any ordinary workspace switch with no warning. Spec `leotheca-workspace-profiles-sdd.md` section 16.3 step 3 ("flush," not cancel) and step 6 ("abort on a save failure," meaning a genuine write error, not merely something being pending) are now both actually implemented: a pending entry gets a real, explicit write attempt (bypassing the ordinary block gate that would otherwise reject it, since this is the coordinator's own authorized flush, not a new edit), an already-in-flight write is awaited, and the whole session's entries are deleted (letting the transition proceed) only once every entry's `revision === savedRevision`; otherwise `prepareForTransition` throws (un-blocking the session first, so editing resumes) and preserves every entry so a subsequent retry attempt picks up exactly where it left off rather than silently discarding the unsaved content.
      - **A genuine race this uncovered, not assumed**: an entry already in flight when the drain starts can gain a *newer* revision while it's being waited on (`change()` bumps `revision` but deliberately skips scheduling a new timer while `inFlight`), and the ordinary reschedule-on-finish is itself gated on the session not being blocked, which it now is during the drain — so that newer revision would otherwise never get written at all. A new `entry.inFlightRevision` field (set/cleared alongside `entry.inFlight`) lets a second, narrower pass give exactly those entries (and only those, never one that was written directly and genuinely failed) a follow-up write; `entry.lastError` alone was tried first and rejected during this same session's own implementation, since a write settling for a stale revision leaves `lastError` exactly as it already was, which could itself be a stale error from an unrelated earlier failure.
      - **Spec 16.6's "Switch without saving" is now real, not merely disclosed as blocked**: `prepareForTransition(session, { discard: true })` skips the flush-and-check entirely (cancel-and-wait only, never throws, always deletes), threaded through `workspaceSaves.prepareForTransition` and `setWorkspacePath(path, token, profile, { discardUnsaved: true })`. `workspaceTransitionRecovery`'s `save_failed` kind (`workspaceTransitionRecovery.ts`) now offers both `retry` and `discard` again (added, then correctly reverted, during F20 Phase 2b-iii-b's own implementation before this fix existed to make it meaningful); `WorkspaceTransitionBanner.tsx`'s "Switch without saving" button is gated behind `window.confirm`, matching spec 16.6's "requires explicit confirmation."
      - **Fixed a real, pre-existing stale test in the same commit, not silently changed to make a new failure disappear**: `saveCoordinator.test.ts`'s "cancels pending outgoing debounces and rejects later edits" encoded the exact cancel-not-flush behavior this fix corrects; replaced with two tests asserting the new behavior (the pending edit is actually written; a *later* edit made after the transition has already started is still correctly rejected) rather than adjusted to keep the old assertion passing.
      - 8 new tests net (1703 to 1711): `saveCoordinator.test.ts` gained 4 net (its one stale test replaced by 2 asserting the corrected behavior, plus 3 new: a genuine flush failure throwing and preserving the entry while un-blocking the session so a real subsequent edit resumes normal autosave, an already-in-flight write failing, and the in-flight-then-newer-revision race actually flushing the newer content). `WorkspaceTransitionBanner.test.tsx` gained 2 (the confirm-then-discard and declined-confirm paths). `settings/workspaceTransition.test.ts` (the real `setWorkspacePath` integration suite) gained 2: a genuine flush failure aborting the switch and restoring A, and `discardUnsaved` proceeding despite the identical failure. `workspaceTransitionRecovery.test.ts`'s own `save_failed` test was updated in place (net 0 new) to expect `discard` alongside `retry` again. Full verification suite green: `tsc --noEmit`, 1711 Vitest tests, `eslint` (0 errors, the same pre-existing 7 warnings from the prior F20 Phase 2b-iii-b commit, none in a file this fix touched), `npm run check-version`, `vite build`, `cargo fmt --check`/`cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`/`cargo test` (57/57, unchanged)/`cargo check`. No Rust or Android source touched. No manual/on-device UI smoke test performed in this sandbox.

</details>

- ✅ **F20 Phase 2b-iii-b: typed transition-state/error model and in-session Retry/Discard UI**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260904T0656Z, 2026-09-04T06:56Z, direct-to-main, no branch, per this repository's current no-PR coordination policy; spec: `spec/leotheca-workspace-profiles-sdd.md` sections 16.1, 16.4-16.6, and 23): Closes out F20 Phase 2b-iii, split from Phase 2b-iii-a (the startup recovery launcher). `src/workspace/workspaceTransition.ts`'s coordinator now exposes a real, section-16.1-shaped `state` signal (`idle`/`saving`/`opening`/`error{targetProfileId, phase, message}`), tracked internally by `run()` itself rather than duplicated by every caller: `saving` fires before `prepareOutgoing`, `opening` before `connectIncoming` (skipped entirely for a transition with no real target, e.g. active-profile forget, which only ever targets "no workspace"), and `idle` right after a successful publish; a failure records which of the four named phases (`save`/`access`/`settings`/`global-config`) it happened in. `run()` gained an optional second `targetProfileId` parameter and `publishFailure` now receives `(error, phase, isCurrent)` instead of just `error`, both purely additive to the existing generation-authority mechanics, which are untouched.
      - **A real, previously-shipped bug found and fixed while wiring this up, not assumed**: `setWorkspacePath`'s `publishFailure` reset `workspacePath`/`workspaceToken` to `null`/`undefined` unconditionally on any failure, even when there was a real outgoing workspace A whose access `prepareOutgoing` had already reconnected two steps earlier, directly contradicting spec 16.4's "A remains or becomes authoritative again" and silently bouncing the user to the blank "no workspace" welcome screen (losing their open tabs) on any in-session switch failure, not just a genuine startup failure. Fixed by branching on whether an outgoing path actually existed: if so, `workspacePath`/`workspaceToken` are restored to their captured outgoing values (never actually lost, since `publishIncoming` never ran) and `restoreLastOpenTabs` re-opens the tabs the transition's own pre-connect drain had already cleared; if not (the genuine startup case F20 Phase 2b-iii-a's `WelcomeDialog` recovery owns), the previous null/defaulted behavior is unchanged. A subtlety this surfaced: restoring `workspacePath` to the *same* value it already held doesn't itself retrigger `FileTree.tsx`'s `[rootPath]`-keyed mount effect (nothing to diff against), so the file tree would otherwise stay stuck empty after the transition's own mid-flight reset sweep; `FileTree.tsx`'s effect now also depends on `workspaceSession` (already bumped on this path) for exactly this reason.
      - **New pure classifier, `src/workspace/workspaceTransitionRecovery.ts`**: `classifyTransitionErrorKind(phase, isAndroid)` maps a failed phase to section 23's typed error kinds (`save_failed`, `permission_missing`/`workspace_missing` for the "access" phase depending on platform, `global_config_save_failed`), and `recoveryActionsFor(kind)` returns that kind's own action list verbatim from section 23 (e.g. `workspace_missing`: Retry, Relink, Open another workspace, Forget), reusing the existing `relinkWorkspaceProfile`/`addWorkspaceFromPicker`/`forgetWorkspaceProfile` actions rather than new mutation paths. `settings/store.ts`'s new `workspaceTransitionRecovery` signal derives the concrete, bound-closure recovery info from `workspaceTransitions.state` plus the target profile's catalog entry, `null` whenever there's nothing to recover (including the no-real-target forget-active case, which stays `WorkspaceForgetUnsavedWorkError`'s own confirmation UI's territory, unchanged). `src/settings/WorkspaceTransitionBanner.tsx` is the new in-session recovery surface: a non-modal banner (mounted in `App.tsx` whenever a workspace is open) rendering exactly the buttons `actions` lists, with real per-button busy state and a dismiss control, deliberately distinct from `WelcomeDialog`'s full-screen startup surface since the outgoing workspace is (per the fix above) still authoritative and visible underneath it.
      - **Explicitly not implemented, disclosed rather than guessed at**: spec 16.6's "Switch without saving" destructive confirmation. Building it correctly needs `saveCoordinator.ts`'s `prepareForTransition` to actually flush a pending (not-yet-fired) debounced save before waiting on in-flight work (spec 16.3 step 3's own word, "flush"), rather than only cancelling a pending timer outright the way it does today, and to report whether anything genuinely failed rather than just "something was pending" — a change to shared drain mechanics every transition (including the already-shipped 2b-i/2b-ii) depends on, not a small addition to this phase. A naive pre-check (`hasUnsavedWork` before every `setWorkspacePath`, mirroring 2b-ii's own forget-active guard) was implemented and then reverted during this same session once a real integration test (`settings/workspaceTransition.test.ts`) showed it aborting an ordinary switch that should have simply waited a moment for an in-flight autosave to finish, which would have made everyday switching worse, not safer. Logged as a new Open Bug below (`saveCoordinator.ts` doesn't actually flush pending saves before a transition drains them") rather than fixed here. `global_config_corrupt` (the catalog file itself failing to decode at startup) is likewise out of scope: it's a startup-only concern this phase's "in-session" framing doesn't cover, and `initSettings` doesn't currently surface `loadGlobalConfig`'s own `corrupt` flag at all, a second, separate, pre-existing gap also logged below rather than fixed in passing.
      - 25 new tests (1678 to 1703 total): `workspaceTransition.test.ts` (the coordinator's own unit tests) gained 8 covering the full `idle`/`saving`/`opening`/`idle` sequence, the no-real-target opening skip, each of the four failure phases, `publishFailure`'s new `(error, phase, isCurrent)` signature, and that a superseded transition's own late failure never touches `state`. `workspaceTransitionRecovery.test.ts` (new) has 10 covering the classifier and action lists exactly against spec 23's own text. `WorkspaceTransitionBanner.test.tsx` (new) has 6 covering empty/populated rendering, per-button busy state, each action's closure being called, the shared relink/grant-access label mapping, and dismiss. `settings/workspaceTransition.test.ts` (the real `setWorkspacePath` integration suite) gained 1 net (two existing "fails closed" tests were updated in place to assert the corrected restore-A behavior rather than counted as new, plus one new test locking in that the genuine startup case still fails fully closed). A real, pre-existing test-isolation bug in `settings/store.test.ts` was found and fixed while running the full suite for the first time with this change (not this phase's own code): a `hasUnsavedWork.mockReturnValue(true)` (not `mockReturnValueOnce`) in one 2b-ii test was queued but never actually consumed by that test (its own `discardUnsaved: true` path short-circuits before ever calling `hasUnsavedWork`), silently leaking into whichever later test called it next; removed, since it was unnecessary for that test's own assertions either way. Full verification suite green: `tsc --noEmit`, 1703 Vitest tests, `eslint` (0 errors; 7 warnings, the pre-existing 6 plus one new `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` false-positive on `FileTree.tsx`'s own `workspaceSession` dependency, the same already-accepted pattern every other signal-reading effect in this codebase triggers), `npm run check-version`, `vite build`, `cargo fmt --check`/`cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`/`cargo test` (57/57, unchanged)/`cargo check`, after bootstrapping the Linux WebKitGTK/GTK3 system libraries per `CONSTITUTION.md`'s cloud-environment bootstrap section. No Rust or Android source touched. No manual/on-device or in-browser UI smoke test was performed in this sandbox, the same disclosed limitation every other UI change in this file carries.

- ✅ **Freehand Phase 1: ink document, stroke-processing math, and edit-history foundation**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (found already implemented on `main`, with no roadmap entry recording it; corrected by this integration pass, Claude-Code-cloud-20260904T0622Z): `src/ink/inkDocument.ts`, `src/ink/strokeProcessing.ts`, and `src/ink/inkEditing.ts` already exist on `main` (39 tests: `inkDocument.test.ts` 8, `strokeProcessing.test.ts` 11, `inkEditing.test.ts` 20), matching `documentation/INK_DRAWING.md`'s own "first implementation slice" description, but the "Freehand drawing and handwriting (stylus ink)" bullet under Open Features still described the entire feature as unstarted, with no phase breakdown reflecting this. `inkDocument.ts` defines the versioned JSON document contract (`InkStroke`/`InkPoint`/`InkViewport`) with a lossless decode that preserves unknown top-level fields and unrecognized future stroke shapes across a decode/save round trip, the same forward-compatibility rule Canvas's own document contract (F-010) already uses. `strokeProcessing.ts` is the DOM-independent math shared by desktop and Android WebViews: Catmull-Rom smoothing over raw pointer samples, with pressure/tilt/time interpolated linearly between measured points so the spline can't invent physically impossible values, and a continuously variable brush width with a bounded secondary tilt contribution. `inkEditing.ts` is pure undo/redo history (`createInkHistory`/`commitInkEdit`/`undoInkEdit`/`redoInkEdit`) plus point-radius eraser logic (`eraseStrokeParts`/`eraseInkAtPoint`) that splits an erased stroke into surviving runs rather than only supporting whole-stroke deletion. `documentation/INK_DRAWING.md`'s own "Deferred to later phases" list was also stale against this: it named "undo/redo stack" and "eraser interaction" as not yet started, when both already exist as pure, fully unit-tested logic; corrected in the same commit as this roadmap entry to describe what is actually deferred, which is UI/input integration only (a workspace `TabKind`, toolbar command, file creation flow, canvas renderer, color picker, pointer-capture drawing surface, pinch-zoom/pan gesture handling, coalesced-event collection, and palm-rejection policy), not the underlying data model or its operations. Also added a `src/ink/` row to `documentation/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s module table, which had no mention of this module at all. **A related, more complete prior attempt was found on an orphaned branch, not recovered by this pass**: `agent/freehand-phase1-ink-foundation` (no common ancestor with current `main`, a casualty of one of this repository's own history rewrites) contains an actual `InkSurface.tsx` pointer-capture component plus additional undo/redo and partial-erasing wiring and a pointer-precision fix (final-sample preservation on pointer-up) beyond what's on `main` today. Its content was not cherry-picked into this change since verifying it against the current codebase and design is real implementation work in its own right, not a documentation correction; left here so a future session claiming the remaining Freehand work knows real prior art exists and where to look, rather than starting the UI layer from zero. No implementation code was changed by this entry, only documentation and this roadmap correction.

</details>

- ✅ **F20 Phase 2b-iii-a: recovery actions for an unavailable active profile at startup, and a recent-profiles list when none is active**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260904T0020Z, branch: agent/f20-phase2b-iii-a-startup-recovery; spec: `spec/leotheca-workspace-profiles-sdd.md` sections 17.2, 17.3, and 9.4): `WelcomeDialog.tsx` (the app's only "no workspace open" surface, shown whenever `App.tsx`'s `rootPath` is empty) is no longer one generic "choose a folder" message for every case. Rather than accept that risk, an inactive Android profile's relink target is committed on the same trust level `addWorkspaceFromPicker` already gives a freshly picked folder before it's ever opened (the platform grant itself is the only validation); real access/settings validation then happens the ordinary way the next time that profile is activated. See the F20 Phase 2b-iii/iv bullets under Open Features.
