- ✅ **F03 Phase 2a: rename/move reference-rewrite planning engine**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260903T1849Z, no branch, direct-to-main; spec: `spec/f03-link-integrity-refactor-center.md` sections 9-10; a further split of the former "F03 Phase 2" bullet, per `skills/phase-splitting-large-specs.md`'s "split further when a phase is still too large" guidance, aligned to the spec's own rollout-plan boundary between its "Phase 2: Note rename preview" and "Phase 3: Transaction executor and recovery": Phase 2a here is the pure planning half of the spec's own Phase 2, not yet the dialog UI or the Apply step, see "F03 Phase 2b" below for the rest): New `refactor/renamePlan.ts`, `planNoteRename(oldPath, newPath, index, readNote)`, computes exactly which structured wikilinks elsewhere in the workspace would need rewriting for a single note's rename or move, and their exact rewritten text, without touching a single file, without any dialog UI, and without the note being renamed itself ever needing an edit.

      **Grounded in this codebase's real resolver, confirmed by reading it rather than assumed from the spec**: spec 7.1 describes a five-step note-resolution precedence (workspace-relative path, referrer-relative path, basename, alias, unresolved), but `linking/store.ts`'s actual `resolveWikilink` only ever implements the last two (confirmed reading its real ~8-line implementation). A path-qualified or `.md`-suffixed wikilink target therefore never resolves in this app today, to anything, which means it can never appear in this module's edit list either. This makes two of spec 7.3's serialization rules ("path-qualified wikilinks remain path-qualified", "`.md` extension presence is preserved") non-issues for this module rather than something it has to implement, and makes spec 9.3's "relative references inside the moved note because its base directory changes" inapplicable too (a basename-only resolver has no concept of a referrer's directory, so moving a note never needs to rewrite anything inside the moved note's own body; this module never reads or edits the note actually being renamed). Each of these narrowings is disclosed directly in `renamePlan.ts`'s own doc comment with the reasoning, not silently assumed.

      **Freshness (spec 9.5, "the preview is immutable... any file modification after analysis invalidates the plan")**: `LinkIndex.wikiLinksByPath` is used only as a fast candidate filter for which notes are even worth reading, never trusted for the plan's actual source ranges or resolution outcome; both are always recomputed against a freshly-read, freshly-`parseWikiLinks`'d version of each candidate note, confirmed by a dedicated test that plants a stale cached offset the real content has since shifted away from and asserts the plan uses the fresh one. A note that no longer actually references `oldPath` by the time it's read is silently excluded rather than planned against stale data (also tested). **One disclosed limit this candidate-filter design doesn't solve**, called out in both the module doc comment and its own test: a note with zero wikilinks at all when the index was last built is never a candidate in the first place, so a reference added to it since then is invisible to this function regardless of how fresh its own read would be; closing this needs a genuinely fresh `rebuildLinkIndex` immediately before planning, left for whichever phase wires this into the real Analyze step.

      **Ambiguity (spec 7.3)**: a basename link that would become ambiguous after the rename (the new basename collides with another existing note) is deliberately not "upgraded" to a path-qualified form the way the spec's fuller text suggests, since the real resolver above cannot resolve a path-qualified target at all, upgrading would silently turn a working link into a broken one; it is reported as a blocked edit with an explanation instead, matching spec 9.2's "primary action is disabled while blocked changes remain" intent one level down at the single-edit granularity.

      15 new tests in `refactor/renamePlan.test.ts`: basic rewrite, fragment/label preservation, a same-basename folder move producing no text change, finding every referrer across the workspace, never touching the renamed note's own body, the ambiguity-block case and its non-trigger case, three freshness cases (a removed reference correctly excluded, the disclosed zero-wikilinks-at-index-time gap, and a shifted offset correctly re-derived fresh), an unreadable candidate note silently skipped, a no-op same-path call, an empty-plan case, and case-insensitive matching that still rewrites to the target's real casing. Followed this file's revert-confirm-restore discipline for the trickiest logic (the freshness re-derivation and the ambiguity check): confirmed those specific assertions fail for the expected reasons against a stale-offset/no-freshness-recheck stub before the real implementation, then restored.

      **Explicitly not done here**, tracked in "F03 Phase 2b" below: the Define/Analyze/Review/Apply dialog UI and its file-tree/note-menu/command-palette/diagnostic-repair entry points (spec 9.1-9.2); Markdown-style `[text](path)`/`![alt](path)` link migration (spec 6.2, this claim is structured wikilinks only); application-metadata path migration (spec 6.4: tabs, bookmarks, workspace settings, collection filters); the actual Apply/mutation step, journal, and rollback (spec's own Phase 3); and folder-level rename/move (spec 9.4, spec's own Phase 4). This claim never mutates a file and ships no UI, so it is inert in the running app until Phase 2b wires it in; this is a foundational module landed ahead of its consumer, the same "pure module before UI" precedent `skills/phase-splitting-large-specs.md` names as proven here already. Full verification suite green: `tsc --noEmit`, 1614 Vitest tests (up from 1599 immediately before this claim, net +15), `eslint` (0 errors, same pre-existing unrelated warnings, untouched by this claim), `npm run check-version`, `vite build`, `cargo check` (this claim touches no Rust source; confirms nothing else regressed). No manual/on-device verification applies: no UI exists yet for this claim to exercise.

</details>

- ✅ **F04 Phase 5d: block-reference DOM rendering hook and Copy block link action**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260903T1836Z, no branch, direct-to-main; spec: `spec/f04-heading-block-links-embeds.md` section 21 Phase 5; narrowed from the former combined "F04 Phase 5d" entry per `skills/phase-splitting-large-specs.md`, see "F04 Phase 5e" below for the remaining scope): Closes two of that entry's three disclosed gaps: spec 7.3's deterministic DOM ID/`data-lt-block-id` rendering hook, and spec 7.4's generated-ID flow via a new command-palette "Copy block link" action.

      **`markdown/blocks.ts`**: generalized the scanner's internal walker (now exported as `scanBlocks`) to report every eligible block (paragraph/list-item/blockquote/fenced-code) whether or not it already carries a `^id` marker, not only ones that do; `scanBlockIds` is now a thin marker-only filter over it, confirmed behavior-identical by its own full pre-existing 44-test suite passing completely unmodified. New `findBlockAtOffset(content, offset)` finds the block enclosing a given cursor position, with or without an existing marker, built on the same walk. Incidentally fixed one genuine pre-existing gap the generalization surfaced directly: a fenced code block that closes as the very last thing in a document (no line ever arrives after it) was previously never recorded at all, even once it had a valid marker, since nothing ever consumed the deferred one-line marker check; now flushed unconditionally at end-of-scan.

      **`editor/blockLinkActions.ts`** (new): implements spec 7.4's full flow as one pure function, `resolveBlockLinkAtCursor(content, cursorOffset)`. Locates the block at the cursor via `findBlockAtOffset`; if it already has a unique (non-duplicate) marker, reuses that id; otherwise generates a fresh id (`generateBlockId`, `crypto.randomUUID()` truncated to 8 hex characters behind a `b-` prefix, this codebase's own established CSPRNG source, shaped after spec 7.4's own `b-7k3m2p9d` example) confirmed unique against every existing id in the note (`uniqueBlockId`, bounded retry loop), and describes exactly where to insert it (`block.contentTo`, `\n^id` for a fenced-code block since its marker is always its own separate line, ` ^id` inline for every other kind). Builds the `[[#^id]]` link text through `wikiSyntax.ts`'s `serializeWikiLink`, F04's one allowed link-text creator, never by string concatenation. **Two disclosed, deliberate narrowings from spec 7.4's literal text**, tracked in "F04 Phase 5e" below rather than silently assumed: only a single "Copy block link" action exists (a separate "Create block link" would invoke the identical underlying operation for no distinguishable outcome, since Copy already performs the create-if-needed flow itself); and a block whose *existing* marker is itself ambiguous (a duplicate elsewhere in the note) is left alone by this action rather than silently minted a second id, the same "never touch what's already there" restraint `outline/headingLinkOperations.ts` already applies to a duplicate heading.

      **`editor/MarkdownEditor.tsx`**: new `blockLinkCopyRequest` prop (mirrors `insertRequest`'s `requestId`-counter pattern) consumed by a dedicated effect that reads the *live* `EditorView`'s own current cursor and document, not the possibly-stale `value` prop, calls `resolveBlockLinkAtCursor`, dispatches one CodeMirror transaction if an id needs creating, then copies the resulting link. Deliberately never touches the current selection itself (unlike `insertRequest`): CodeMirror maps the existing selection forward across the edit on its own, exactly like any other edit typed elsewhere on the same line. New `app/App.tsx` command-palette entry "Copy block link" (gated on `viewMode.value !== "preview"` and `headingLinksEnabled`, the same escape hatch this whole feature already respects) raises the request via new `editor/blockLinkRequest.ts` (mirrors `outline/outlineNavigation.ts`'s existing request-signal shape).

      **`editor/MarkdownPreview.tsx`**: `stripBlockIdMarkers` now replaces (not merely deletes) a *uniquely*-identified block's marker with an invisible anchor element instead of plain removal; a duplicate (ambiguous) marker keeps the prior plain-strip behavior unchanged, since it has no single element it could unambiguously name. The anchor is an inline `<span>` spliced into the block's own text for a paragraph/list-item/blockquote marker (safe inside the surrounding `<p>`/`<li>`/`<blockquote>`), or a block-level `<div>` on its own line for a fenced-code marker (its marker is always a separate line, and a block-level tag inline would instead split the surrounding text's own `<p>` in two, per how browsers parse `<div>` inside `<p>`; this exact bug was caught and fixed during this session's own revert-confirm-restore cycle, see below). A new `useEffect` (alongside the existing image-resolution/embed-resolution effects) finds every `[data-lt-block-anchor]` element once the HTML actually reaches the DOM, resolves its real host element from `data-lt-block-kind` (closest `p`/`li`/`blockquote` for the three inline kinds, the immediately preceding sibling for fenced-code), sets a deterministic `id` (`lt-block-<id>`) and `data-lt-block-id` on it, and discards the anchor; a host that can't be found is a silent no-op for that one marker, never a crash.

      Followed this file's revert-confirm-restore discipline, and it caught a real bug before this was considered done: the first implementation used a single `<span>` anchor tag for every block kind including fenced-code; the new fenced-code test failed with the anchor's host resolving to `undefined` instead of the `<pre>` element, because `marked`/CommonMark does not recognize a bare `<span>...</span>` alone on its own line as a block-level HTML passthrough (unlike the block-level tag names CommonMark's HTML-block rules actually cover), so it got auto-wrapped in a new `<p>` instead of standing alone as a sibling of `<pre>`. Fixed by using `<div>` (matching `embedFrameHtml`'s own established precedent in this same file) for the fenced-code case specifically, confirmed by the same test then passing. Every other new test (paragraph/list-item/blockquote anchor placement, duplicate-marker exclusion, the `headingLinksEnabled` escape hatch, multiple independent ids in one note) was also confirmed to fail for the expected reason against the pre-fix code before this was restored and confirmed green.

      25 new tests: 12 in `markdown/blocks.test.ts` (`scanBlocks`'s markerless boundaries for every kind, the end-of-document and fence-opens-immediately fenced-code edge cases, `scanBlockIds`/`scanBlocks` equivalence), 7 in `editor/blockLinkActions.test.ts` (id generation/uniqueness, existing-unique-marker reuse, ambiguous-marker refusal, fresh-id insertion for every block kind, collision avoidance), 4 in `editor/MarkdownEditor.test.tsx` (create-and-copy without moving the selection, reuse-existing, no-op outside any block, no double-apply on a repeated `requestId`), 7 in `editor/MarkdownPreview.test.tsx` (DOM id/`data-lt-block-id` for every block kind, duplicate-marker exclusion, the disabled-feature escape hatch, multiple independent ids). `documentation/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s `editor/`/`linking/` rows updated to match. Full verification suite green: `tsc --noEmit`, 1599 Vitest tests (up from 1563 immediately before this claim, net +36: the 25 above plus 11 more that landed in the same window from unrelated concurrent roadmap items already merged to `main`), `eslint` (0 errors, same pre-existing unrelated warnings, untouched by this claim), `npm run check-version`, `vite build`, `cargo check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test` (57/57) — this change touches no Rust source, so these confirm nothing else regressed rather than testing the change itself. No manual/on-device UI smoke test was performed in this sandbox (a pure TypeScript editor/rendering change with no platform-specific surface, the same class of gap several other roadmap items already carry).

- ✅ **Fix: black fringe/ring on the Android app icon's curved edges**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260903T1650Z, no branch, direct-to-main small bugfix, found and fixed at the maintainer's own explicit live report, with a screenshot from a real device, of "weird black background at curved borders"): Root cause, confirmed with pixel-level inspection (Pillow) rather than guessed: `android/app/src/main/res/mipmap-*/ic_launcher_round.png` (the pre-flattened round icon the manifest's `android:roundIcon` points at, used directly by launchers that render icons as circles) had the *exact same* navy/cream/gold RGB content as its sibling `ic_launcher.png` at every density (confirmed pixel-identical via `ImageChops.difference`), but a completely different alpha mask: `ic_launcher.png`'s circular edge is properly antialiased (a soft multi-step alpha ramp, matching the master `resources/icon.png`'s own antialiasing quality), while `ic_launcher_round.png`'s was a hard 0/255 binary cutoff with zero antialiasing at any density. A hard-edged alpha mask is a known, well-documented source of dark fringing once any consumer resamples or re-masks the bitmap (exactly what a launcher does to fit an icon into its grid/search-result size) — the launcher's resampling blends across the hard edge, and since every fully-transparent pixel in these assets also stored pure black RGB `(0,0,0)` rather than the navy background color underneath it (confirmed at multiple corners across all 5 densities and all 3 PNG types, including the adaptive-icon `ic_launcher_foreground.png`), that blend pulls toward black instead of navy, producing the reported ring. Fixed in two steps, both content-preserving (verified no visible/opaque pixel changed, only pixels that were already either fully transparent or already-correct): (1) replaced `ic_launcher_round.png` at every density with `ic_launcher.png`'s own (already properly antialiased) pixel data, since the two were already meant to be the same image; (2) across all 15 launcher PNGs (`ic_launcher.png`/`ic_launcher_round.png`/`ic_launcher_foreground.png` × 5 densities), rewrote every fully-transparent pixel's RGB from black to the actual navy background color `#152229`, a standard defensive fix for this exact class of resize/mask haloing bug, touching only pixels whose alpha was already 0. Verified with a supersampled render simulating the real adaptive-icon compositing pipeline (navy background + foreground layer + circular mask, downsampled) and by compositing the fixed `ic_launcher_round.png` directly over a contrasting background: both render with a clean edge, no fringe, in this sandbox's own Pillow-based preview. No generation script exists in the repo for these assets (a prior session's one-off, per that item's own roadmap entry), so this fix operated directly on the checked-in PNGs via Pillow rather than re-deriving from source. **What's not verified**: no physical Android device in this sandbox to see the fixed icon rendered by a real launcher; the maintainer's own screenshot was the original repro and only a real on-device check (or a fresh Release build install) can close the loop on this specific report. No TypeScript/Rust source touched, so no code verification suite applies; this is a binary-asset-only fix.

</details>
- ✅ **Fix: Workspace switcher dropdown invisible on Android**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260903T1610Z, no branch, direct-to-main small bugfix, found and fixed at the maintainer's own explicit live report, with a screenshot, that tapping the toolbar's "Workspace" dropdown showed nothing: "its hidden in the background"): Root cause was CSS overflow clipping, not a z-index/stacking-context problem as first suspected. `App.css`'s `@media (max-width: 720px)` block sets `.toolbar { overflow-x: auto; }` so its buttons can scroll horizontally on a narrow viewport (every Android phone) — and per the CSS spec, setting one axis's `overflow` away from `visible` forces the other axis away from `visible` too, so `.toolbar` clips vertically as well, not just horizontally. `.workspace-switcher-menu` was `position: absolute; top: calc(100% + var(--space-1))`, positioned to drop down *below* `.toolbar`'s own box — exactly the region the toolbar's own overflow clips on mobile. The dropdown was genuinely rendering, with a higher `z-index` (20) than every other candidate explanation considered and ruled out first (the mobile `.sidebar` overlay's `z-index: 10`, which a plain z-index comparison already put underneath it), but clipped invisible before z-index/stacking order ever mattered. Fixed by switching `.workspace-switcher-menu` to `position: fixed`, with `top`/`left` now computed in `WorkspaceSwitcher.tsx` from the trigger button's own `getBoundingClientRect()` at open time and applied as an inline style, escaping `.toolbar`'s clip region entirely — the same `position: fixed` plus JS-computed-coordinates approach `workspace/FileContextMenu.tsx`'s `.context-menu` already uses in this codebase for the identical reason, not a new pattern. Followed this file's revert-confirm-restore discipline: reverted `WorkspaceSwitcher.tsx` only (kept the new test), confirmed the new positioning test failed for the expected reason (`expected '' to be '68px'`, since the menu had no inline `top`/`left` at all pre-fix), then restored the fix and confirmed it passes. 1 new test in `WorkspaceSwitcher.test.tsx` (11 total, up from 10) asserting the menu's inline `top`/`left` are derived from a mocked trigger rect, not a static CSS offset. Full verification suite green: `tsc --noEmit`, 1553 Vitest tests (up from 1552, net +1), `eslint` (0 errors). **What's not verified**: no physical Android device or on-device build in this sandbox to visually confirm the dropdown now appears where expected (right below the trigger, not clipped, not obscured by anything else); the maintainer's own screenshot was the original repro and only a real on-device check can close the loop on this specific report.

</details>

- ✅ **Fix macOS/Windows release build failure: case-colliding file names**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260903T1520Z, no branch, direct-to-main small bugfix, found and fixed at the maintainer's own explicit live request to investigate why the release pipeline was failing): The `Release` workflow's `macos` and `windows` jobs had been failing on every single push to `main` for a long time (every prior run back through at least run #217 shows `conclusion: failure` for the workflow as a whole), silently masked because the overall workflow only reports success/failure at the whole-run level while `publish` itself (gated only on `validation`/`version-guard`/`linux`/`android`) kept succeeding regardless, so the rolling `dev-build` release never stopped updating and nobody was forced to look. Root cause, found from the real job logs (`Build universal DMG`/`Build MSI` failing inside `npm run build`'s `tsc` step): `src/outline/` had both `headingLinkActions.ts` (the copy/insert-link logic module) and `HeadingLinkActions.tsx` (the shared row-action component), differing only in the first letter's case. Linux's case-sensitive filesystem, every CI job except `macos`/`windows` and every local dev environment used so far, resolves the two paths independently and never noticed; macOS (APFS, case-insensitive by default) and Windows (NTFS, case-insensitive) collide them, and TypeScript's module resolution picked the wrong file for a bare `./HeadingLinkActions` import in `HeadingBreadcrumbs.tsx`/`OutlineRowContent.tsx`, both of which need the component, not the logic module, breaking the whole `npm run build`. Fixed by renaming the logic module to `headingLinkOperations.ts` (matching this codebase's existing convention of giving a pure-logic module a distinct, description-based name rather than a case-only variant of its paired component's name, e.g. `workspaceProfiles.ts`/`WorkspaceSwitcher.tsx`), updating every import and doc-comment cross-reference (`headingLinkOperations.test.ts`, `HeadingLinkActions.tsx`, `outlineNavigation.ts`, `wikiSyntax.ts`, `documentation/ARCHITECTURE.md`). Checked every directory in the tracked repository, not just this one, for any other filename pair colliding only by case; found none. Pure rename, no logic changed: full verification suite green (`tsc --noEmit`, all 1539 Vitest tests unchanged in count, `eslint` 0 errors, `vite build`, `npm run check-version`). **Confirmed on the real target platforms, not just inferred**: pushed directly to `main` (commit `9498e02`), then watched the next `Release` run (33772067436, triggered by the following README commit `5c7d5d1`) to completion — `macos`'s `Build universal DMG` step and `windows`'s `Build MSI` step both completed successfully (previously both failed within 5-8 seconds at the `tsc` step), and the workflow's overall conclusion is `success` for the first time in this repository's visible run history. The release body no longer needs a "macOS/Windows build not included" note for an ordinary push.

</details>

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260903T1223Z, branch: agent/f04-phase5c-crossnote-block-precheck, spec: `spec/f04-heading-block-links-embeds.md`, section 21 Phase 5; split from the former combined "F04 Phase 5b" entry per `skills/phase-splitting-large-specs.md`, narrowed to the one sub-item that mirrors Phase 5a's already-shipped pattern exactly, see "F04 Phase 5d" below for the rest of that former entry's scope): Closes the disclosed gap that a `[[Note#^block-id]]`/`![[Note#^block-id]]` cross-note fragment still only verified at the note level, never against the target note's actual blocks, always rendering "resolved" once the note itself existed, the same class of gap Phase 5a already closed for cross-note headings. New `LinkIndex.blocksByPath` (`linking/store.ts`) is a sparse map mirroring `headingsByPath` exactly, populated in `rebuildLinkIndex` from the same per-note read and mtime/size cache already used for wikilinks/headings/tasks (`markdown/blocks.ts`'s `scanBlockIds`, no second workspace walk); the persisted on-disk cache's `CachedNote` gained a `blocks` field, validated by a new `isValidBlockRecord` decoder mirroring `isValidHeadingRecord`, and `LINK_INDEX_CACHE_VERSION` bumped 6 -> 7 (same precedent as every earlier `CachedNote` shape change: an old-shaped cache entry has no `blocks` field, and without the bump could still "match" a current file's mtime/size and be reused as a false cache hit reporting zero blocks forever). New `wikiResolver.ts` export `crossNoteBlocksFor(record)`, the exact analog of `crossNoteHeadingsFor`: `undefined` (meaning "don't verify, resolve at the note level") for a same-note record, a non-block fragment, or a note target that doesn't itself resolve; the target note's blocks from `LinkIndex.blocksByPath` otherwise, including a deliberate empty array (not `undefined`) when the resolving note has no entry in that sparse map, so a genuinely nonexistent cross-note block correctly reports "missing-fragment" rather than silently passing as note-level "resolved." Wired into both `MarkdownPreview.tsx`'s `renderWikilinksStructured` (the existing `blockStatus`/href/title machinery, already correct for the same-note case, needed zero further changes once fed the right `targetBlocks`, the same "no downstream changes needed" result Phase 5a got for headings) and `editor/livePreview.ts`'s `classifyBlockLink` (now returns "missing"/"ambiguous" for a cross-note fragment too, not just "resolved"/"broken"; `embedLinkClass` inherits this for free since it already delegates to `classifyBlockLink` for a block-fragment embed). Embeds' actual content extraction is unaffected, confirmed by reading the code rather than assumed: that path only ever checks `target.status === "missing-note"`/`notePath` existence, never `target.block`. Three existing tests asserting the old "resolved once the note exists, without verifying the block" behavior (one each in `MarkdownPreview.test.tsx`, `livePreview.test.ts`'s block-links describe, and implicitly covered by the new embed tests) were rewritten to assert the new verified resolved/missing/ambiguous set instead, the same update Phase 5a's own landing made for the equivalent heading tests. <details>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260903T1100Z, branch: agent/f04-phase5a-crossnote-heading-precheck, spec: `spec/f04-heading-block-links-embeds.md`, section 21 Phase 5; split from the former combined "F04 Phase 5" entry per `skills/phase-splitting-large-specs.md`, narrowed to the heading half of its own "Preview/Source-mode cross-note pre-check" sub-item, see "F04 Phase 5c" above for the block half and "F04 Phase 5d" below for the rest): Closes the disclosed gap that neither Preview nor Source-mode decoration verified a cross-note `[[Note#Heading]]`/`![[Note#Heading]]` fragment against the target note's actual headings, always rendering "resolved" once the note itself existed. `linking/store.ts`'s `LinkIndex.headingsByPath` already existed and its own doc comment already stated it exists specifically "to verify a *cross-note* `[[Note#Heading]]` fragment without a second file read per link" — built for exactly this by an earlier phase and never wired up until now. New `linking/wikiResolver.ts` export `crossNoteHeadingsFor(record)`: returns `undefined` (meaning "don't verify, resolve at the note level" in `resolveWikiLinkTarget`'s own existing contract) for a same-note record, a non-heading fragment, or a note target that doesn't itself resolve; returns the target note's headings from `LinkIndex.headingsByPath` — deliberately including an empty array, not `undefined`, when the resolving note has no entry in that sparse map, since that map's own convention is "absent means zero headings," not "not looked up yet" — once the note resolves, so a genuinely nonexistent cross-note heading correctly reports "missing-fragment" instead of silently passing as note-level "resolved." Wired into both `MarkdownPreview.tsx`'s `renderWikilinksStructured` (the existing headingStatus/href/title machinery already handled same-note missing/ambiguous correctly and needed zero further changes once fed the right `targetHeadings`) and `editor/livePreview.ts`'s `classifyHeadingLink` (now returns "missing"/"ambiguous" for a cross-note fragment too, not just "resolved"/"broken"; `embedLinkClass` inherits this for free since it already delegates to `classifyHeadingLink` for a heading-fragment embed, so `![[Note#Heading]]`'s Source-mode decoration is corrected as well, matching this entry's own "and now embeds too" language from the original combined Phase 5 bullet). Embeds' actual content extraction is unaffected: that path only ever checked `target.status === "missing-note"`/`notePath` existence, never `target.heading`, so this change is inert for embed rendering itself (confirmed by reading the code, not assumed) — only the two decoration passes' pre-click/pre-render status changed. Explicitly out of this narrow phase's scope: the cross-note *block* fragment pre-check (needed a new `LinkIndex.blocksByPath` sparse map mirroring `headingsByPath`, which didn't exist yet), landed separately as "F04 Phase 5c" above; block-ID eligibility for headings, copy/create-block-link actions, and the DOM-ID/`data-lt-block-id` work remain in "F04 Phase 5d" below. Shares `LinkIndex`'s own pre-existing startup-timing characteristics (a link can transiently read as unverified during the very first index build right after a workspace opens, same as note-level "does this note exist" resolution already could) — not a new limitation this phase introduces, only extending the same existing authority one level deeper, disclosed directly in `wikiResolver.ts`'s own new doc comment. Followed this file's revert-confirm-restore discipline: stashed the three implementation files (keeping the new/updated tests), confirmed all 15 new/changed assertions failed for the expected reasons (`crossNoteHeadingsFor is not a function`, wrong resolved/status classes) against the pre-fix code, then restored the fix and confirmed all pass. `documentation/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s `editor/`/`linking/` rows updated to match. This entry adds three more, each distilled from a real session's hard-won findings rather than written speculatively: **`ci-failure-triage.md`** (get the actual failing step and its real log tail before theorizing; classify a failure as this-diff/drifted-generated-artifact/external/flake with real evidence — a prior-green run comparison, one confirmatory re-run — not a guess; never claim a fix confirmed without checking the run it triggered); **`packaging-submission-pipelines.md`** (the F-Droid and Flathub submission pipelines' non-obvious gotchas: each pins its own exact commit SHA independently, a bad merge once silently replaced a real SHA with a non-existent tag; Flathub's offline `node-sources.json`/`cargo-sources.json` must be regenerated from the *currently-pinned* commit's own lockfiles specifically, a subtly wrong-commit regeneration produced the same-looking failure as a stale one; `npm ci --offline` alone is not sufficient for this project's real eslint peer-dependency conflicts and needs `--legacy-peer-deps` too; F-Droid's official buildserver Docker image is a floating tag whose own regressions can look identical to a repository bug); and an addition to `roadmap-workflow.md`'s "Reclaiming an abandoned claim" section (check the claim branch's most recent commit *author*, not just its timestamp, before ever reassigning — a real, live, minutes-old maintainer commit is not the same situation as a stale automated session's claim, even under an explicit maintainer instruction to reassign claims generally, since that instruction is reasonably read as targeting stale bot claims, not the maintainer's own concurrent work). - ✅ **F04 Phase 3f: embed Source-mode decoration**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260903T0650Z, branch: agent/f04-phase3f-embed-decoration, spec: `spec/f04-heading-block-links-embeds.md` section 21 Phase 3): Adds dedicated Source-mode decoration for an embed (`![[Note]]`/`![[Note#Heading]]`/`![[Note#^block-id]]`) in `editor/livePreview.ts`, closing this specific gap the former combined "F04 Phase 3f/5" entry named (see the "F04 Phase 5" entry below for the rest of that entry's scope, split off unchanged). **A real, previously-shipped bug found and fixed, not just a missing feature**: before this claim, an embed carrying a heading or block fragment was not actually undecorated, it was already being decorated, incorrectly, by the pre-existing heading-link/block-link passes, since both filtered only on `record.fragment?.kind`, never on `record.kind`. Confirmed directly with a throwaway probe test before writing any fix (`buildLiveDecorations` against `"See ![[#Intro]] here."`) rather than assumed from reading the code: those passes hid `from, from + 2` assuming that span is always the opening `[[`, true for a plain link but not for an embed, whose `sourceFrom` includes its leading `!` (see `linking/wikiSyntax.ts`'s own doc comment on that field); for an embed this hid `![` instead, leaving a stray, un-hidden `[` character visible in the rendered line and no visual sign that markup had even been hidden as `[[`. A *second* problem existed alongside it: the plain-`WIKILINK_PATTERN` pass, blind to any leading `!`, independently matched the same `[[...]]` substring and applied its own separate, conflicting "broken" mark, so a resolved embed carried two overlapping, contradictory decorations at once. Fixed by a new dedicated third pass (`embedLinkClass`) that computes correct offsets for the `!`-shifted range (hides only the real `[[`/`]]`, leaving `!` itself visible as the visual cue that this is an embed, not a link) and registers both its own full range and the inner `[[...]]` range `WIKILINK_PATTERN` would independently compute into the existing `fragmentLinkRanges` dedup set, so neither older pass touches an embed anymore (both now explicitly exclude `record.kind === "embed"`). `embedLinkClass` dispatches on the embed's own fragment kind (heading, block, or none for a plain whole-note embed) and delegates to the *existing* `classifyHeadingLink`/`classifyBlockLink` for the fragment cases rather than a second, duplicate resolution implementation, since both already key only on `record.noteTarget`/`record.fragment`, never `record.kind`, and so already work correctly for an embed record as-is; a missing/ambiguous fragment reuses the exact same `HEADING_LINK_CLASS`/`BLOCK_LINK_CLASS` lookup a plain link already uses (the state means the same thing either way), while a fully "resolved"/"broken" embed gets two new dedicated classes, `cm-live-embed-resolved`/`cm-live-embed-broken` (`App.css`), the same color pairing as the link equivalents with an added italic so an embed still reads as visually distinct from a plain link at a glance even when both point at fully valid targets. **Also checked, not assumed, before claiming**: the former combined entry's own "embeds... have no autocomplete trigger at all" wording was stale and is corrected here rather than carried forward or reimplemented; `wikilinkCompletions`/`headingLinkCompletions`/`blockLinkCompletions` in `MarkdownEditor.tsx` all trigger via CodeMirror's `matchBefore` against a regex anchored only at a literal `[[`, with no anchoring to the start of the typed text, so a leading `!` immediately before `[[` never prevented any of the three from matching; confirmed directly with a probe test (`wikilinkCompletions` against a document ending in `"![["`, real suggestions returned) rather than trusted from the prior entry's own description. No autocomplete work was needed or attempted. 16 new tests in `livePreview.test.ts` (58 total, up from 42, in a new "embeds (F04 Phase 3f)" describe block): the exact stray-bracket regression case above (asserting `"!["` is never in the hidden set while `"[["`/`"]]"` both are), same-note heading/block resolved/missing/ambiguous, whole-note and cross-note resolved/broken, exactly-once decoration (never also a plain-wikilink or link-pass class), marker visibility while actively editing, inline-code-span exclusion, and coexistence with a plain link and a heading-link in the same document. Followed this file's revert-confirm-restore discipline: reverted `livePreview.ts`/`App.css` locally and confirmed 8 of the 10 status-assertion tests (the two pure marker-visibility/code-exclusion tests that don't depend on the new pass existing at all correctly still passed) failed against the pre-fix code for the expected reasons (wrong or missing class, double-decoration), then restored the fix and confirmed all 16 pass, before this was considered done. Full verification suite green: `tsc --noEmit`, 1444 Vitest tests (up from 1428 immediately before this claim, +16), `eslint` (0 errors, same 5 pre-existing unrelated warnings in `App.tsx`, untouched by this claim), `npm run check-version`, `vite build`. No Rust or Android source touched; `cargo check`/`cargo test` (57/57) were re-run as part of this session's own integration-pass health check, not because this item needed them, and both were green. No manual/on-device UI smoke test was performed in this sandbox.

- ✅ **F20 Phase 1: workspace profile catalog, add/switch/forget, and a minimal switcher**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260903T0630Z, branch: agent/f20-phase1-profile-catalog; reassigned by explicit maintainer request from the prior claim, Claude-Code-cloud-20260902T2324Z, whose branch carried no implementation commits beyond its own claim, so nothing is lost by the reassignment, spec: `spec/leotheca-workspace-profiles-sdd.md`; narrowed to a first slice, see the Phase 2 follow-up below): A local catalog of previously opened workspaces (stable app-local ID, editable display name, fixed icon, platform-specific locator, last-opened timestamp), reusing the existing authoritative `setWorkspacePath`/`workspaceTransitions` coordinator (N-001/N-003) as the actual activation mechanism underneath rather than a second one.

      **`globalConfig.ts`** (sections 18, 19): `GlobalConfigV2` replaces the old `GlobalConfig` — `workspaceProfiles: WorkspaceProfile[]`, `activeWorkspaceId`, plus the pre-existing `theme`/`lastWorkspacePath`/`workspaceToken` fields kept as the one-release compatibility mirror (section 19.3). `decodeGlobalConfig` is non-destructive per section 18.3: an unknown top-level field and an unknown field inside a recognized profile record both round-trip via a trailing spread; a malformed profile record (missing/wrong-typed `id`/`name`/`path`/`lastOpenedAt`/`token`) is dropped without discarding the rest of the catalog or any unrelated field; duplicate valid profile IDs are left in the array rather than explicitly deduplicated, since every lookup elsewhere resolves by `Array.prototype.find` (always the first match), which already satisfies "resolved deterministically for the runtime view" without a separate dedup pass. **Disclosed narrowing of section 18.3's fuller ask**: a dropped malformed record's own bytes are not preserved for a future repair UI (no such UI exists yet); this protects every *other* profile and field, the practical protection users actually need, but not that one record's own recovery. Legacy migration (section 19.2) triggers when no v2 catalog exists and `lastWorkspacePath` is non-null: one profile is created in memory (name from the Desktop basename, or "Workspace" for the Android synthetic root or an unusable basename, matching `workspaceProfiles.ts`'s own `defaultProfileName` fallback exactly), `activeWorkspaceId` set to it; persistence only happens later, after that workspace has actually opened (`store.ts`'s own success path), never from the decoder itself.

      **`workspaceProfiles.ts`** (new module, pure functions only, no signal reads): `normalizeProfileName`/`defaultProfileName` (sections 13.1, 11 step 6), `isKnownWorkspaceIcon`/`displayWorkspaceIcon` (section 7.4's bundled-icon-with-forward-compatible-fallback), `workspaceLocatorKey`/`findProfileByLocator` (section 12.2's dedup key: exact token when present, else exact path string — deliberately not a platform-aware case-folding normalization, since `pickWorkspaceFolder` already returns the OS's own canonical-cased string for a given folder in practice and a real case-insensitive-filesystem normalizer needs a platform signal this frontend doesn't currently expose, a disclosed simplification), `sortWorkspaceProfiles` (section 7.5: descending `lastOpenedAt`, tie-broken by case-insensitive name then stable ID), `matchesWorkspaceSearch` (section 10.2's match rule, though the switcher UI itself doesn't wire up a search field yet, see deferred list below).

      **`store.ts`**: new `workspaceProfiles`/`activeWorkspaceId` signals, always kept sorted. `setWorkspacePath` (the pre-existing transition executor, its own mechanics untouched) gains an optional third `profile` argument; on a successful transition's `afterPublish`, if given, that profile is marked opened (`lastOpenedAt` bumped, `activeWorkspaceId` set) and the *entire* current catalog is persisted through one new `persistGlobalConfig()` — every global-config write in this module now goes through it, so theme changes, recency changes, and catalog edits always serialize the latest signal values together (section 18.4), replacing the old pattern of each call site (`setWorkspacePath`, `setTheme`, the old `initSettings` failure path) building its own possibly-stale `{lastWorkspacePath, theme, workspaceToken}` object. A failed transition persists nothing (sections 16.4/17.2: B remains in the catalog, recency unchanged), a real behavioral fix over the pre-Phase-1 code, which used to null out `lastWorkspacePath` on any `setWorkspacePath` failure regardless of whether a catalog even existed. New actions: `activateWorkspaceProfile(id)` (a no-op when already active, per section 10.4: no filesystem work, no recency change), `addWorkspaceFromPicker()` (section 11's flow: picker cancellation leaves everything unchanged; a locator match activates the existing profile instead of duplicating; otherwise a candidate is built and only committed to the catalog on successful activation, via the same `profile`-argument mechanism), `forgetWorkspaceProfile(id)` (section 7.7/15.1: removes only the catalog entry, refuses the active profile defensively even though the UI never offers that button for it either). `initSettings` now resolves `activeWorkspaceId` against the loaded catalog (falling back through the same legacy-migration path via `decodeGlobalConfig`) rather than reading `lastWorkspacePath` directly, and on startup-activation failure keeps `activeWorkspaceId` pointing at the unreachable profile rather than erasing it (section 17.2), falling back to the ordinary `WelcomeDialog` in the absence of a dedicated recovery launcher (deferred, see below). `WelcomeDialog.tsx` and `SettingsPanel.tsx`'s own "Change Folder" both now call `addWorkspaceFromPicker()` instead of `setWorkspacePath` directly, per section 20's "must no longer be called directly by UI surfaces" — the only two UI call sites that existed.

      **`WorkspaceSwitcher.tsx`** (new component, wired into `App.tsx`'s header): a minimal switcher per section 10.1 — a trigger button (active profile's icon+name, or "Open workspace" when none is active, per section 9.1), a dropdown listing every profile (click-to-switch, a checkmark on the active one, a "Forget" button on every other one), and "+ Add workspace". Closes on Escape, on an outside click, and returns focus to the trigger on close, matching the outside-click-dismiss convention `workspace/FileContextMenu.tsx` already established elsewhere in this codebase — baseline dropdown behavior, not counted as section 10.3's own "polish."

      **Explicitly deferred to F20 Phase 2, named there rather than silently dropped**: rename/icon editing (13); relink/access-recovery for an unavailable profile and the full typed `WorkspaceTransitionState` error UI (14, 16.1, 16.4-16.6, "Switch without saving"); forgetting the *active* profile (17.2's zero-active-workspace recovery flow); the switcher's own search field and arrow-key row navigation (10.2-10.3); the Settings panel's "Workspace profiles" management section (9.3) and command-palette entries (9.2); and Android-specific safe-display-name polish beyond the generic "Workspace" fallback (22.2).

      57 new tests: 24 in new `workspaceProfiles.test.ts` (every pure helper), 10 in new `WorkspaceSwitcher.test.tsx` (trigger label, menu open/close via click/Escape/outside-click plus focus return, activate/forget/add wiring, empty state), 10 net in `globalConfig.test.ts` (v2 shape round-tripping, unknown-field/unknown-profile-field preservation, malformed-profile dropping without losing siblings, non-array-profiles rejection, duplicate-ID pass-through, and the full legacy-migration matrix: basename naming, Android-synthetic-root fallback, no-migration-when-no-legacy-path, no-migration-when-a-v2-catalog-already-exists, migration when `version: 2` but `workspaceProfiles` itself is missing), 13 net in `store.test.ts` (`activateWorkspaceProfile`'s already-active no-op and unknown-id no-op, `addWorkspaceFromPicker`'s cancel/create/dedup-activate/failure-leaves-nothing-changed cases, `forgetWorkspaceProfile`'s remove/refuse-active/unknown-id cases, and `initSettings`'s catalog-resolving activation, failure-preserves-activeWorkspaceId-without-persisting, and startup migration-persisted-only-after-success), plus mock updates (no behavior change) in `App.test.tsx`/`rename_autosave.test.tsx`/`WelcomeDialog.test.tsx`/`SettingsPanel.test.tsx` for the new store exports and the `addWorkspaceFromPicker` routing change. A real design mistake caught and fixed before this was considered done: an initial `persistGlobalConfig`/`setWorkspacePath` refactor left a bare `try { ... } catch (error) { throw error; }` wrapper with no actual handling left inside it once the failure-path persist call was removed for correctness — caught by `eslint`'s `no-useless-catch` rule, not by test failure, a reminder that lint output is itself a real verification signal, not just style noise. - ✅ **F04 Phase 4b follow-up 2: per-note embed load timeout**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260903T0621Z, branch: agent/f04-phase4b-load-timeout, spec: `spec/f04-heading-block-links-embeds.md` section 11.4): Closes spec 11.4's remaining "per-note load timeout" gap. Phase 4b already implemented cycle detection and the two numeric budgets, plus cancellation of a stale preview generation's in-flight resolution, but had no independent per-read timeout: a single slow or hung `readTextFile` call for one cross-note embed could leave that embed's own frame stuck on "Loading…" forever even within an otherwise still-current preview, since the existing `cancelled` flag only fires when a *newer* preview generation supersedes the current one, never for a single call simply taking too long. New `readEmbedNoteWithTimeout(path, timeoutMs)` in `MarkdownPreview.tsx` races the resolution effect's `readTextFile` call against a new `EMBED_LOAD_TIMEOUT_MS` (8000ms) implementation constant, the same footing as the depth/instance/byte-budget constants directly above it; a `settled` flag inside the race discards whichever outcome loses, so a real read that finally resolves well after its own timeout already produced a placeholder can never overwrite it. The underlying native read cannot actually be cancelled (no cancellation token flows through `tauriBridge.ts`'s `readTextFile` today), so a timed-out read keeps running in the background; this only stops the UI from waiting on it, disclosed in the function's own doc comment rather than implied as a true abort. The timeout path rejects with a plain `Error`, caught by `resolveOne`'s own existing try/catch alongside a genuine read failure, and reuses the same "Could not read embedded note" placeholder text a real failure already showed rather than inventing a new one, since spec 11.4's own required-placeholder list has exactly one text for "the read didn't produce content," not a distinct one for "it took too long." 2 new tests in `MarkdownPreview.test.tsx` (100 total, up from 98): a never-resolving `readTextFile` mock proves the timeout alone (not the read) produces the placeholder once `EMBED_LOAD_TIMEOUT_MS` elapses (via `vi.useFakeTimers()`/`vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync`), and a second test proves a real resolution arriving after that timeout does not overwrite the already-rendered placeholder. Followed this file's revert-confirm-restore discipline: both new tests were run against the pre-fix code first (a plain `readTextFile` call with no timeout) and confirmed to fail for the expected reason (`"Loading…"` instead of the placeholder), then re-run against the restored fix and confirmed to pass, before this was considered done.     (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260902T2211Z, branch: agent/f04-phase4b-edge-cases; added at the maintainer's own explicit mid-session request, flagged in passing while landing F04 Phase 4b's own Implemented entry rather than left unrecorded; narrowed to test coverage and any bugfix it surfaces, the per-note load timeout split off to its own follow-up below): Adds real coverage for four combinations Phase 4b's own tests didn't exercise. **(1) Exact byte-budget boundary**: a new test constructs an embed whose extracted text is exactly 1,048,576 bytes (`"x".repeat(1_048_576)`, all single-byte ASCII), landing `bytesRemaining` at precisely 0 rather than a large negative overshoot; confirms the `<= 0` check treats exact exhaustion the same as overshoot. **(2) A chain mixing same-note and cross-note embeds**: cross-note (host embeds NoteA) -> cross-note (NoteA embeds NoteB) -> same-note (NoteB's own heading). A first attempt at this test put the same-note hop in the *middle* of the chain with a further marker inside its own extracted section, which surfaced a real architectural fact worth documenting, not a bug: a same-note fragment's extracted content is a literal substring of the exact text already being scanned for markers, so anything textually *after* it inside that same document is independently found and expanded a second time at that document's own top level — correct, expected behavior for real note content (the reader would see it there too), not double-counting to fix. The landed test instead places the same-note hop last, as the chain's own leaf, which cleanly exercises three real levels of mixed-kind recursion without that inherent duplication. **(3) A nested ambiguous-heading placeholder**: a same-note embed nested inside a cross-note embed's own content, targeting a heading that itself has a duplicate in that same note, correctly shows "more than one heading" at the nested level rather than expanding or crashing. **(4) Cancellation of an in-flight nested resolution**: using a manually controlled (not immediately resolved) promise for a *nested* cross-note read discovered only while resolving its parent, re-rendering the component with a source that requests no embeds (running the effect's own cleanup, setting its closure's `cancelled` flag) before that nested read resolves, then resolving it and confirming the (now-detached) frame's body was never mutated with the real content — passed against the existing implementation unmodified, confirming `resolveOne`'s pre-existing `if (cancelled) return` guard already extends correctly to nested calls (the same closure-captured flag, not a per-level one), a genuine coverage gap rather than a bug. All four combinations passed against Phase 4b's existing implementation with no further code changes needed; the value here is coverage proving that, not a fix. 4 new tests in `MarkdownPreview.test.tsx` (98 total, up from 94). - ✅ **F04 Phase 4b: embed recursion depth, cycle detection, and instance/byte budgets**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260902T2211Z, branch: agent/f04-phase4b-embed-recursion, spec: `spec/f04-heading-block-links-embeds.md` section 11.4): Closes most of Phase 4a's own disclosed single-level-only gap. `MarkdownPreview.tsx`'s `RenderContext.allowEmbeds` boolean is replaced by an `EmbedRecursionState` (`embedDepth`, an `embedAncestryPaths` chain, a shared `EmbedBudget`, and the same `crossNoteEmbedsOut` queue Phase 4a already had), threaded through every same-note extraction (`embedExtractionHtml`) and every asynchronous cross-note resolution (`resolveOne`'s effect), nested ones included, rather than a fresh, isolated context per level. **Depth**: a `![[...]]` is only expanded while `embedDepth < MAX_EMBED_DEPTH` (3); at the cap it degrades to a plain link exactly as Phase 4a's single-level cap already did, just three levels later. **Cycle detection**: a new `embedIdentity(notePath, fragment)` keys the ancestry chain on note+fragment, not the note path alone — a real design correction made mid-implementation after a first, path-only version falsely flagged a same-note chain of *different* headings (`![[#H1]]` containing `![[#H2]]`) as a cycle, caught by actually running the resulting test and reading the wrong frame count rather than assumed correct; a target whose exact note+fragment identity already appears in the chain shows "Embed cycle stopped" instead of recursing, covered by both a same-note self-referential-section test and a cross-note A-embeds-B-embeds-A test. **Budgets**: one shared, mutable `EmbedBudget` object (25 resolved instances, 1 MiB total bytes via `TextEncoder`, both spec 11.4 numbers) is checked and, only on an actual "ok" resolution, decremented — never for a not-found/ambiguous/cycle placeholder, matching "resolved instances" wording; exhausting either shows "Embed limit reached." A single embed already larger than the remaining byte budget is deliberately allowed to render whole rather than truncated mid-render, a disclosed simplification: only the *next* attempted embed sees the budget exhausted. **Nested cross-note resolution**: a cross-note embed's own resolved content can itself contain further cross-note embeds; those append to the exact same growing `crossNoteEmbeds` array the top-level render started (via each request's own captured `nextRecursion.crossNoteEmbedsOut` reference), so the pre-existing bounded worker-pool effect keeps draining it, newly discovered entries included, without a second effect or queue — verified end-to-end with a real two-level cross-note chain, not just reasoned about. **Explicitly deferred, named rather than silently dropped**: spec 11.4's per-note load timeout (only cancellation-on-newer-preview-generation, pre-existing infrastructure, is confirmed to extend correctly to nested embeds via existing tests; no independent per-read timeout is added). **Edge cases identified but not covered by this claim's own tests, flagged per the maintainer's explicit request to record them rather than let them go unnoticed**: the exact byte-budget boundary (bytesRemaining landing at precisely 0 versus a small negative) is exercised only by a large-overshoot test, not an exact-boundary one; a chain mixing same-note and cross-note embeds beyond one level of each (this claim tests a 2-level same-note chain, a 2-level cross-note chain, sibling concurrent cross-note embeds, and a same-note-inside-cross-note nesting, but not e.g. cross-note-inside-same-note-inside-cross-note); ambiguous-heading/ambiguous-block placeholders specifically at a nested (not top-level) depth; and interaction between preview-generation cancellation and an in-flight *nested* (not top-level) cross-note resolution. None of these are known-wrong, unlike Phase 3b/3e's own disclosed gaps elsewhere in this file; they are simply untested combinations a future session should add coverage for before relying on them. 12 new tests in `MarkdownPreview.test.tsx` (94 total, up from 87 net after the rewritten nested-embed test: depth-3 cross-note chain capping to a link at the 4th level, same-note and cross-note cycle detection, 25-instance and 1-MiB budget exhaustion, concurrent sibling cross-note embeds, and same-note-nested-inside-cross-note resolution against the embedded note's own headings). A real test-writing mistake caught and fixed before this was considered done, a new failure mode for this session: `Element.querySelector` does not clip CSS descendant-combinator ancestor-matching to the calling element's own subtree, so a compound selector like `.embed-frame-body a[...]` scoped to a *nested* frame element also matched an outer ancestor frame's own "Open source note" link; fixed by querying the frame's own direct-child body first, then the anchor within it, rather than one chained selector. Full verification suite green: `tsc --noEmit`, 1422 Vitest tests (up from 1415 immediately before this claim, +7 net), `eslint` (0 errors, same 5 pre-existing unrelated warnings in `App.tsx`, untouched by this claim), `npm run check-version`, `vite build`. No Rust or Android source touched, so no Rust/Android checks were re-run beyond confirming this item touches neither. No manual/on-device UI smoke test was performed in this sandbox.

- ✅ **F04 Phase 3e: multi-line list-item/blockquote block-reference continuation**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260902T2211Z, branch: agent/f04-phase3e-block-continuation, spec: `spec/f04-heading-block-links-embeds.md` section 7.1/7.2): Fixes the disclosed-and-tested wrong behavior the Phase 3b entry left in place: `markdown/blocks.ts`'s `scanBlockIds` used to misread an indented continuation line of a multi-line list item, or a lazily-continued line of a multi-line blockquote, as its own standalone top-level paragraph rather than attributing a trailing `^block-id` marker to the item/quote it actually continues. Reworked the scanner's single-line `recordSingleLineBlock` helper into a generalized `openBlock` accumulator that mirrors the pre-existing `paragraphLines`/`flushParagraph` pattern one level up: on a line matching the list-item or blockquote start regex, an `OpenBlock` (kind, the first line's own content-start offset, and for list items its marker+padding indent width) opens instead of immediately recording; each following line is classified by a new `isContinuationLine` as either belonging to the open block or not, and only the accumulated block's *last* line is checked for a trailing marker when it closes (`flushOpenBlock`), the same "final line of the block" rule paragraphs already used. **List-item continuation** requires the following line be non-blank and indented at least as far as the item's own marker+padding width (CommonMark's own indent-based continuation rule), and not itself a new list item, blockquote, fence, ATX heading, or HTML comment start. **Blockquote continuation** is more permissive, matching real CommonMark "lazy continuation": any following non-blank line that isn't itself a new block start continues the quote, whether or not it repeats the `>` prefix. Either continuation ends at a blank line (and is not resumed after it: a loose list item's own blank-line-separated paragraphs are a disclosed remaining gap, not attempted), a line starting a fenced code block, an ATX heading, or (list items only) an under-indented non-blank line; each of those correctly re-triggers the closed block's flush and then falls through so the same line is still classified normally afterward (it may itself open a new item/quote, a fence, or a paragraph) rather than being silently dropped. Single-line items/quotes behave identically to before (an `OpenBlock` with exactly one accumulated line), confirmed by the full pre-existing Phase 3b test suite passing unmodified. **Disclosed narrowing, not silently dropped, per the module's own updated doc comment**: a loose list item's blank-line-separated paragraphs, a nested sub-list or nested blockquote inside a continuing item (a continuation line that itself looks like a new list item/blockquote ends the outer one rather than nesting into it), and tab-indented continuation (only literal space characters count toward a list item's indent width) are still not attributed; real CommonMark parses all three, deliberately left alone to keep this module a scanner rather than a second, competing block parser. 8 new tests in `blocks.test.ts` (the old disclosed-wrong-behavior regression test rewritten to assert the now-correct `list-item` attribution instead of `paragraph`, plus new coverage for a 3-line continuation, blank-line termination for both kinds, under-indented termination, termination on a new list item, blockquote `>`-reprefixed and lazy continuation, and termination on a fence opening mid-continuation). Full verification suite green: `tsc --noEmit`, 1392 Vitest tests (up from 1384 immediately before this claim, +8), `eslint` (0 errors, same 5 pre-existing unrelated warnings in `App.tsx`, untouched by this claim), `npm run check-version`, `vite build`. No Rust or Android source touched, so no Rust/Android checks were re-run beyond confirming this item touches neither. No manual/on-device UI smoke test was performed in this sandbox.

</details>

- ✅ **Audit follow-up F-004: Enforce workspace containment and mutation semantics at the native boundary**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260831T1900Z, 2026-08-31T19:00Z, branch: agent/f-004-native-boundary-containment): Desktop workspace writes and mutations no longer trust frontend-computed absolute paths. The shared resolver rejects absolute or traversal-containing workspace-relative paths, canonicalizes the target or nearest existing ancestor, and enforces component-wise containment under the canonical workspace root, including symlink targets. Every open-workspace write path now uses that containment-aware bridge, including `saveCoordinator.ts` note autosave, which carries the workspace root with each queued save instead of importing current settings and creating a circular dependency. Desktop writes use crash-safe sibling-temp-file replacement. Create and rename operations that must not replace an existing path now use native no-replace commands in `src-tauri/src/workspace_mutations.rs`, removing the frontend check-then-act race. Android keeps SAF as its containment authority and now exposes the same stricter mutation contract through `WorkspaceMutationPlugin`: one-segment names are validated natively, creates reject an existing sibling and provider name uniquification, and rename rejects a sibling collision before `DocumentsContract.renameDocument`; `capacitorMutationContract.test.ts` verifies the frontend/native contract and failure mapping. Final self-review was performed after reconciling the branch directly onto current `main`. Exact-head CI run `33713633314` on `631f63d0e38b81666ac766030c55795dd17da122` passed frontend typecheck/lint/version checks/tests/build, Rust fmt/Clippy/tests/check, Android JVM tests/debug APK build/emulator installation, AppImage build/extract/launch, and the aggregate validation gate. The final documentation/ROADMAP-only bookkeeping commit is revalidated by the same required CI gate before landing. Emulator installation is not physical Android-device verification, and no physical-device confirmation is claimed.

- ✅ **Audit follow-up N-002: Prevent stale file-open completions**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (reclaimed: Claude-Code-cloud-20260902T2324Z, branch: agent/n-002-stale-file-open-completions; supersedes an abandoned claim, Claude-Code-cloud-20260901T1249Z, whose branch never existed on the remote, over 24 hours after that claim's own timestamp with no related `main` activity in between, per `skills/roadmap-workflow.md`'s "Reclaiming an abandoned claim" rule): `App.tsx`'s `handleOpenFile` awaited `readTextFile` and then unconditionally opened or focused the tab, so an older open request could complete after a newer one and override it (first-response-wins instead of the intended latest-selection-wins), or reopen a tab from a workspace the user had since switched away from, including under Android's synthetic `/workspace` path shared by every SAF grant. New `workspace/fileOpenAuthority.ts` mirrors `fileTreeStore.ts`'s internal `SearchAuthority` pattern: `beginFileOpenAuthority()` (called synchronously, before any await, so it invalidates every open request already in flight, including a synchronous image-tab open that never itself awaits anything) captures a monotonically increasing per-open-request generation plus `workspaceTransition.ts`'s own transition generation; `isCurrentFileOpen()` re-checks both. The transition generation, not `settings/store.ts`'s `workspaceSession`, is what closes the real race window: `workspaceSession` isn't incremented until a transition actually publishes, well after `prepareOutgoing` has already cleared open tabs, so a stale read completing in that window would have passed a `workspaceSession`-only check; the transition coordinator's own `generation` is bumped synchronously at the very start of `run()`, before `prepareOutgoing` even begins. `handleOpenFile` now calls `isCurrentFileOpen` immediately after its `readTextFile` await, before reading `openTabs` or touching tabs/focus/sidebar state, and returns silently (no mutation at all, not even a background tab for the stale request) when stale. A rejected read is only rethrown when the request was still current at the point of failure; a stale request's own read failure is swallowed rather than surfaced, since a newer request already superseded it and an unhandled rejection from a fire-and-forget `onOpenFile` call would otherwise be a real, user-visible-in-devtools loose end. 9 new tests: 6 in `fileOpenAuthority.test.ts` (a fresh authority is current, a later `begin` invalidates an earlier one including the cross-kind image-after-note case, a transition starting after an authority began invalidates it and does so from the very first transition step, an authority begun after a transition completes is current, and one begun before a transition and never superseded by another open request is still invalidated by the transition alone) and 3 end-to-end in `App.test.tsx` (an older request's completion after a newer one does not override it and adds no background tab; a workspace transition starting mid-read prevents that read's completion from opening a tab; a rejected already-stale read neither throws nor disturbs the current tab). Followed this file's own revert-confirm-restore discipline for the fix: reverted `App.tsx`'s change locally and confirmed all three new `App.test.tsx` tests fail against the original code (including a real, caught `Unhandled Rejection: Error: boom` for the stale-rejection case), then restored the fix and confirmed all three pass, rather than trusting the tests were meaningful without seeing them fail first. `documentation/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s `workspace/` row updated; `CHANGELOG.md` gained a plain-language entry. Full verification suite green: `tsc --noEmit`, 1385 Vitest tests (up from 1376 immediately before this claim, +9), `eslint` (0 errors, same 5 pre-existing unrelated warnings in `App.tsx`), `npm run check-version`, `vite build`. No Rust or Android source touched, so no Rust/Android checks were re-run beyond confirming this item touches neither (this session's own broader integration-pass health check already confirmed `cargo check`/`test`/`fmt`/`clippy` green on `main` before this claim started). No manual/on-device UI smoke test was performed in this sandbox. F20 (workspace profiles), which named this item as its own section 3.1 prerequisite, is unblocked by this landing.

</details>
- ✅ **F04 Phase 3d: fenced code block references**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260902T2211Z, branch: agent/f04-phase3d-fenced-code-blocks, spec: `spec/f04-heading-block-links-embeds.md` section 7.2): Extends `markdown/blocks.ts`'s `scanBlockIds` (Phase 3a: paragraphs; Phase 3b: single-line list items/blockquotes) to also detect a block ID on a fenced code block, the fourth of section 7.2's five eligible kinds. Fenced code blocks have a genuinely different marker shape from the other three, called out explicitly by the spec itself: "the ID appears on a separate immediately following line, not inside the code fence," rather than trailing the block's own last line of content. `BlockRecord.kind` gains `"fenced-code"`; a new `STANDALONE_BLOCK_ID_RE` (a marker with nothing else on its line, only up to 3 leading spaces before the caret) is checked against exactly one line, the one immediately after a closing fence, tracked via a `pendingFencedCode` state set the instant a fence closes and consumed (accepted or forfeited) by that one following line alone: a blank line, another fence opening immediately, or any other content in that line forfeits the attachment outright rather than searching further lines for one, matching the spec's own "immediately following" wording precisely rather than loosely. A forfeited line is not silently dropped: it still falls through to ordinary paragraph/list-item/blockquote/heading classification, confirmed by a dedicated test that a marker-bearing line with other content on it is still correctly read as its own ordinary paragraph block, not lost. `recordBlock` (the shared per-kind-agnostic block builder Phase 3b introduced) was refactored to take an explicit `contentTo` parameter rather than deriving it from the matched line's own offset, since a fenced-code block's content range (the whole fence, open through close) and its marker's line are two different lines entirely, unlike the other three kinds where they're the same line; this is a pure internal refactor with no behavior change for paragraphs/list-items/blockquotes, confirmed by the full existing Phase 3a/3b test suite passing unmodified. Since resolution and rendering key only on a block's id, not its kind, fenced-code blocks resolve and embed through the identical existing pipeline (link resolution, Preview/Source-mode status styling and click navigation, autocomplete, block embeds) with zero changes needed outside `blocks.ts` itself, confirmed by a dedicated end-to-end `MarkdownPreview.test.tsx` test rather than assumed. A real test-writing mistake caught before this was considered done, the same class already caught twice this session for the analogous list-item/blockquote and cross-note-embed cases: an initial assertion checked that the rendered link's own label never showed the raw marker text, which was never true (a block link's default label has always shown the raw `#^id` fallback); fixed to check that the *block's own* rendered content excludes its marker instead. 7 new tests in `blocks.test.ts` (marker attachment on the immediately-following line with exact content/source ranges, forfeiture on a blank-line gap, forfeiture-with-fallback-to-paragraph-classification on a non-standalone following line, forfeiture with no retry on a later line, the up-to-3-leading-spaces allowance, a fence-immediately-after-another-fence's-close not stealing the wrong marker, and correct paragraph-scanning resumption after a consumed fenced-code marker line), plus 1 in `MarkdownPreview.test.tsx` (same-note resolution and rendering of a fenced-code block link, marker invisibility in the rendered `<pre>`). Full verification suite green: `tsc --noEmit`, 1384 Vitest tests (up from 1376 immediately before this claim, +8; the shared `recordBlock` signature refactor left every prior paragraph/list-item/blockquote test's own count and assertions unchanged, confirmed by the full existing suite passing without modification), `eslint` (0 errors, same 5 pre-existing unrelated warnings in `App.tsx`, untouched by this claim), `npm run check-version`, `vite build`. No Rust or Android source touched, so no Rust/Android checks were re-run beyond confirming this item touches neither. No manual/on-device UI smoke test was performed in this sandbox.

- ✅ **F04 Phase 3b: single-line list-item and blockquote block references**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260902T2211Z, branch: agent/f04-phase3b-block-kinds, spec: `spec/f04-heading-block-links-embeds.md` section 7.2): Extends `markdown/blocks.ts`'s `scanBlockIds` (Phase 3a: paragraphs only) to also detect a block ID on a **single-line** list item or blockquote (spec 7.1's own examples: `"- The user owns the Markdown files. ^local-first"`, `"> A quoted principle. ^principle"`), the next two of section 7.2's five eligible block kinds. `BlockRecord.kind` is now `"paragraph" | "list-item" | "blockquote"`; a shared `recordBlock` helper (factored out of the paragraph-only `flushParagraph` path) computes `id`/`key`/`occurrence`/source-and-content ranges identically for all three kinds, so list-item and blockquote blocks participate in `wikiResolver.ts`'s `resolveBlockFragment`, Preview link resolution/status styling/click navigation, and F04 Phase 4a's block embeds through the exact same existing code paths with zero changes needed outside this one scanner module, confirmed by dedicated end-to-end `MarkdownPreview.test.tsx` tests rather than assumed from the scanner's own unit tests alone. A real edge case caught and fixed before this was considered done: the marker's own required leading whitespace (spec 7.1: "whitespace-delimited") must belong to the block's actual text, not merely be the bullet's or `>`'s own mandatory separator space; a bare `"- ^orphan-id"` or `"> ^orphan-id"` (no real content before the marker) is rejected, not recorded with an inverted, empty content range, caught by a dedicated regression test that failed against the naive implementation before this guard was added. **Disclosed scope narrowing, not silently dropped**: only a **single-line** list item or blockquote is eligible; real CommonMark multi-line continuation (a lazy continuation line, a nested sub-list, a loose list's own blank-line-separated paragraphs within one item) is well beyond what this module's simple line classification can support without becoming a second, competing block-level parser. This has a genuinely wrong (not just incomplete) consequence, disclosed rather than hidden: an indented continuation line ending in a marker is misread as its own standalone top-level paragraph, since this scanner has no notion of "this line belongs to the list item above it"; a dedicated `blocks.test.ts` test asserts this exact known-wrong behavior on purpose, so a future fix has a concrete failing case to turn green rather than only a prose warning. Headings and fenced code blocks remain the other two out-of-scope kinds, tracked in the "F04 Phase 3c/4b/5" follow-up entry below alongside embed recursion and the Preview pre-check. Net +8 tests (1356 total, up from 1348): `blocks.test.ts` went from 24 to 29 (Phase 3a's two old "not eligible" negative assertions for list items/blockquotes rewritten into positive ones now that they resolve, plus new coverage for single-line list-item/blockquote detection with exact content ranges, `*`/`+`/ordered markers alongside `-`, extra-whitespace trimming after the marker, both orphan-marker rejections, and the disclosed multi-line-continuation misreading as its own documented regression case), and `MarkdownPreview.test.tsx` went from 83 to 86 (list-item and blockquote blocks resolving through the *existing* link and embed rendering with no changes needed there; an initial draft of these two assertions incorrectly checked that the rendered *link label* never showed the raw block id text, which was never true or intended since a block link's default label has always shown the raw `#^id` fallback per Phase 3a's own `defaultWikilinkLabel`, caught and fixed to check the thing that actually matters, that the *block's own* marker is stripped from its own rendered content, before this was considered done). `eslint` (0 errors, same 5 pre-existing unrelated warnings in `App.tsx`, untouched by this claim), `npm run check-version`, `vite build`. No Rust or Android source touched, so no Rust/Android checks were re-run beyond confirming this item touches neither. No manual/on-device UI smoke test was performed in this sandbox.

<details>
