    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260902T2211Z, branch: agent/f04-phase4a-embeds, spec: `spec/f04-heading-block-links-embeds.md` section 11 and section 21 Phase 4; narrowed to a single-recursion-level first slice, see below): Renders `![[Note]]`, `![[Note#Heading]]`, and `![[Note#^block-id]]` as a read-only embedded "application frame" in Preview (spec 11.2: source note name, an `Open source note` action, resolved content or a placeholder, an accessible `Embedded content from ...` label), reusing every relevant piece of existing F04 infrastructure rather than a second implementation of any of it. `linking/wikiSyntax.ts`'s `parseWikiLinks` now recognizes a leading `!` immediately before `[[` as `kind: "embed"` (previously always `"link"`, per that field's own forward-compatibility comment since Phase 1); note/fragment parsing and resolution are otherwise byte-for-byte identical between a link and an embed record, only how `editor/MarkdownPreview.tsx` renders the resolved target differs. **Extraction** (`extractEmbedSection` in `MarkdownPreview.tsx`): a plain `![[Note]]` embeds the note body after frontmatter (spec 11.1, via a new `frontmatterBodyStart` export on `editor/frontmatterEdits.ts`, the same delimiter detection `hasFrontmatterBlock` already uses); `![[Note#Heading]]` embeds that heading through its own already-computed `HeadingRecord.sectionTo` (the exact "next heading of equal-or-higher level" boundary the outline feature already relies on); `![[Note#^block-id]]` embeds the exact block content via F04 Phase 3a's `resolveBlockFragment`. **Rendering paths**: a same-note embed extracts and renders synchronously, straight from the note's own already-available source (no new file read); a cross-note embed emits a `data-lt-embed-id` placeholder frame ("Loading…") during the synchronous render pass and is resolved by a new dedicated `useEffect` (a bounded worker pool, the same concurrency convention as the pre-existing image-attachment effect) that reads the target note, extracts its section, and replaces the placeholder body in place, sanitizing the freshly-injected HTML through `DOMPurify` a second time since it never passed through the top-level render pass's own single sanitize call. Both paths converge on one shared `renderEmbeddedMarkdownToHtml`: it strips the extracted section's own block-id markers, resolves its own local image attachments against the **embedded note's own directory** (spec 11.3, reusing `markLocalImageAttachments` with a different `noteDir`; a cross-note embed's own newly-injected images are queued through the same bounded resolver immediately after insertion, since they postdate the top-level image effect's own run), and reprocesses its own wikilinks with the embedded note as the same-note context (spec 11.3: "same-note wikilinks inside the embed refer to the embedded note, not the host note") by calling back into `renderWikilinksStructured` itself with a new `allowEmbeds: false` context flag. That flag is this phase's actual recursion boundary: a `![[...]]` marker encountered while rendering an already-embedded section is not expanded into a further nested frame, degrading instead to an ordinary, still-functional link to the same target (verified by a dedicated cross-note test asserting exactly one frame renders, not two). Required placeholders implemented: "Embedded note not found" (no async read attempted for this case), "Embedded heading not found", "Embedded block not found", ambiguous-heading/ambiguous-block text, and "Could not read embedded note" (a failed cross-note read). "Open source note" is a plain anchor built through the existing `buildWikilinkHref`, so it reuses the container's already-existing wikilink click handler verbatim (same-note reveals via `outline/outlineNavigation.ts`, cross-note opens via `onOpenFile`) rather than a second navigation code path. **Disclosed scope narrowings, not oversights**: this phase implements exactly one level of embedding, not spec 11.4's full recursion (max depth 3, max 25 resolved instances, 1 MiB load budget, cycle detection, "Embed cycle stopped"/"Embed limit reached" placeholders), and has no total per-preview cap on cross-note embeds resolved (only per-embed read concurrency is bounded); spec 7.3/11.3's DOM-ID-collision namespacing was checked, not assumed, to be currently moot (this codebase's `marked.setOptions` configuration does not generate heading `id` attributes at all, confirmed by direct inspection, not left as a guess); Source-mode decoration and `[[Note#^`/`![[` autocomplete for embeds are not implemented, the same disclosed gap Phase 2 already recorded for plain block references. - ✅ **F04 Phase 3a: paragraph block-reference resolution and navigation**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260902T2211Z, branch: agent/f04-phase3a-block-references, spec: `spec/f04-heading-block-links-embeds.md` section 7 and section 21 Phase 3; narrowed to a first slice, see below): The first slice of F04's remaining "block references" scope: Phase 1's parser already recognized a `#^block-id` fragment structurally but never resolved or navigated it (it degraded to opening the note only), and Phase 2 explicitly left it to the pre-existing plain-wikilink pass. This claim adds real resolution and click navigation for an existing block ID. New `src/markdown/blocks.ts` is the third module of the shared structure-scanner infrastructure (`headings.ts`, `tasks.ts` before it): `scanBlockIds(content)` detects an explicit block ID (spec 7.1's grammar, `[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9-]{0,63}`, a whitespace-delimited token at the end of a block's final line) attached to a **paragraph block only** in this phase, a disclosed narrowing (see below), skipping fenced code blocks and block-level HTML comments the same way `headings.ts`/`tasks.ts` already do, and correctly excluding heading/blockquote/list-item lines from paragraph accumulation so none of those is ever misread as paragraph content. Duplicate ids are tracked case-insensitively per spec 7.1's own duplicate-detection rule (an `occurrence` count mirroring `HeadingRecord`'s). `linking/wikiResolver.ts` gained `resolveBlockFragment` (the same resolved/missing-fragment/ambiguous-fragment shape as the existing `resolveHeadingFragment`) and a `targetBlocks` field on `WikiResolutionContext`, wired into `resolveWikiLinkTarget`'s existing `fragment.kind === "block"` branch exactly the way the equivalent heading branch already works: a same-note fragment resolves fully and synchronously against the current note's own scanned blocks; a cross-note fragment resolves at the note level only until a caller supplies the target note's own scanned blocks (mirroring headings' click-time-only cross-note verification). `editor/MarkdownPreview.tsx` renders a `[[Note#^block-id]]`/`[[#^block-id]]` link with the same resolved/missing/ambiguous styling (`linking/linking.css`'s `blockStatus=missing`/`blockStatus=ambiguous` selectors, the same dotted-underline convention the equivalent heading states already use) and the same click-navigation split as headings: same-note reveals directly via the existing `outline/outlineNavigation.ts` reveal signal, cross-note passes a `blockId` through `onOpenFile` (parallel to the existing `headingKey`) for `App.tsx`'s `handleOpenFile` to resolve against the freshly-read target note's content and batch into the same tab-switch-plus-reveal update headings already use. Per spec 7.3 ("a valid block ID token is not shown as ordinary rendered text"), a new `stripBlockIdMarkers` removes each detected block's own marker (and its required leading whitespace) from the source text before it reaches `marked.parse`, using the block's own pre-scanned offsets against the pristine source, before any other offset-shifting rewrite pass in that file (image-attachment/wikilink rewriting), documented as a load-bearing ordering constraint; gated behind the same `headingLinksEnabled` flag as the rest of F04's structured rendering, so turning that setting off is still a genuine escape hatch back to fully-visible `^block-id` text, exactly as before this feature existed. **Disclosed scope narrowings, not oversights**: (1) only a **paragraph** block can carry a resolvable ID in this phase; spec 7.2 also lists headings, list items, blockquotes, and fenced code blocks as eligible, none of which `scanBlockIds` detects yet, so a link to one of those reports "missing-fragment," indistinguishable from a genuinely nonexistent id (teaching `headings.ts` about a trailing marker specifically would mean stripping it from `rawText`/`displayText`, a change to a foundational module several already-shipped features depend on, well beyond this slice). (2) Spec 7.3's deterministic DOM ID/`data-lt-block-id` attribute and its copy-link affordance are not implemented: navigation reuses the existing reveal-signal mechanism exactly as headings already do (which does not itself scroll Preview's own rendered DOM, a pre-existing, shared limitation, not something this claim introduces), so nothing in this phase actually needs a stable DOM anchor yet. (3) Spec 7.4's "Copy block link"/"Create block link" actions and generated-ID flow, and Source-mode decoration/`[[Note#^` autocomplete for block references, are not implemented; a `^block-id` link still renders via the pre-existing plain-wikilink pass in Source mode, the same disclosed gap Phase 2's own entry already recorded for blocks. All three, plus the remaining block-kind eligibility, are tracked in the "F04 Phase 3b/4/5" follow-up entry below, not silently dropped. `documentation/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s `editor/`/`linking/` rows updated; a pre-existing, unrelated unresolved merge-conflict-marker corruption in that same file (`<<<<<<< ours`/`=======`/`>>>>>>> theirs` literally checked into the `editor/`/`linking/` table rows, predating this claim) was found in passing while updating those exact rows and reconciled (both sides' distinct facts merged, nothing dropped), a small enough fix in the same file to make in passing rather than a separate claim. 38 new tests: 24 in `blocks.test.ts` (single- and multi-line paragraphs, id-grammar edge cases including the 64-character cap and rejected characters, exclusion from headings/list items/blockquotes/fenced code/HTML comments, setext-heading interaction, duplicate-id occurrence counting, and CRLF), 9 in `wikiResolver.test.ts` (`resolveBlockFragment`'s own resolved/case-insensitive/missing/ambiguous matrix, plus `resolveWikiLinkTarget`'s same-note, cross-note-with-blocks-supplied, missing, and ambiguous block-fragment paths, replacing the old single "degrades to note-level" test with one that also covers the newly-real resolution path), 9 in `MarkdownPreview.test.tsx` (marker invisibility, same-note resolved/missing/ambiguous rendering and click-reveal, cross-note rendering and click-driven `onOpenFile` block-id passing, and the `headingLinksEnabled=false` escape hatch), and 2 in `App.test.tsx` (a real end-to-end cross-note click through `App`'s actual `handleOpenFile`, both the block-found and block-not-found cases). - ✅ **F04 Phase 2: Source-mode heading-link decorations and autocomplete**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260902T1155Z, branch: agent/f04-phase2-decorations-autocomplete, spec: `spec/f04-heading-block-links-embeds.md` section 21 Phase 2): Two additions on top of F04 Phase 1's `wikiSyntax.ts`/`wikiResolver.ts`, reused directly rather than a second matching implementation, per this claim's own instruction. **Source-mode decoration**: `editor/livePreview.ts` gained a dedicated heading-link decoration pass, run separately from the pre-existing plain-wikilink regex (`WIKILINK_PATTERN`) rather than teaching that regex about fragments. It scans each visible range's text with F04 Phase 1's `parseWikiLinks`, and for every valid record whose fragment is a heading (`kind: "heading"`, excluding plain `[[Note]]`/`[[Note|Label]]` links and `^block-id` fragments, both left to the existing plain-wikilink pass exactly as before this claim), classifies it into one of four states: a same-note fragment (`[[#Heading]]`) resolves fully and synchronously against a `scanHeadings` pass over the current document (lazily computed at most once per decoration rebuild, only if a same-note heading-link is actually present, since a rebuild runs on every keystroke and selection change) via `resolveHeadingFragment`, giving `resolved`/`missing`/`ambiguous`; a cross-note fragment resolves through `resolveWikiLinkTarget` at the note level only (`resolved` if the note exists, including spec 5.3's legacy-filename fallback, otherwise `broken`), the same disclosed scope narrowing `MarkdownPreview.tsx`'s own cross-note rendering already uses, since verifying a cross-note heading would mean reading another note's file from inside this synchronous, every-keystroke decoration pass. `resolved`/`broken` reuse the existing `cm-live-wikilink-resolved`/`cm-live-wikilink-broken` classes as-is; two new classes, `cm-live-wikilink-heading-missing` and `cm-live-wikilink-heading-ambiguous` (`App.css`, dotted underline over `--text-secondary`), cover the two states a plain wikilink can never be in, matching `linking/linking.css`'s existing dotted-underline convention for the equivalent Preview-mode states rather than inventing a new visual language. A heading-link's exact source span is recorded and skipped by the plain-wikilink pass so the same occurrence is never decorated twice or misclassified as an unresolvable literal note name (what `[[Note#Heading]]` looked like in Source mode before this claim). **Autocomplete**: `editor/MarkdownEditor.tsx` gained `headingLinkCompletions(path)`, a second `autocompletion({ override: [...] })` entry alongside the existing `wikilinkCompletions` (whose own trigger regex now stops at a `#`, since a note name can never contain one, so it no longer competes for the same keystroke). It matches `[[<NoteName>#<query>` or same-note `[[#<query>` (a `(?!\^)` right after the `#` excludes `[[Note#^`, since block-reference completion is out of scope here, see the follow-up entry below), and suggests headings via the shared scanner (`markdown/headings.ts`'s `scanHeadings`), never a second matching implementation: an empty note portion or one that resolves to the editor's own currently open `path` reads from the live, possibly-unsaved CodeMirror document directly (matching spec 9.2's "for the current unsaved note, headings come from the canonical in-memory scanner result"); a different, already-existing note is read fresh from disk on every trigger via `readTextFile` (no headings metadata index exists yet to serve section 9.2's "workspace metadata index" wording, a disclosed scope narrowing, not a cache) and yields no suggestions if that note doesn't resolve or fails to read. A selected heading inserts its own `displayText` (spec 6.2's "visible heading text") plus the closing `]]`; a new `escapeWikiLinkText` in `linking/wikiSyntax.ts` (the exact inverse of the existing `unescapeWikiLinkText`) escapes `\`/`#`/`|`/`[`/`]` first, so a heading literally titled e.g. "Issue #12" or "Before | After" still round-trips correctly through `parseWikiLinks` once inserted instead of being reinterpreted as a second delimiter. Each option's `detail` surfaces spec 9.2's level, line number, and duplicate-heading warning (`H2 · line 12 · duplicate`); full breadcrumb-ancestry display is not implemented, a disclosed narrowing to keep this claim's scope and test surface focused on the functional completion flow. **Explicitly out of scope, per this claim's own instructions and unchanged by it**: block references (`^block-id` completion and resolution), embeds, and Preview's cross-note existence pre-check, all tracked in the "F04 Phase 3a" entry and its own "F04 Phase 3b/4/5" follow-up below. 72 new tests: 22 in `livePreview.test.ts` (all four decoration states for both same-note and cross-note targets, marker-hiding/active-line behavior matching the existing plain-wikilink convention, exactly-once decoration with no double-classification, inline-code-span exclusion, block-reference fragments left to the pre-existing pass, and the existing visible-range-scoping convention), 6 in `wikiSyntax.test.ts` (`escapeWikiLinkText` round-tripping through `parseWikiLinks` for `#` and `|`, brackets and backslashes, and an exact-inverse property test against `unescapeWikiLinkText`), and 14 in `MarkdownEditor.test.tsx` (`headingLinkCompletions`: same-note and cross-note suggestion and filtering, live-document use for the currently open note versus a disk read for a different note, unresolved-note and unreadable-file null results, the block-reference-fragment bail-out, duplicate-heading detail marking, and completion-text escaping). `documentation/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s `editor/` and `linking/` rows updated. - ✅ **F03 Phase 1: read-only link-integrity diagnostics**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260902T1155Z, branch: agent/f03-phase1-diagnostics, spec: `spec/f03-link-integrity-refactor-center.md`, section 20 Phase 1 ("land diagnostic records and a read-only center... validate against fixture workspaces"), narrowed further per this claim's own roadmap note): Workspace-wide, read-only diagnostics classifying every parsed wikilink (plain `[[Note]]` and F04 Phase 1's `[[Note#Heading]]`/`[[#Heading]]` forms) as resolved, broken, missing-heading, or ambiguous-heading, reusing `linking/wikiSyntax.ts`'s `parseWikiLinks` and `linking/wikiResolver.ts`'s `resolveWikiLinkTarget`/`resolveHeadingFragment` rather than a second link-scanning or resolution implementation, with click-to-navigate to each finding's exact source location.       **How the data is computed, the design question this claim's own instructions asked to be disclosed rather than guessed at**: extends `linking/store.ts`'s single existing workspace read pass (the same one F02 Phase 1's `tasksByPath` already extended) instead of adding a second workspace walk or a second signal/store module. New `LinkIndex.wikiLinksByPath`/`LinkIndex.headingsByPath` (both optional on the type, so every pre-existing test fixture across the codebase that builds a `LinkIndex` literal without them still type-checks; every real `rebuildLinkIndex` call always populates both) hold each note's structured `parseWikiLinks`/`scanHeadings` output, computed in the exact same per-note read `rebuildLinkIndex` already performs for wikilinks/aliases/tags/tasks, with the same mtime/size cache-hit and unreadable-path-carries-forward behavior (cache version bumped 5 to 6, since `CachedNote`'s shape changed again, following the exact precedent F02 Phase 1's own `tasks` bump set; new `isValidWikiLinkRecord`/`isValidHeadingRecord` field-shape validators guard the two new cached fields the same way `isValidTaskRecord` already guards `tasks`, dropping a malformed persisted entry and forcing a real re-read rather than trusting or crashing on it). `diagnostics/diagnostics.ts`'s `computeWorkspaceLinkDiagnostics` is then a pure downstream function over `LinkIndex`, costing no extra file I/O or extra parsing beyond what indexing already does for its own purposes, and needing cross-note heading data (unlike `MarkdownPreview.tsx`, which deliberately only verifies a *same-note* fragment today to avoid a per-render file read for a cross-note one) is exactly why this claim reads every note's headings during indexing rather than lazily. `classifyWikiLink` calls the shared resolver twice per heading-fragment link: once with no target headings to learn which note (if any) the link resolves to, once more with that note's headings (from `headingsByPath`, already in memory) to verify the fragment, mirroring `MarkdownPreview.tsx`'s own two-step use of `resolveWikiLinkTarget`. **Documented, load-bearing caveat**: `resolveWikiLinkTarget`'s note resolution goes through `linking/store.ts`'s `resolveWikilink`, which always reads the live `linkIndex` signal directly, not any argument passed to it; `classifyWikiLink`/`computeWorkspaceLinkDiagnostics` therefore only classify correctly when their `index` argument already IS the current `linkIndex.value` (true at the one real call site, `DiagnosticsPanel.tsx`, and enforced in tests by assigning the fixture to the real signal before calling either function, the same convention `wikiResolver.test.ts`'s own fixtures already use) — this was caught by the test suite itself (every classification came back "broken" until the fixture builder was fixed to also set the live signal), not assumed correct without running it.
      **Scope decisions, disclosed rather than silently narrowed**: only the four statuses this claim's own instructions named are classified. Ambiguous-note detection (the spec's separate `ambiguous-note` diagnostic) is not implemented: `resolveWikilink` deliberately resolves a basename or alias match to its first candidate today, and changing that resolution behavior to expose every candidate would risk changing what every other consumer of `resolveWikilink` already depends on, well beyond a read-only diagnostics claim's touch set. A malformed wikilink (spec 5.2's "a recognized `#`/`^` fragment marker with nothing after it", e.g. `[[Note#]]`) is bucketed under "broken" rather than a fifth status, since it is exactly as unusable as a link to a nonexistent note and the roadmap claim's own wording never promised a distinct label for it, just that it would not be silently dropped from the results; a dedicated test proves it is not. A `^block-id` fragment is reported as resolved at the note level, matching `resolveWikiLinkTarget`'s own current behavior (block scanning is F04 Phase 2 scope, not built yet); a legacy-fallback link (spec 5.3, a pre-F04 filename that happens to contain a raw `#`/`|`) is reported as resolved, matching how the shared resolver already treats it, dropping the fragment interpretation entirely per that section's own rule, not a bug in this claim's classification. New `diagnostics/DiagnosticsPanel.tsx` follows `TaskHubPanel.tsx`'s established sidebar-panel convention exactly (a toolbar toggle button, a panel replacing the file tree, an empty-state hint, click-to-navigate via `outline/outlineNavigation.ts`'s existing `requestOutlineReveal` reveal signal, not a second navigation mechanism) and `App.tsx`'s existing `toggleSidebarPanel`/command-palette wiring pattern; findings render flat, sorted by source path then source position (matching `TaskHubPanel`'s own `flattenTasks` ordering precedent) rather than grouped, since findings from the same note already read as a visual group once sorted this way — deliberately deferred, not attempted: filtering, grouping controls, and the spec's own "Open target"/"Choose target"/"Preview fix"/"Dismiss for session" actions (section 8.3), all recorded in the F03 Phase 2 follow-up entry below alongside the previewed-refactoring scope it already covers. 32 new tests (up from 1063 to 1095): 16 in `diagnostics.test.ts` (every classification status against real `parseWikiLinks`/`scanHeadings` fixture content, not hand-built record literals, matching this codebase's established fixture convention; same-note fragment resolution, the malformed-as-broken decision, the block-id and legacy-fallback resolved-at-note-level cases, exact source-range propagation, path-then-position sort order, and the no-`wikiLinksByPath`-at-all degrade-to-empty case), 10 in `DiagnosticsPanel.test.tsx` (empty-workspace placeholder, a resolved link never appearing, each status's distinct label and the ambiguous-heading candidate count, the header count, click-to-navigate opening the correct note and requesting the correct reveal range, waiting for an async `onOpenFile`, and stable path-sorted ordering), and 6 in `store.test.ts` (population from the shared note read with no second `findMarkdownFiles` call, mtime-based cache reuse, no settings on/off gate, carrying a previously-read note's wikiLinks/headings forward across a later transient read failure, and dropping a malformed persisted `wikiLinks`/`headings` cache entry each). - ✅ **F04 Phase 1: heading-link parser, resolution, and Preview navigation**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260902T1045Z, branch: agent/f04-phase1-heading-links, spec: `spec/f04-heading-block-links-embeds.md`; scoped to the spec's own section 21 Phase 1 rollout step, "land structured syntax and serializer fixtures, extend metadata records and cache version, add heading links and same-note navigation behind a feature flag," narrowed further for a single claim): New `src/linking/wikiSyntax.ts` is the one structured wikilink syntax parser (`parseWikiLinks`) F04's own spec (section 2) says every consumer must eventually share: it recognizes `[[Note]]`, `[[Note|Label]]`, `[[Note#Heading]]`, `[[#Heading]]` (same-note), `[[Note#Heading|Label]]`, and `[[#Heading|Label]]`, per section 5.2's grammar (first unescaped `|` splits the label, first unescaped `#` in the target expression splits the fragment, backslash escapes `\`/`#`/`|`/`[`/`]`), with exact source ranges on every parsed `WikiLinkRecord`. A `#^block-id` fragment parses structurally (distinguished from a heading fragment) but is not resolved, per this claim's explicit scope; a leading `!` (embeds) is not recognized at all yet, `kind` always comes back `"link"`. New `src/linking/wikiResolver.ts`'s `resolveHeadingFragment` matches a fragment's spec-6.2-normalized key (reusing `markdown/headings.ts`'s existing `normalizeHeadingKey`/`scanHeadings`, not a second matching implementation, per this claim's own instruction) against a note's already-scanned headings, returning `resolved` (unique match), `missing-fragment` (note found, heading doesn't exist, a status distinct from a fully broken note-link), or `ambiguous-fragment` (more than one heading shares the normalized key, exposing every candidate rather than silently picking the first occurrence, per spec section 6.3's explicit "ambiguous, not resolved by order" rule, the disambiguation behavior this claim's own instructions called for). `resolveWikiLinkTarget` composes note resolution (reusing `linking/store.ts`'s existing `resolveWikilink`, unmodified) with fragment resolution, and implements spec section 5.3's legacy compatibility fallback: when the structured note portion doesn't resolve but the complete raw `[[...]]` text (hash/pipe and all) is itself an existing note's literal name, the link resolves as a plain whole-note link exactly as it did before this feature, protecting a pre-existing filename that happens to contain a raw `#` or `|` from being broken by the new grammar. `MarkdownPreview.tsx` renders through this parser/resolver when `WorkspaceSettings.headingLinksEnabled` (new, defaults on, off falls back to the byte-for-byte pre-F04 regex-based rendering) is on: a same-note fragment resolves fully and synchronously against the note's own already-available source (resolved/missing/ambiguous all distinctly styled via `linking/linking.css` and given a discoverable `title` for the latter two, per section 16's "textual status, not color alone"); a cross-note fragment resolves at the note level only during Preview's render pass (see the disclosed gap below) and renders with the same "resolved" look as a plain note link once its target note exists. Preview-only click navigation reuses the outline feature's existing reveal mechanism rather than inventing a new one, per this claim's own instruction: a resolved same-note heading link calls `outline/outlineNavigation.ts`'s `requestOutlineReveal` directly; a resolved cross-note heading link passes the heading's raw text up through a new optional third argument on `onOpenFile`, and `App.tsx`'s `handleOpenFile` (the one place that already reads the target note's fresh content to open it) resolves the heading against that content and batches the tab switch with the reveal request via `@preact/signals`' `batch()`, so `MarkdownEditor`'s reveal effect (keyed only on the `reveal` prop's identity) can never fire against the previous note's still-displayed content in the interval between the two signal writes. A missing or ambiguous heading on click is a silent no-op reveal, matching spec section 10.4's "open the note if its path still resolves" (the section's own "show a moved/missing message" and "offer search/diagnostics" UI is not built; not disclosed as done). **Explicitly deferred, disclosed rather than silently dropped**: Source-mode live-preview link decorations/styling, autocomplete for heading targets, block references (`^block-id` resolution and navigation, parses structurally only), and embeds, per this claim's own instructions; additionally, Preview does not asynchronously read and verify a cross-note heading's existence before the user clicks (unlike the existing local-image-attachment pattern in the same file, which this deliberately does not extend to cross-note heading verification in this phase), so a cross-note `[[Note#Heading]]` whose heading has actually moved or been deleted still displays with a "resolved" look until clicked, at which point it correctly opens the note without a false reveal; this is a disclosed scope narrowing to keep Preview's render pass free of new per-render file reads, not an oversight, and is queued as part of the Phase 2-5 follow-up entry below. `[[Note|Label]]`'s display-label separator, listed in the spec as an already-supported form (section 5.1), was in fact not functional before this claim (the pre-F04 regex used the complete raw text, pipe included, as the note name, so a note literally named e.g. `Foo|Bar` would have resolved while `[[Real Note|a label]]` would not); it now works correctly as a side effect of the shared structured parser, disclosed here as a genuine behavior change, not silently assumed already-working. New `WorkspaceSettings.headingLinksEnabled` (default on, per this codebase's existing opt-out-by-default convention for net-new functionality) is the feature flag this claim's own scope required; off, `[[...]]` parses exactly as it did before this feature existed, with a dedicated Settings toggle and test coverage proving the flag's off-path is unchanged. 65 new tests: 22 in `wikiSyntax.test.ts` (every supported syntax form, escaping of all five escapable characters, empty-fragment/empty-block-id malformed detection, the pre-F04 "no unterminated/empty bracket pair becomes a link" behavior preserved, multi-line and multi-link-per-document scanning), 17 in `wikiResolver.test.ts` (the full resolution status matrix including duplicate-heading ambiguity and both legacy-fallback directions, `#`- and `|`-containing filenames), 16 in `MarkdownPreview.test.tsx` (resolved/missing/ambiguous same-note rendering and click-reveal, cross-note rendering and click-driven `onOpenFile` heading-key passing, malformed-link literal fallback, legacy-fallback rendering and click, and the `headingLinksEnabled=false` escape hatch), 2 in `App.test.tsx` (a real end-to-end cross-note click through `App`'s actual `handleOpenFile`, both the heading-found and heading-not-found cases), plus one `workspaceSettings.test.ts` default-value test and one `SettingsPanel.test.tsx` toggle-wiring test. `src/linking/store.test.ts` (existing plain-wikilink suite) re-run and confirmed fully green, unmodified, since `linking/store.ts` itself was not touched by this claim. `documentation/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s `linking/` and `editor/` rows updated. `MarkdownHelpDialog.tsx` gained the three new syntax forms. Full verification suite green: `tsc --noEmit`, 1020 Vitest tests (up from 955 immediately before this claim, +65), `eslint` (0 errors, same 4 pre-existing unrelated warnings in `App.tsx`, which this claim does touch but adds nothing new to), `npm run check-version`, `vite build`. No Rust or Android source touched, so `cargo`/Android checks were not re-run beyond confirming this item touches neither. No manual/on-device UI smoke test was performed in this sandbox.

- ✅ **F02 Phase 1: shared task scanner and read-only workspace Task Hub**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260902T1045Z, branch: agent/f02-phase1-task-hub, spec: `spec/f02-workspace-task-hub.md`; narrowed to a first slice per the roadmap's own claim note): Delivers a read-only sidebar panel aggregating GitHub-Flavored-Markdown task-list items across the workspace, a projection of the existing shared workspace metadata index, not a second task database, with click-to-navigate to each task's exact source location. New `src/markdown/tasks.ts` is the second module of the shared structure-scanner infrastructure (`headings.ts` was the first): `scanTasks(content)` recognizes `-`/`*`/`+` unordered GFM task-list items (`[ ]`, `[x]`, `[X]`) at any indentation with exact source/marker/text ranges, skips fenced code blocks and block-level HTML comments the same way `headings.ts` already does, rejects malformed markers (`-[ ]`, `- []`, `- [~]`) and ordered-list tasks (out of scope per the spec's own section 5.1) by construction rather than a separate exclusion list, and assigns a nesting depth via a simple indentation stack (documented as a deliberate simplification short of full CommonMark list-block parsing, including its one known ambiguity: a 4-plus-space-indented line with an open shallower task above it is always read as nesting under it, even where strict block parsing might read the same indentation as that item's own indented code). `src/linking/store.ts`'s `rebuildLinkIndex` now calls `scanTasks` in the exact same per-note read already used for wikilinks/aliases/tags (no second workspace walk), populating a new `LinkIndex.tasksByPath` map with the same mtime/size cache-hit and unreadable-path-carries-forward behavior the existing wikilink/tag caching already has (cache version bumped 4 to 5, since the cached shape itself changed). This lands on top of `main`'s own concurrent "Runtime-decode the persisted link-index cache file" entry below (merged in during this claim, since both touched `linking/store.ts`): the merge required also extending that entry's new `isValidCachedNote` decoder to validate the new `tasks` field the same way it already validates `wikilinks`/`aliases`/`tags`, via a new `isValidTaskRecord` field-shape check, so a malformed persisted `tasks` entry is dropped and forces a real re-read the same way a malformed `aliases` entry already was, rather than silently trusting a garbage shape or crashing; a dedicated regression test for exactly that case was added during the merge. Unlike `tagsEnabled`/`frontmatterAliasesEnabled`, task extraction has no settings on/off gate: F02 is a standalone spec-driven feature, not net-new functionality queued from the daily competitor-changelog scan, so `CONSTITUTION.md`'s opt-out-toggle requirement for that queue doesn't apply. New `src/tasks/TaskHubPanel.tsx` follows `TagsPanel.tsx`'s structural conventions (a toolbar toggle button, a sidebar panel replacing the file tree the same way Tags/Bookmarks already do, an empty-state hint) and `OutlinePanel.tsx`'s navigation convention: a task row shows a disabled, read-only checkbox reflecting its actual marker state, its display text (inline Markdown formatting stripped via `headings.ts`'s existing `stripInlineMarkdownFormatting`, reused rather than duplicated), its note title, and nesting-depth indentation; clicking a row opens its note (via the existing `handleOpenFile`) and reveals the task's exact text range by reusing `outline/outlineNavigation.ts`'s `requestOutlineReveal`/`outlineRevealRequest` signal, the same mechanism `OutlinePanel` and `HeadingBreadcrumbs` already drive `MarkdownEditor`'s `reveal` prop with, not a second navigation mechanism; this is the first cross-note use of that signal (every prior use revealed a location in the already-active note), verified safe by tracing `MarkdownEditor.tsx`'s own effect order (its file-switch `[path]` effect, which swaps the CodeMirror document via `view.setState`, is declared and therefore always committed before its `[reveal]` effect in the same or a later render, so a reveal requested right after `onOpenFile` resolves always applies against the newly-loaded document, never the one being navigated away from) and exercised by a real click-to-navigate component test. **Explicitly deferred to F02 Phase 2** (already recorded as its own follow-up item below, scope unchanged by this entry): toggling a checkbox from the panel, filtering (status/due/path/tags/text), grouping, sorting, and the due-date convention (spec sections 5.2-5.3, 6.3-6.5): this Phase 1 slice implements none of those, only detection, aggregation, and read-only display/navigation. Also explicitly not implemented, a further narrowing the original claim note didn't spell out: the spec's own eligibility exclusions for the Templates folder and `.leotheca/` (section 5.4) are not separately enforced here, since the existing `linkIndex` (backlinks/tags) this feature's aggregation deliberately reuses does not apply them either; a note living in the configured Templates folder is indexed and shown the same as any other note today, a pre-existing gap in the shared index this item did not introduce and did not attempt to close, disclosed rather than silently inherited. 43 new tests: 24 in `tasks.test.ts` (every supported marker/bullet character, checked/unchecked and mixed-case completion, nested and sibling depth including across a loose list's blank line, malformed-marker and ordered-list rejection, fenced/tilde-fenced code block and single/multi-line HTML comment exclusion, the top-level-indented-code-block exclusion, LF/CRLF equivalence, and exact source/marker/text range assertions), 5 in `store.test.ts` (4 in the new "rebuildLinkIndex: tasks" suite: population from the shared note read with no second `findMarkdownFiles` call, mtime-based cache reuse, no settings gate even with both existing toggles off, and carrying a previously-read note's tasks forward across a later transient read failure; plus the one merge-added `isValidTaskRecord` regression test above, dropping a persisted cache entry whose `tasks` field is malformed), and 14 in `TaskHubPanel.test.tsx` (empty-workspace state, listing with correct checked/unchecked state and note titles, the task count, a disabled checkbox, an empty-task placeholder label, click-to-navigate opening the correct note and title, click-to-navigate requesting the correct reveal range and calling `onNavigated`, waiting for an async `onOpenFile` before requesting the reveal, stable path-sorted multi-note ordering, and nested-task indentation, plus 3 direct `flattenTasks` unit tests). - ✅ **Audit follow-up F-008: Runtime-decode persisted workspace data without destructive recovery**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260831T1950Z, branch: agent/f-008-runtime-decode-persisted-data; closed by this integration pass, Claude-Code-cloud-20260902T2211Z, after confirming every sub-item is already complete on `main`): `loadWorkspaceSettings`, global config, and bookmarks used to trust `JSON.parse(...) as Type` or a shallow merge outright, with no check that a malformed field's type or value actually matched what it claimed. Workspace settings now decode every persisted field at runtime via `src/settings/decode.ts`'s small, reusable decoders (`decodeString`, `decodeEnum`, `decodeNumberInRange`, `decodeBoolean`, `decodeStringArray`, `decodeRelativeFolder`, `decodeArrayDroppingInvalidEntries`), reject an `attachmentsFolder`/`templatesFolder` that is absolute or contains a `..` segment, drop a workspace-escaping `lastOpenPaths` entry, preserve unknown top-level fields across a decode/re-save round trip, and surface real corruption via a `workspaceSettingsCorrupted` signal with an explicit "Rewrite settings file" repair action in `SettingsPanel`, rather than silently overwriting the corrupt file with defaults. A real bug surfaced and closed before this item was considered done: `updateWorkspaceSettings()` originally persisted on every call with no guard for `workspaceSettingsCorrupted`, so an ordinary note switch or settings change would silently overwrite the corrupt file's real bytes before the user ever clicked repair; `updateWorkspaceSettings` now skips persisting while corrupted (the in-memory signal still updates), and `repairWorkspaceSettingsFile` clears the flag first so its own explicit write is not itself skipped. Global config and bookmarks runtime-decode their persisted shapes too (`decodeGlobalConfig`, `decodeBookmarks`), dropping only individually invalid entries rather than trusting malformed data; neither has a dedicated recovery UI, a deliberate choice: global config's only writes are already user-initiated (opening a workspace, changing the theme), never a silent autosave, and a bookmark missing its own `id` has no recoverable value worth preserving. The one deliberately deferred sub-item, the persisted link-index cache file's own runtime decode, was intentionally left to the already-active F-012 claim to avoid a merge conflict between two claimed roadmap items, and is closed by the "Runtime-decode the persisted link-index cache file" entry directly below. Verification suite was green at each stage: `tsc --noEmit`, 716 Vitest tests after the workspace-settings/global-config/bookmarks decoders and the persistence-guard fix, `eslint` (0 errors), `vite build`, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test` (48/48, untouched by this TypeScript-only change), and `cargo check`. No Android on-device verification is claimed or required for this persisted-data decoding closure.

</details>
- ✅ **Runtime-decode the persisted link-index cache file**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260902T1040Z, branch: agent/link-index-cache-decode; closes the gap both audit follow-up F-008 and F-012's own entries below explicitly left open: "the persisted cache file is still parsed with a raw type assertion, no runtime decoder"): `linking/store.ts`'s `loadPersistedCacheIfNeeded` trusted `JSON.parse(raw) as { version; entries }` outright, with no check that any `CachedNote` entry's fields actually matched their declared shape. This was a real, confirmed crash risk, not just a hygiene gap: the cache-hit path a few lines down calls `cached.aliases.length`/`for (const tag of cached.tags)` etc. unconditionally, and a malformed entry (a hand-edited file, a partial write, a future format change) throwing there propagates out of `mapWithConcurrency`'s `Promise.all` and aborts the *entire* rebuild, not just the one bad note, the same class of "one bad note takes down the whole index" bug F-012 already fixed for read failures, just for cache-shape corruption instead. Verified this is real, not hypothetical, before trusting the fix: reverted the new decoder locally and confirmed the new regression test then fails with an actual uncaught `TypeError: wikilinks is not iterable` thrown from inside `rebuildLinkIndex`, exactly as described, not a theoretical concern. New `isValidCachedNote`/`decodePersistedLinkIndexCache` validate the top-level `{version, entries}` shape and each entry's `mtime`/optional `size`/`wikilinks`/`aliases`/`tags` fields before anything is trusted; an invalid top-level shape starts fresh (identical to today's already-safe "no cache file yet" behavior), and an individually malformed entry is silently dropped, forcing a real re-read for just that one note, never a crash and never trusted data. Deliberately a small, local, self-contained type guard rather than reusing `settings/decode.ts`'s `DecodedField`/corrupt-flag machinery: that machinery exists to surface a user-facing corruption signal for data with no other copy (settings, bookmarks); this cache is a pure, fully-derivable optimization where the existing, already-documented philosophy is "a bad entry just triggers a real re-read, never data loss," so a plain boolean type guard that drops bad entries is the right level of engineering, not under- or over-built for what this file already promises. 4 new tests in `store.test.ts`: the confirmed crash-prevention regression above, a mixed cache file where only the one malformed entry is dropped and the other valid entry is still used as a cache hit, a non-object top-level shape, and an `entries` field that isn't itself a record. Full verification suite green: `tsc --noEmit`, 955 Vitest tests (up from 951), `eslint` (0 errors, same 4 pre-existing warnings), `vite build`. No Rust or Android source touched.

</details>

- ✅ **F06 Phase 4a: Large-outline virtualization**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260902T0641Z, branch: agent/f06-phase4a-outline-virtualization, spec: `spec/f06-note-outline-heading-breadcrumbs.md` section 16, section 20 Phase 4): Splits the scale half out of Phase 4 ("Add large-outline virtualization, complete keyboard semantics, screen-reader validation, and compact hardening"), following the same phase-splitting precedent as 2a/2b/2c. A note with more than `LARGE_OUTLINE_THRESHOLD` (500, matching section 16's own stated threshold) headings now renders its outline through new `VirtualizedOutlineList.tsx` instead of `OutlinePanel.tsx`'s existing recursive nested `<ul>` tree (`OutlineRow`): a flat list in its own bounded, independently scrolling container, mounting only the rows within the current scroll position plus a small overscan margin rather than every row up front. Below the threshold, `OutlineRow`'s nested rendering and its existing tests are completely unchanged. New `src/outline/outlineVirtualization.ts` holds the pure, DOM-free building blocks: `flattenVisibleRows` materializes the same depth-first, collapse- and filter-aware row order the existing nested renderer already produces (needed up front for windowing, where the old renderer could just skip recursing into a collapsed subtree lazily), and `computeVirtualWindow` is the fixed-row-height windowing math (a row's height is set via inline style rather than left to CSS, so the same JS constant that sizes the window also sizes the DOM, and the two can never drift). Both are plain functions of numbers/arrays, deliberately without any DOM dependency, since jsdom cannot provide real layout measurement; `VirtualizedOutlineList` wires them to a real scroll container via `onScroll` and a `ResizeObserver`, both mocked in tests the same way `GraphView.test.tsx` already mocks `clientHeight`. New `src/outline/OutlineRowContent.tsx` factors the row's own visible markup (chevron plus label button) out of `OutlineRow` into its own module so both the nested and virtualized renderers share one implementation and can never visually drift apart, and so neither renderer file needs to import the other (avoiding a circular import between `OutlinePanel.tsx` and `VirtualizedOutlineList.tsx`). Per this codebase's standing rule that a performance shortcut needs a test proving what happens when its "skip this" decision is exercised for real (the search `-exclude` bug in `CONSTITUTION.md`'s guardrails section is the precedent this follows): `computeVirtualWindow` has a test that walks a simulated full scroll pass in fixed steps and asserts every single row index across a 733-row list is covered by some window, not just that a couple of spot-checked `scrollTop` values look right; and a dedicated regression test proves the windowing recovers a correct, non-empty, non-out-of-range window after a filter shrinks the list out from under an already-deep scroll position (verified to actually catch the regression: temporarily disabling the scrollTop-clamping effect made this specific test fail before restoring the fix). 22 new tests (14 in `outlineVirtualization.test.ts`, 8 in `VirtualizedOutlineList.test.tsx`, covering the threshold boundary itself, non-exhaustive upfront rendering for a 2,000-heading fixture, scrolling to and selecting a far-down row, collapse and filter interaction with the virtualized window, and the shrink-while-scrolled recovery case above). `documentation/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s `outline/` row updated to mention the three new files. Complete keyboard tree semantics (spec section 15.1), screen-reader validation (15.2, needs a real screen reader this cloud sandbox cannot run), and compact/touch hardening (15.4) are explicitly not attempted here; compact/touch hardening landed in the Phase 4b entry above, and the rest is tracked in the Phase 3/4c entry below. Full verification suite green: `tsc --noEmit`, 914 Vitest tests (up from 892), `eslint` (0 errors, same 4 pre-existing unrelated warnings), `npm run check-version`, `vite build`, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test` (52/52, untouched, this item touches no Rust), and `cargo check`, after bootstrapping the Linux WebKitGTK/GTK3 system libraries per `CONSTITUTION.md`'s cloud-environment bootstrap section. - ✅ **Image resolution: dedupe repeated fileSrc() calls within one Preview render**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260901T2110Z, branch: agent/image-src-dedup): The item as originally worded (an image data-URL cache keyed by path) conflicts with `CONSTITUTION.md`'s "Speed of use" caching prohibition, since a cache persisted across renders or time can go stale the moment an external sync tool replaces the image file; that scope remains permanently out of bounds. Redirected to the safe, bounded direction the item's own prior finding pointed at: `MarkdownPreview.tsx`'s attachment-resolution effect previously called `fileSrc()` once per `<img>` element, so a note embedding the same image more than once (a diagram referenced twice, a repeated icon) triggered one redundant native read per repeat occurrence. It now groups `<img>` elements by their decoded absolute path first, resolves each unique path once, and fans the result out to every element sharing it. This is scoped to a single render pass only, not persisted across renders or time, so it carries none of the staleness risk the original scope did. 2 new tests (same path resolved once and applied to both occurrences; two different paths still resolved independently). Full verification suite green: `tsc --noEmit`, 842 Vitest tests (up from 840), `eslint` (0 errors, same 4 pre-existing unrelated warnings), `vite build`. No Rust or Android source touched; no real-world timing benchmark was run, since the benefit (fewer native calls for a repeated image) follows directly from the code change.

- ✅ **Audit follow-up F-010: Define a lossless canvas document and reference contract**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: Claude-Code-cloud-20260831T2336Z, branch: agent/f-010-canvas-document-contract): The previous `parseCanvas` silently discarded any node/edge it didn't fully recognize and any top-level field it didn't know about, the moment *any* unrelated card was edited and the file re-saved: real, user-owned data loss triggered by something as small as dragging a card. New `src/canvas/canvasDocument.ts` replaces it with a versioned, tolerant format: `decodeCanvas` keeps every top-level field it doesn't itself define (spread-then-overlay, the same technique F-008's `decodeWorkspaceSettings` uses for its own unknown fields) and every node/edge array entry that isn't a valid `CanvasNode`/`CanvasEdge` but is still a real JSON object, re-emitting them unchanged on the next save even though they aren't shown as editable cards. Only a genuinely unusable source (invalid JSON, a non-object top level, or a `nodes`/`edges` field present but not an array) still returns `null`; a *missing* `nodes` or `edges` field defaults to empty rather than invalidating the whole file, the same "missing isn't corruption" distinction F-008 applies to settings. `CanvasView.tsx` already refuses to render an editable canvas (and therefore never calls `onChange`) for a `null` document, so an invalid document was already structurally never overwritten; no separate corruption-recovery UI was needed the way F-008 needed one for settings, since nothing here has a destructive default to guard against. A shape-valid edge referencing a genuinely nonexistent node id is still dropped outright (dangling, not malformed-but-retainable), same as before this item. `resolveCanvasFileReference` resolves a card's `filePath` against the open `.canvas` file's own directory through `workspace/paths.ts`'s `resolvePathWithinWorkspace`, the same containment contract F-004 established for the native write boundary, instead of the previous behavior of handing an unchecked, ambiguous path straight to `handleOpenFile`'s `readTextFile` call, a real arbitrary-file-read risk from a crafted or corrupted canvas file. A legacy absolute in-workspace `filePath` (the only shape this app ever wrote before this contract existed) is still accepted, but only once validated through the same containment boundary via `isPathWithinWorkspace`, never trusted just because it's absolute; `CanvasView.tsx`'s "Open" button is disabled with an explanatory `title` when a reference can't be resolved inside the workspace, rather than silently no-opping. 26 new tests in `canvasDocument.test.ts` and `CanvasView.test.tsx` cover invalid JSON, a non-object top level, wrong-typed and missing `nodes`/`edges`, a dangling and a self-referential edge, a retained malformed node whose id an edge still resolves against, a retained malformed edge, a dropped non-object array entry, an unrecognized top-level field surviving a round trip, an already-present newer `version` not being downgraded, mixed-resource-kind cards round-tripping unchanged, and (component-level) that editing one card never drops another card's malformed record, that a workspace-escaping `filePath` disables Open, and that an unparseable document never calls `onChange`. `documentation/ARCHITECTURE.md` gained a `canvas/` module row. Full verification suite green: `tsc --noEmit`, 740 Vitest tests (up from 716 after F-008; the 24 above plus 2 that moved from the deleted `CanvasView.test.ts`), `eslint` (0 errors, same 4 pre-existing unrelated warnings), `npm run check-version`, `vite build`, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test` (48/48, untouched — this item touches no Rust), and `cargo check`, after bootstrapping the Linux WebKitGTK/GTK3 system libraries per `CONSTITUTION.md`'s cloud-environment bootstrap section.

<details>

    : The root `VERSION` file is now the single canonical semantic version. `scripts/versionConsistency.js` (pure, unit-tested comparison functions; `scripts/versionConsistency.test.js` has 35 tests covering every check's pass and failure paths, including the exact original bug shapes: a wrong `versionCode` alongside a correct `versionName`, and a moving-branch `commit: main` alongside otherwise-correct version fields) and its `scripts/checkVersion.js` CLI validate `package.json`, `src-tauri/Cargo.toml`'s `[package]` version (without false-matching a dependency's own `version = "..."` line), `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`, the web-clipper extension manifest, Android's `versionName` plus a deterministically derived monotonic `versionCode` (`major*10000 + minor*100 + patch`), the Flatpak AppStream metainfo's most recent `<release>`, and the F-Droid draft metadata's version fields and build-recipe `commit` ref against it, wired into `.github/workflows/ci.yml` as a required `npm run check-version` step (covered by the existing `validation` aggregate). Fixed the real drift this exposed: Android was `1.0`/`versionCode 1` against everyone else's `0.1.0`; the Flatpak metainfo and Homebrew cask draft said `1.0.0`; the F-Droid draft's build recipe referenced the moving `main` branch instead of an immutable ref, now `v0.1.0` (that tag does not exist yet, recorded honestly in the F-Droid README and in-file comments, not claimed as already real). A separate `version-guard` job in `.github/workflows/release.yml`, gating `publish` the same way `validation` does, activates only on a real `v*` tag push and fails it if the tag doesn't equal `v` plus `VERSION`'s contents or if `CHANGELOG.md` has no matching versioned heading yet, so a real release can't be cut with a version tag or changelog that has silently drifted from the canonical source. The Homebrew cask draft's version placeholder was also aligned for honesty, but is intentionally not part of the automated check: it is still fully blocked on an unrelated `TODO` (a real DMG checksum), and validating a stub that can't be real yet would just be noise. Full verification suite green: `tsc --noEmit`, 669 Vitest tests (up from 633, the 35 new ones covering this item plus the pre-existing suite unaffected), `eslint` (0 errors, the same 4 pre-existing unrelated warnings), `vite build`, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test` (26/26), and `cargo check`, all after bootstrapping the missing Linux WebKitGTK/GTK3 system libraries per `CONSTITUTION.md`'s cloud-environment bootstrap section.

</details>

Root cause: the manifest's pinned git source still pointed at the old `v1.0.0` tag, which predates commit `6f58e52`'s removal of the unused `eslint-plugin-preact` dependency; that stale commit's `package-lock.json` still needs `eslint-plugin-preact`'s transitive `eslint-plugin-compat`, whose peer-dependency override forces `npm ci --offline` to hit the real registry for a bare `eslint` metadata lookup that the already-regenerated `node-sources.json` (built against the current, fixed lockfile) has no offline cache entry for. Fixed by re-pinning the manifest's source to a current commit whose `package-lock.json`/`Cargo.lock` are confirmed byte-identical to what `node-sources.json`/`cargo-sources.json` were actually regenerated against, since no real `v0.1.0`-style release tag exists yet to pin to instead. See the dated comment in `flatpak/com.leonardschwier.leotheca.yml` and `flatpak/README.md` for the full account.

- ✅ **Expand-All Native Traversal**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    : Replace expand-all's per-directory bridge calls with a single native recursive walk (`find_all_entries` on desktop, `findAllEntries` on Android) covering both files and directories, so a directory with nothing directly inside it is still discovered and expanded, not just one containing a file somewhere in its subtree, which a files-only walk could never report. Verified with Rust unit tests (including one asserting an empty directory survives the walk) and TypeScript unit tests for the grouping logic, plus the full desktop verification suite. The Android-side walk mirrors the already-shipped `findAllFiles` pattern exactly (same `DocumentFile`/SAF APIs, same depth cap) but native Android code isn't unit-testable here; CI's Android build is this change's actual verification gate for that platform, no on-device confirmation is claimed.

</details>
- ✅ **F-005: Search memory guard — isTextFile whitelist, batch size pre-add flush, conservative unknown-size default**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    : The audit (F-005) found three gaps in the search content-read batching that had already been fixed in layers 1-3 (file count cap → byte size cap → oversized file skip) but left residual risks: (1) non-text files (PDFs, videos, archives, executables) were passed through the native side's string serialization — on Android `ByteArrayOutputStream.toString("UTF-8")` replaces invalid bytes rather than rejecting them, producing garbage; on Rust the file was read but wasted IPC and memory on content never meant to be searched. Fix: added `isTextFile(path)` in `types.ts` with a conservative whitelist of text extensions (md, txt, html, json, code files, config files, etc.); non-whitelisted extensions are treated like images — matchable by name but never content-read. (2) The old batch size check (`pendingBytes += size; if (pendingBytes >= cap) flush()`) added the file's size *then* checked, allowing one batch of three 5 MB files to go out at 15 MB. Fix: changed to check `pendingBytes > 0 && pendingBytes + size >= cap` *before* adding, then add and flush, ensuring no batch ever grows unboundedly. (3) When the native walk doesn't report a file's size, `entry.size` was `?? 0`, letting an unknown-sized file blend into a zero-size batch and defeating the byte cap. Fix: added `CONSERVATIVE_UNKNOWN_SIZE = 4 MB` — when size is unknown, the batch assumes 4 MB so one batch can never hold more than two unknown-size entries. Verified by 10 new tests (non-text file exclusion, batch size bound enforcement, unknown-size default behavior, realistic-size batching) plus all 597 existing tests, `tsc --noEmit`, `vite build`, and `cargo check` green.

</details>
- ✅ **Audit follow-up N-004: Contain local Markdown attachment reads**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    : Adds a canonical workspace-aware lexical read-containment helper for preview attachment paths and requires local Markdown images to pass it before `fileSrc()` can run. Absolute paths, traversal escapes, out-of-workspace bases, component-prefix confusion, and Windows-style traversal are rejected without a native read, while valid nested and sibling attachments remain supported. A negative-control CI run on the pre-fix claim commit proved the escape regression reached `fileSrc()`, while final implementation CI passed frontend typechecking, the full frontend test suite, production build, Rust check/tests, Capacitor sync, and the Android debug APK build. Packaged asset-protocol scope is not relied on as the security boundary, and no Android on-device verification is claimed.

</details>
- ✅ **Audit follow-up N-005: Bound Markdown attachment resolution concurrency**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    : Replaces unbounded local preview attachment reads with a six-worker queue. Rerender or unmount stops stale workers before they schedule more queued reads, already-invoked reads cannot mutate obsolete DOM, and one failed attachment does not block the remaining current queue. Tests-only negative-control CI run `33353118131` proved the pre-fix preview started all 12 requested reads and all 10 obsolete reads immediately; implementation CI run `33353227555` passed the same focused regressions plus frontend typechecking/tests/build, Rust check/tests, and the Android debug APK build. No Android on-device verification is claimed.

</details>

<details>

Existing ordering, comments, CRLF/LF endings, unrelated booleans and numbers, and unsupported YAML remain byte-for-byte unchanged. Plain, single-quoted, and double-quoted scalar styles are retained for safe selected edits; nested structures, multiline values, and ambiguous quoted-comma lists are shown read-only rather than normalized. Golden regression fixtures cover all required cases. A pre-fix negative control reproduced unrelated scalar normalization and comment movement; implementation run `33354455523` passed frontend typechecking, 622 tests, production build, Rust check/tests, and the Android debug build. No Android on-device verification is claimed.

</details>
- ✅ **Audit follow-up F-006: Give search requests explicit ownership**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    : Full-text search now carries a monotonically increasing request generation together with workspace-session identity, so stale enumeration or content-batch completions cannot replace newer results, undo a clear, or clear the newer request's loading state. Search authority is invalidated on clear and workspace reset, including Android grant changes that retain the synthetic `/workspace` path. Tests-only CI run `33356013650` failed all five intended pre-fix races: both A/B completion orders, clear during A, stale content-batch completion, and a workspace-session switch. Implementation CI run `33356099917` then passed frontend typechecking/tests/build, Rust check/tests, Capacitor sync, and the Android debug APK build. No Android on-device verification is claimed.

</details>
- ✅ **Audit follow-up F-013: Measure and bound graph layout work**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    : Graph layout now retains exhaustive pairwise forces for small graphs but caps larger layouts at 500,000 estimated force interactions using deterministic repulsion sampling and bounded attraction-edge sampling. Same-turn layout requests coalesce behind generation ownership, and a result must still own the latest generation after computation before it can publish. Representative 100, 500, 2,000, and 5,000-node budgets are covered, with a 2,000-note chain benchmark that ran in about 79 ms on CI and has a generous 2-second regression ceiling. Initial run `33357857127` caught that making the first layout asynchronous broke immediate node taps; the first bounded layout remains synchronous while repeated resize/filter work is coalesced. Repaired implementation run `33357970915` passed frontend typechecking, all 633 frontend tests, production build, Rust check/tests, Capacitor sync, and the Android debug APK build. No Android on-device verification is claimed.

</details>
- ✅ **Audit follow-up N-001/N-003: Make workspace transitions authoritative**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: ChatGPT-GPT-5.6-Sol-automation-20260830T2200Z, branch: agent/n-001-n-003-workspace-transitions, merged by Claude-Code-cloud-20260901T1900Z): The save coordinator's `resetForSession(session)` used to filter the newly published session rather than the outgoing one, so old-session debounce timers survived; invoked writes also could not safely be orphaned after an Android grant changed. New `src/workspace/workspaceTransition.ts` exports a `createWorkspaceTransitionCoordinator`: each `run()` call takes a monotonically increasing generation, and each of its named steps (`prepareOutgoing`, `connectIncoming`, `loadIncoming`, `publishFailure`, `afterPublish`) re-checks whether its generation is still current before doing anything observable. A later `setWorkspacePath` call always wins over an earlier one still in flight, even if the earlier one's settings read resolves last. `settings/store.ts`'s `setWorkspacePath` is the sole caller: `prepareOutgoing` rebinds the outgoing Android grant if the folder picker already reseeded the synthetic `/workspace` path, drains any queued or in-flight workspace-settings write and every operation `tauriBridge.ts` is currently tracking (see below), then clears tabs; `connectIncoming` activates and validates the new grant (a `listDir` probe, since a successful `restoreWorkspaceAccess` alone does not prove an expired Android grant is actually still readable) before `loadIncoming`/`publishIncoming` load and commit the new workspace's settings, including audit follow-up F-008's `workspaceSettingsCorrupted` signal, set or cleared from the incoming workspace's own decode result, never carried over from the outgoing workspace. `publishFailure` fails the transition closed to a recoverable inactive state rather than leaving stale UI active. Two other modules participate as drain points the coordinator can wait on: `src/workspace/saveCoordinator.ts` (per-`{session, path}` debounced note-content writes) gained `prepareForTransition(session)`: it blocks new edits for that session, cancels its pending debounce timers, and awaits any already-invoked in-flight write before resolving, replacing the old `resetForSession`, which cleared the wrong session and did not wait for in-flight work. `src/workspace/tauriBridge.ts` wraps every workspace-facing bridge function (both the older unchecked ones and audit follow-up F-004's containment-checked `writeWorkspaceTextFile`/`writeWorkspaceBinaryFile`/`createWorkspaceDir`/`renameWorkspacePath`/`deleteWorkspacePathPermanent` functions alike) in `trackWorkspaceOperation`, which counts operations currently in flight; `drainWorkspaceOperations()` resolves once that count returns to zero. This is what lets `prepareOutgoing` wait for, say, an in-flight rename through the native containment boundary to finish before an Android grant change could orphan it. Other stores (`bookmarks/store.ts` today) register a reset callback with `workspaceTransitions.registerReset`, invoked once per transition right after outgoing work drains and before the incoming grant activates, so stale workspace-scoped UI state cannot survive into the new workspace's identity. Global-config writes are serialized through a new `saveGlobalConfigOrdered` so a slower write from an invalidated transition can never land after a later one's write. New deferred-promise tests cover pending and in-flight outgoing saves, late save callbacks surviving generation but being dropped, the same synthetic Android `/workspace` path across a token change, and A-then-B publish ordering. Merged into `main` after resolving a real four-file conflict against F-004 and F-008, which both landed on `main` after this branch was created: `tauriBridge.ts` now keeps both F-004's new containment-checked `writeWorkspaceTextFile`/`writeWorkspaceBinaryFile`/`createWorkspaceDir`/`renameWorkspacePath`/`deleteWorkspacePathPermanent` functions and this item's `trackWorkspaceOperation` drain wrapping, applied to both the old and the new functions alike, so a rename or delete resolved through the native containment boundary still participates in the transition drain. `bookmarks/store.ts` kept F-008's `writeWorkspaceTextFile`-based `saveBookmarks` alongside this item's `registerReset`. `settings/store.ts`'s `setWorkspacePath` keeps this item's transition-coordinator structure, with `publishIncoming` and `publishFailure` now also setting and clearing F-008's `workspaceSettingsCorrupted` signal from the freshly loaded result for the workspace actually being switched into, rather than leaving the outgoing workspace's flag value in place; a transition that fails closed clears the flag entirely, since no settings file is loaded for the resulting inactive state. `saveCoordinator.ts`'s own note-content write still goes through the old, unchecked `writeTextFile`, matching what F-004's own entry already recorded as its one deliberate remaining gap: routing it through `writeWorkspaceTextFile` here was evaluated and found to need more than a local swap, since the coordinator only carries a numeric session id, not a workspace root, and `writeWorkspaceTextFile` needs both a workspace root and a path relative to it; importing `workspacePath` from `settings/store.ts` to get one creates a real circular import (`settings/store.ts` already reaches `saveCoordinator.ts` through `workspaceSaves.ts`), and threading a workspace root through `change`/`flush`/`retry` instead would mean re-touching every call site and is a genuine redesign, not something to force into this merge. Left open in F-004's own entry for a future session. Full verification suite green after the merge: `tsc --noEmit`, 759 Vitest tests (up from 633 on this branch and 748 on `main`), `eslint` (0 errors after fixing one pre-existing unused-destructure error this merge's new lint step in `ci.yml` exposed in `saveCoordinator.ts`'s `debugEntries`, same 4 pre-existing unrelated warnings), `vite build`, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test` (51/51, untouched by this item's own TypeScript-only changes), and `cargo check`.

</details>
- ✅ **Desktop AppImage blank gray screen**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    : Root cause: `initSettings()` called `getAppVersion()` without error handling — if that call fails (common on fresh installs or with certain Electron/WebView environments), `settingsLoaded.value` stays `false` forever and the WelcomeDialog never renders. Fixed in commit `3a2daa0` by wrapping the `initSettings()` call in `App.tsx` so its rejection still sets `settingsLoaded.value = true`, ensuring the WelcomeDialog always appears. Confirmed still present and unchanged on current `main`.

</details>
- ✅ **Android Widget "favorites" hamburger bug**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    : Fixed in commit `3a2daa0`. The `open-favorites` deep link handler (`App.tsx:275-279`) now sets `sidebarOpen.value = true` alongside `bookmarksOpen.value = true`, so the sidebar opens on Android. This matches the pattern used by `toggleSidebarPanel()`.

</details>
- ✅ **File explorer "Expand All" UX**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    : Picked option (a) from the two the item raised: `FileTree.tsx`'s mount effect now calls a new `expandFirstLevel(rootPath)` (`fileTreeStore.ts`) instead of a plain `loadChildren(rootPath)`. It loads the root's own listing, then marks every immediate subdirectory expanded and loads each one's children too, so opening a workspace shows one level of structure without an explicit "Expand all" tap. Deliberately bounded to that one level rather than option (b)'s deeper default or the existing "Expand all" button's full recursive `findAllEntries` walk: the cost scales with the root's own top-level directory count, not the whole tree, matching this codebase's established lazy-expansion tradeoff (deferred link-index/bookmark loading elsewhere). A subdirectory whose own listing fails to load is still marked expanded (so collapsing and re-expanding it retries, like any manually toggled folder) but simply shows no children, via `Promise.allSettled` isolating one failure from the rest. The existing manual "Expand All" button/spinner and its full recursive behavior are unchanged. 9 new/updated tests: 3 new `expandFirstLevel` store-level tests (multi-directory expansion, a no-subdirectory root, one subdirectory's listing failing without blocking the others) plus `FileTree.test.tsx`'s pre-existing collapsed-by-default assumptions rewritten for the new default (an explicit new test for the auto-expand itself, a two-level fixture proving the second level still requires a manual click, and the collapse/re-expand/cache and file-selection tests adjusted to start from the now-auto-expanded state). Full verification suite green: `tsc --noEmit`, 720 Vitest tests (up from 716), `eslint` (0 errors, same 4 pre-existing unrelated warnings), `npm run check-version`, `vite build`, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test` (48/48, untouched — this item touches no Rust), and `cargo check`.

</details>
- ✅ **MarkdownPreview image loading is sequential**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    : Fixed in commit `3a2daa0` (changed from a sequential `for` loop to firing resolves in parallel), then superseded by audit follow-up N-005's bounded six-worker queue, which is what `MarkdownPreview.tsx` actually runs today. Confirmed still present on current `main`.

</details>
- ✅ **CodeMirror full teardown on file switch**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    : The real full-recreate was actually forced two layers up: `App.tsx` rendered `<MarkdownEditor key={current.path} .../>`, so React/Preact destroyed and remounted the whole component (DOM node included) on every file switch regardless of what `MarkdownEditor.tsx` did internally. Removed that `key`, and `MarkdownEditor.tsx` now creates its `EditorView` once for the component's lifetime (a `useEffect` with an empty dependency array) instead of on every `path` change. A separate effect keyed on `path` reconfigures that same view via `view.setState(EditorState.create({ doc: value, extensions: ... }))` when the file actually changes, skipping its own first run since the mount effect already built the initial state. Deliberately `setState`, not the roadmap text's literal suggestion of `view.dispatch({ changes })`: `dispatch`'s changes are undo-tracked, so a whole-document swap through it would let Ctrl+Z after switching files undo back into the *previous* file's content; `setState` starts a genuinely fresh document and a fresh, empty history for the newly opened file, matching the old destroy-and-recreate behavior's actual undo-isolation guarantee while dropping its DOM/extension-construction cost. The paste/drop-to-attach extension closes over `path` directly (needed so a saved attachment's `notePath` is always the file open when it's pasted), so it's deliberately rebuilt on every file switch via a shared `buildExtensions` helper, not left stale from the previous file. 3 new component-level tests (`MarkdownEditor.test.tsx`, using CodeMirror's own `EditorView.findFromDOM` to reach the live instance rather than relying on the component to expose one) prove content updates on a path+value switch, that undo cannot reach back into the previous file's content, and that a paste-attachment save after switching resolves relative to the *new* file's folder, not the old one. Verified this doesn't overlap the active N-001/N-003 claim by diffing its branch against `src/app/App.tsx` and `src/editor/*` first: no hunk touches the `MarkdownEditor` JSX or import lines. Full verification suite green: `tsc --noEmit`, 747 Vitest tests (up from 744), `eslint` (0 errors, same 4 pre-existing unrelated warnings), `npm run check-version`, `vite build`, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test` (48/48, untouched — this item touches no Rust), and `cargo check`.
