- ✅ **Audit follow-up F-012: Make link-index cache freshness and failures explicit**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (claim: ChatGPT-GPT-5.6-Sol-automation-20260831T1610Z, 2026-08-31T16:10Z, branch: agent/f-012-link-index-freshness; reclaimed by Claude-Code-cloud-20260901T1900Z after verifying the branch sat unchanged at its own claim commit for over 24 hours with zero implementation activity, the standing abandonment-recovery rule): Strengthens `linking/store.ts`'s wikilink/alias/tag cache identity from mtime alone to mtime plus byte size, and isolates a single unreadable note instead of letting it abort the entire rebuild. **Cache identity**: `findMarkdownFiles`'s native walk (Rust's `find_markdown_files`, Android's `walkForMarkdownFiles`) now also reports each file's size, from the exact same `fs::metadata`/`DocumentFile` call already fetching mtime, not an extra bridge round trip; a cache hit now requires both mtime and size to match the last successfully read state, narrowing (not fully closing, an accepted and tested residual risk) the same-tick-edit collision a coarse or colliding mtime alone could hide. `LINK_INDEX_CACHE_VERSION` bumped 3 → 4 so an old pre-size cache file is discarded rather than trusted with a missing size field. Incidentally fixed a real latent bug found while wiring this through: Android's `findMarkdownFiles` bridge function fabricated `size: 0` when backfilling its shared walk-results cache for a later `findAllFiles` call within the same 30-second TTL, now carries the real size through instead. **Unreadable-note isolation**: a note that fails to read no longer throws out of the whole `mapWithConcurrency` batch (previously a single failure rejected the entire rebuild, discarding every other note's freshly read data); it's now recorded in a new `linkIndexUnreadablePaths` signal, and the rebuild falls back to that note's last confirmed cache entry (unchanged, not the failed attempt's own unconfirmed mtime/size) so a transient failure doesn't erase its contribution to backlinks/aliases/tags, while still allowing the very next successful read to correct it once it recovers. `App.tsx` surfaces a small `.app-title-hint-warning` notice ("N notes couldn't be indexed", with the paths in its title tooltip) next to the existing "Indexing…" hint when this is non-empty. Also corrected a stale doc comment: `resetLinkIndexCache` said "not wired to any UI today," when N-001/N-003 had already wired it into the workspace-transition coordinator; comment now describes the real wiring and also clears the new signal. 8 new tests (7 in `store.test.ts`, 1 Rust): same-mtime-different-size forces a re-read; same-mtime-same-size is documented as the accepted residual miss; mtime-with-no-size always re-reads; one failed read among 20 no longer aborts the rebuild and is reported in `linkIndexUnreadablePaths`; a clean rebuild reports an empty `linkIndexUnreadablePaths`; a note's last-known links/aliases/tags survive a later failed re-read and a subsequent recovery re-reads it correctly; `resetLinkIndexCache` forces a real re-read even when mtime/size would otherwise still match; Rust's `find_markdown_files` reports real, distinct sizes from the same metadata call as mtime. Full verification suite green: `tsc --noEmit`, 771 Vitest tests (up from 764), `eslint` (0 errors, same 4 pre-existing unrelated warnings), `npm run check-version`, `vite build`, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test` (52/52, up from 51), and `cargo check`. Android's `walkForMarkdownFiles` size addition mirrors the already-shipped `walkForAllFiles` pattern exactly (same cheap `DocumentFile.length()` field, no extra query) but has no JVM unit test, the same disclosed gap this codebase's other native-walk changes have; CI's Android debug-APK build is this change's verification gate for that platform, no on-device confirmation is claimed. The persisted cache file itself is still parsed with a raw type assertion, no runtime decoder; that remaining gap (shared with audit follow-up F-008, which deliberately left this file untouched to avoid a merge conflict with this claim) is left open for a future session. **Update 2026-09-02**: closed by the "Runtime-decode the persisted link-index cache file" entry above.

</details>
- ✅ **Desktop Tauri concurrency**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    : `linking/store.ts`'s link-index rebuild read notes at a flat concurrency of 8 on every platform, chosen for Android SAF document-provider compatibility, leaving real cold-start rebuild time on the table for Tauri desktop, where IPC round trips to the Rust backend are far cheaper. `workspace/tauriBridge.ts` gained `isNativePlatform()`, a thin wrapper over `Capacitor.isNativePlatform()` exposed as a function rather than a precomputed constant specifically so tests can override it per case with `vi.fn()`. `linking/store.ts` now picks 8 on Android and 24 on desktop (the middle of the 16-32 range this item suggested, not the extreme end, since an individual markdown file's read is already small and the win has diminishing returns past this). 1 new test proves the observed concurrent-in-flight count is bounded by 8 on Android and by 24, while exceeding 8, on desktop, using the same in-flight-counter technique the existing concurrency-cap test already used. Full verification suite green: `tsc --noEmit`, 772 Vitest tests (up from 771), `eslint` (0 errors, same 4 pre-existing unrelated warnings), `npm run check-version`, `vite build`, `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test` (52/52, untouched, this item touches no Rust), and `cargo check`. No timing benchmark was run on a real large vault (this sandbox cannot install either desktop shell), so the "2-4x" figure this item originally estimated is not independently reconfirmed; the mechanism itself (a higher concurrency cap directly increases the number of simultaneous in-flight native reads, confirmed by the new test) is what was actually verified.

</details>

- ✅ **Maintenance review: InkSurface pointer lifecycle and mouse-button handling**

<details>
    <summary>Details</summary>

    (autonomous: Mistral-Vibe-20260907T1213Z, direct-to-main): Maintenance review of `src/ink/InkSurface.tsx` and its tests at `ddfb916` for observable pointer-input defects. Found and fixed two defects: (1) non-primary mouse button handling — right-click (button 2) and middle-click (button 1) could start ink strokes alongside the primary button; (2) lost pointer capture protection — pointer move events were accepted even when `hasPointerCapture()` returned false, and stroke completion didn't verify capture before committing. Modified `supportedPointer()` to check `pointerType === "pen" || (pointerType === "mouse" && button === 0)`, added capture verification in `handlePointerMove` and `finishStroke`, and discarded strokes when capture was lost. Added 3 regression tests covering right-click, middle-click, and lost pointer capture scenarios. All defects were reproduced with focused regression tests that fail against the pre-fix code and pass after. Verification: `tsc --noEmit`, ESLint (0 errors in touched files), `vite build`. Touch drawing remains out of scope per spec constraints.

</details>
